- **Decision & rationale:** Per CLAUDE.md §2 ("stop and flag" a library limitation rather than silently working around it), each gap is implemented as a documented, tested best-effort heuristic instead of a full solution, and none of it is hidden:
  - Heading detection (`app/ingestion/pdf_parse.py`) uses only the numbering/regex signal (a heading must be alone on its line, matching `^\d+(\.\d+){0,4}\.?\s+<short title>$`), not font size. Markdown sources (`.md`/`.markdown`/`.txt` — also accepted by `scripts/prepare_sample_guidelines.py` and the CI-only synthetic fixtures under `tests/fixtures/guidelines/`) get first-class `#`-heading detection instead, since that signal is unambiguous there.
  - Figure detection (`app/ingestion/chunking.py::_extract_pdf_figures`) uses `pypdf`'s embedded-image list; `figure_ref.bbox` is always `null` (unavailable via `pypdf`) and `has_embedded_text` is set from a nearby "Figure N" caption regex rather than true layout adjacency.
  - `token_count` is a whitespace-word-count proxy, not a real tokenizer count — no tokenizer dependency is added for this; close enough to bucket into the 350–600 target window but not exact.
  - Criteria extraction (`_extract_criteria`) is a single per-cell regex on comparison operators (`>=`, `<=`, `>`, `<`, `=`); text that doesn't match that shape simply yields an empty `meta.criteria[]`, per the "where extractable" qualifier in the rule itself.
  - A table is only reclassified `criteria` (vs. `table`) when its nearest section heading's text contains "criteria" — a narrow, literal reading of rule 4 rather than a general classifier.
- **Reversible?:** yes — every heuristic is isolated in its own function with its own test (`tests/test_pdf_parse.py`, `tests/test_chunking.py`); swapping in a layout-aware parser later replaces these functions without touching the chunk schema.

### 48. BM25 sparse vectors: stable-hash indices + Qdrant server-side IDF, not a fitted corpus vocabulary
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-14 / Phase 2
- **Requirement ID(s):** ARCH §6 ("Sparse / BM25"), ARCH-002, ARCH-003
- **Ambiguous or undecided:** ARCH §6 asks for "a standard BM25 term weighting over a clinical-aware analyzer," stored as a Qdrant named sparse vector. The first implementation of `app/retrieval/sparse.py` (same day, superseded before anything else was built on it) fit `rank_bm25.BM25Okapi` per ingestion batch and built a batch-local `term -> index` vocabulary. Re-examining that design before wiring it into `app.retrieval.hybrid`: a Qdrant collection is shared across every ingested `document_version`, so two independently-fitted local vocabularies (e.g. from two separate ingestion batches) would silently assign the SAME term to DIFFERENT indices in different chunks — corrupting sparse dot-product scores across documents the moment more than one batch shares a collection. This is a correctness bug, not a style preference, and it was caught by tracing through how query-time sparse vectors would actually reach a multi-batch collection, before any persistence code was built on top of it.
- **Decision & rationale:** Replaced the fitted-vocabulary design with a stable hash (`blake2b`, 64-bit truncated, mod 2^24) mapping each analyzed term to a fixed index, computable independently at ingest time and query time with no shared state to persist or keep in sync. Each chunk's sparse vector stores raw term frequency (`Counter` over `analyze(text)`); Qdrant's `SparseVectorParams(modifier=Modifier.IDF)` (set in `QdrantVectorStore.ensure_collection`) multiplies by the corpus's own IDF at query time, computed server-side from what is actually indexed — Qdrant's documented pattern for BM25-like sparse search, verified against a real (embedded, in-memory) Qdrant instance in this session (`tests/test_vectorstore.py`, plus an ad hoc check confirming the IDF modifier measurably changes ranking). This is TF × server-computed-IDF, not the full saturating BM25 formula (no per-document length normalisation / k1 term) — "BM25-style," a narrower claim than the textbook formula, but correct-by-construction across a multi-batch shared collection, which the fitted-vocabulary design was not.
- **Reversible?:** yes — `app/retrieval/sparse.py` has no persisted state; the analyzer function (`analyze`) is unchanged from the superseded design, only the indexing scheme changed, before any chunk had actually been upserted with the old scheme.

### 49. RRF `k` is not configurable through the Qdrant client
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-14 / Phase 2
- **Requirement ID(s):** ARCH §7 step 3, `RRF_K` config (ARCH §20)
- **Ambiguous or undecided:** ARCH §7 specifies "Reciprocal Rank Fusion (RRF, k=60)" and `settings.rrf_k` (default 60) exists as a config knob. Qdrant's Query API exposes server-side RRF fusion only as `FusionQuery(fusion=Fusion.RRF)` — verified against the installed `qdrant-client` — with no parameter to set the RRF constant; Qdrant's own internal implementation uses a fixed constant.
- **Decision & rationale:** Used Qdrant's server-side fusion as ARCH §7 step 3 already specifies ("so fusion is server-side" — ARCH §6), rather than pulling both ranked lists client-side and re-implementing RRF locally just to make `k` tunable (that would also forfeit the point of server-side fusion). `settings.rrf_k` stays in config as documentation of the assumption (Qdrant's fixed constant is the same value, 60, that the architecture already named) rather than being read anywhere; `app/retrieval/vectorstore.py`'s docstring flags this so a future implementer doesn't assume the config value does something it doesn't.
- **Reversible?:** yes — if Qdrant later exposes a tunable `k`, or a client-side fusion fallback is added, `settings.rrf_k` is already in place to wire up.

### 50. Audit writer: minimal retrieval-event implementation now, full writer stays Phase 4
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-14 / Phase 2
- **Requirement ID(s):** PRD-085, ARCH-035, ARCH §18
- **Ambiguous or undecided:** `prompt.txt`'s Phase 2 scope explicitly lists "basic audit logging on every retrieval." `app/audit/log.py`'s Phase-1 stub docstring said the writer (`write_event`) was Phase 4 work, and `TRACEABILITY.md`'s PRD-085 row agreed ("writer Phase 4"). Flagged to the operator as a real conflict rather than resolved unilaterally (CLAUDE.md §1): unlike the citation/grounding split (#46), retrieval-event logging needs no agent output — `app.retrieval.hybrid.retrieve` already produces exactly the `{chunk_id, score, fusion, rerank}` detail `audit_event.retrieved` needs.
- **Decision & rationale:** Operator chose: implement a minimal `write_event` now for the `retrieval` action (real DB insert, hash-chained via the Phase-1 `canonical_row`/`row_hash` helpers), wired into `app.retrieval.hybrid.retrieve` via an optional `session` parameter (`None` in offline unit tests, skipping the write; a real `Session` in any DB-backed caller). The full writer's other action types (`answer`, `hitl_action`, `login`, `config_change`) and the chain-verifier job stay Phase 4 as originally planned, since those genuinely need the callers that produce them (agents, HITL flows, auth) to exist first.
- **Reversible?:** yes — `write_event`'s signature accepts any of the `AuditEvent` columns as keyword arguments, so Phase 4 extends usage rather than changing the function.

### 51. Local embedding/reranker backends implemented but not run against real model weights this session
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-14 / Phase 2
- **Requirement ID(s):** ARCH-004, ARCH-012, PRD-103
- **Ambiguous or undecided:** `EMBEDDING_BACKEND=local` and `RERANKER_BACKEND=local` (the latter already decided in Phase 1, DEVIATIONS #44) needed real implementations. The configured models (`BAAI/bge-large-en-v1.5`, `BAAI/bge-reranker-v2-m3`) together are several GB to download; network is available in this environment, but downloading and running them purely to smoke-test two thin, standard-API wrapper calls was judged disproportionate for this change.
- **Decision & rationale:** Implemented both against the documented `sentence-transformers` API (`SentenceTransformer.encode(..., normalize_embeddings=True)`; `CrossEncoder(...).predict(pairs, batch_size=...)`), with the actual model-loading call behind a module-level indirection point (`_LOCAL_MODEL_LOADER`) so tests inject a fake encoder/scorer and verify the wrapper logic (prefix application, batch size, device resolution, empty-input short-circuit) without a real load. `sentence-transformers`/`torch` are in fact already installed in this session's `backend/.venv` (from Phase 1's DEVIATIONS #44b/#44c real Docker build work) and were used to import-check both modules, but no actual model weights were downloaded or run. **Not done:** an end-to-end real load of either configured model. Recommended before relying on either backend for a demo.
- **Reversible?:** n/a — this is a verification gap, not a design decision; closing it later requires no code change, just running it.

### 52. `FileKEKProvider` implements AES-256-GCM with a locally-generated, persisted KEK
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-14 / Phase 2
- **Requirement ID(s):** ARCH-032, ARCH-033
- **Ambiguous or undecided:** ARCH-032/033 specify envelope encryption and a `file`-backed KEK for dev, with a "DEV KEY — not for real data" warning (already logged in Phase 1), but left the concrete construction (algorithm, nonce handling, key provisioning) to the implementer.
- **Decision & rationale:** AES-256-GCM via the already-pinned `cryptography` package (`AESGCM`); a random 32-byte key is generated on first use and persisted at `crypto_kek_file` with `0600` permissions (owner read/write only) rather than requiring the operator to provision one out of band — consistent with this being an explicitly-labelled dev-only path. A fresh random 12-byte nonce is generated per `encrypt` call and prepended to the ciphertext (`nonce || ciphertext_with_tag`), so the encrypted blob is self-contained and `decrypt` needs no external state beyond the KEK. `aad` (additional authenticated data) is supported and, when a caller passes a row's stable id, binds the ciphertext to that row so blobs can't be swapped between rows undetected — used by `app.ingestion.records` (`patient_record.payload_enc`) once that lands.
- **Reversible?:** yes — swapping in a different `CryptoProvider` (e.g. `vault`, Phase 4) only requires satisfying the same `encrypt`/`decrypt` protocol; no caller depends on the AES-GCM framing directly.

### 53. Conflict detection's contradiction check is a lexical heuristic, not a real NLI model call
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-14 / Phase 2
- **Requirement ID(s):** ARCH §7.6, PRD-014
- **Ambiguous or undecided:** ARCH §7.6 offers two ways to detect (b)-type conflicts between top recommendation chunks: "a lightweight NLI/contradiction pass" — worded as one option among a small set, not a mandated model call.
- **Decision & rationale:** Implemented the deterministic lexical option: two `recommendation` chunks that share at least 3 significant analyzed terms (via `app.retrieval.sparse.analyze` — "about the same topic") but disagree on the presence of a negation cue ("not," "contraindicated," "should not," etc.) are flagged. Chosen over a model call for Phase 2 because (a) it needs no LLMGateway call (which isn't implemented yet — `app/llm/gateway.py::chat` is still `NotImplementedError`, Phase 2/3 per its own docstring) and (b) it is deterministic and fully offline-testable, consistent with "determinism where it matters" (CLAUDE.md §4). Per ARCH §7.6's own instruction, this starts conservative (over-flags rather than under-flags): a missed contradiction is a safety defect, a false positive just costs a review. A model-based NLI pass can be added later as a second signal without changing the flag shape (`{chunk_id_a, chunk_id_b, reason}`).
- **Reversible?:** yes — `_lexically_contradicts` is an isolated, swappable function; adding a model-based check is additive.

### 54. `LLMGateway.chat()` implemented against an OpenAI-ish wire contract, with a transport-security warning
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-14 / Phase 2
- **Requirement ID(s):** ARCH-005, PRD-101, PRD-102, PRD-082, ARCH §17.2
- **Ambiguous or undecided:** `app/llm/gateway.py::LLMGateway.chat()`'s HTTP implementation was needed to unblock the auto-generated question utility's narrative-generation step (§15.1 step 4), which the offline stub (`app.llm.stub.stub_chat`) deliberately cannot serve — it always returns a fixed "no grounded answer in stub mode" string, by design (a safety property, not a gap). Two things needed a decision: (1) what wire contract to speak to "the self-hosted LLM gateway," which `ARCHITECTURE.md` never pins down as a concrete API shape; (2) whether `http://` (the docker-compose stub's default) was an acceptable transport for `LLM_GATEWAY_URL` in general — raised by the operator mid-conversation and treated as a real gap, not a style nit: the real gateway is **external** (ARCH §19), unlike Postgres/Redis/Qdrant which share the private compose network, so "TLS at proxy, private net" (ARCH-031) doesn't cover it, and this call path carries PHI-adjacent content for SCOPE-2 flows (stage classification, missing-info), not just guideline text.
- **Decision & rationale:**
  - **Wire contract:** an OpenAI-ish `POST /v1/chat/completions` — `{"model", "system", "messages", **params}` in, `{"model", "choices": [{"message": {"content"}}], "usage"?}` out. This already matches two things in the repo rather than being invented fresh: `app/llm/stub_server.py` (the dev/CI stub, itself written in Phase 1 with this exact shape) and the real, operator-confirmed-working shape for the sibling `/v1/embeddings` endpoint (DEVIATIONS.md #42 — an "ollama-backed response shape," and Ollama's OpenAI-compatibility mode uses the same `/v1/chat/completions` convention for chat).
  - **Fallback + retry:** each model in `model_chain` (primary + `MODEL_ID_FALLBACKS`) gets `settings.llm_max_retries` attempts (exponential backoff, capped at 4s) before moving to the next model; `LLMGatewayError` is raised only once every model in the chain has been exhausted. Failure logs never include `messages`/`system` content (PHI redaction, `app.logging`) — only `model_id`, `attempt`, and the exception string.
  - **New config:** `llm_gateway_api_key` (Bearer token, empty default, set only via a local gitignored `.env` — same pattern as `embedding_gateway_api_key` from DEVIATIONS #42).
  - **Transport security:** `Settings.validate_gateway_transport()` (called from `LLMGateway.__init__`, alongside the existing `validate_model_config`) warns — does not block, since the stub profile legitimately uses `http://` — when `LLM_GATEWAY_URL` is plaintext against a host other than the recognized docker-compose-internal/local set (`llm-gateway`, `localhost`, `127.0.0.1`, `0.0.0.0`). `httpx.Client`'s default TLS certificate verification is never disabled. `.env.example` and `README.md`'s config table updated with this caveat in the same change.
  - **`LLMGateway.embed`/`.rerank` left `NotImplementedError`, clarified as dead ends**: `app.ingestion.embed.embed_texts` and `app.retrieval.rerank.rerank` are the actual dispatch points for those (local/stub/gateway) and never routed through `LLMGateway` — their docstrings now say so explicitly, since the pre-existing stub methods on `LLMGateway` read as though they were the real path.
- **Reversible?:** yes — the wire contract is isolated to `_post_chat_completion`; a different real gateway's actual shape (if it turns out not to match this convention) would mean adjusting that one method and `stub_server.py`, not the public `chat()` interface or any caller.

### 55. Curated abbreviation-expansion map for query construction
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-14 / Phase 2
- **Requirement ID(s):** ARCH §7 step 1
- **Ambiguous or undecided:** ARCH §7 step 1 requires "expand common abbreviations via a curated map only; never invent clinical content" but does not name the abbreviations. Left for the implementer.
- **Decision & rationale:** `app/retrieval/hybrid.py::_ABBREVIATIONS` is a small (10-entry), literal map (SBI, LBW, KMC, NBU, BP, HR, RR, SpO2, IV, IM) chosen for the bundled neonatal dev corpus (WHO newborn health / SBI in young infants, Kenya MOH newborn care protocols) rather than a general clinical-abbreviation dictionary — inventing a large one would itself risk introducing wrong expansions. `_expand_abbreviations` appends `(expansion)` after a whole-word, case-insensitive match rather than replacing the original term, so exact-term/BM25 matching on the abbreviation itself still works while the dense retriever also sees the expanded vocabulary. Extend deliberately, one confirmed term at a time, per the module docstring.
- **Reversible?:** yes — a literal dict and one pure function; extending or trimming it doesn't touch any other module.

### 56. Real Postgres round-trip found and fixed a timezone-drop bug across the whole schema
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-14 / Phase 2
- **Requirement ID(s):** ARCH §4 (data model preamble: "Times are UTC"), ARCH-035, PRD-085 (follows directly from implementing `app.audit.log.write_event`)
- **Ambiguous or undecided:** none — this was a concrete, verified bug, not a judgment call, found by actually round-tripping a row through a real (ephemeral, Dockerized) Postgres rather than assuming the Phase-1-authored models were correct. Logged per the same "verify, don't assume" standard as DEVIATIONS #44c/#45a.
- **What happened:** `write_event`'s offline unit tests (a fake session, monkeypatched DB-touching functions) all passed, but a real round-trip check surfaced that `verify_chain` reported every row as broken immediately after writing it — with no tampering. Root-caused: `app/db/models/audit.py`'s `ts: Mapped[datetime] = mapped_column()` has no explicit `DateTime(timezone=True)`, so SQLAlchemy compiles it to Postgres `TIMESTAMP WITHOUT TIME ZONE`. The in-memory value at write time (`datetime.now(UTC)`, tzinfo-aware) hashes differently than the same value read back from the DB (tzinfo silently dropped), so `_row_fields`'s canonical serialization — and therefore the hash — differs between write time and any later read, even with zero tampering. A grep across `app/db/models/*.py` found the same pattern (`Mapped[datetime...] = mapped_column()` with no explicit timezone) on **12 more columns** across `hitl.py`, `eval.py` (×4), `memory.py` (×4), `records.py`, `corpus.py`, and `iam.py` — every non-`TimestampMixin` datetime column in the entire Phase-1-authored schema had this same latent bug, silently contradicting ARCH §4's own "Times are UTC" convention.
- **Decision & rationale:** Fixed all 13 columns (including `audit.ts`) to `mapped_column(DateTime(timezone=True))`, matching the pattern `TimestampMixin.created_at` already used. Judged in-scope for this change (not deferred as "someone else's Phase-1 debt") because it directly broke the correctness of the `write_event`/`verify_chain` implementation this change adds, the fix is a one-line-per-column mechanical change with no behavioural redesign, and the blast radius (silent tz-drop on HITL SLA timestamps, memory TTL windows, IAM session expiry, rubric timestamps) is a real correctness risk elsewhere too, not just in audit. Added `tests/test_db_models_timezone_aware.py`, an offline structural test (introspects `Base.metadata` for any `DateTime` column with `timezone=False`) so this class of bug can't recur silently — confirmed it fails when the bug is reintroduced. No Alembic migration exists yet to update (the real initial migration is still-pending Phase 2 work — DEVIATIONS to follow), so this fix only changes what a future `--autogenerate` will produce; nothing to migrate on any real deployment yet.
- **Reversible?:** yes — a type-annotation-only change on 13 columns (`DateTime()` → `DateTime(timezone=True)`); no data existed anywhere to migrate.

### 57. `patient.mrn_hash` added — `mrn_enc` alone cannot support "dedupe on MRN"
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-14 / Phase 2
- **Requirement ID(s):** PRD-002, PRD-003, ARCH §5.2, ARCH-039
- **Ambiguous or undecided:** none — a concrete implementability gap, found while implementing `app.ingestion.records.ingest_records`'s "dedupe on MRN" requirement. `patient.mrn_enc` is AES-256-GCM ciphertext with a fresh random nonce per encryption (DEVIATIONS #52), so two encryptions of the *same* plaintext MRN never compare equal — there is no way to look up "does a patient with this MRN already exist" against `mrn_enc` without decrypting every existing row's MRN and comparing in the clear, which doesn't scale and works against the point of encrypting it.
- **Decision & rationale:** Added `CryptoProvider.deterministic_hash(data: bytes) -> str` (HMAC-SHA256, key derived from the KEK via HKDF with a domain-separation label distinct from the AEAD key, so the lookup hash can never help recover the encryption key) and `patient.mrn_hash: Mapped[str]` (unique, indexed) — computed from the plaintext MRN at ingest time, used *only* as a lookup key, never decrypted from or exposed. `_find_patient_by_mrn_hash(session, mrn_hash)` looks up by this column; a match reuses the existing `Patient` row, a miss creates one with both `mrn_enc` (recoverable, for cases needing the real value under RBAC) and `mrn_hash` (lookup only) set. Verified against a real (ephemeral, Dockerized) Postgres: two records with the same MRN produced one `patient` row, decrypting `mrn_enc` back to the original plaintext.
- **Reversible?:** yes — a new column + a new `CryptoProvider` method, both additive; no existing data to migrate.

### 58. Chunk-level topic tagging reuses the document's own manifest `topic_tags` as the "keyword map"; passed as a Celery task argument, not a DB column
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-14 / Phase 2
- **Requirement ID(s):** ARCH §5.1 step 5, ARCH-038, ARCH §15.1 step 1 (consumer)
- **Ambiguous or undecided:** ARCH §5.1 step 5 says to "assign topic_tags from a lightweight taxonomy (section headings + keyword map)" but doesn't name the taxonomy or where the keyword map comes from. Separately, `document`/`document_version` (`app/db/models/corpus.py`) have no column for the manifest's document-level `topic_tags` (ARCH-038 lists it as manifest metadata, but Phase 1's corpus-model update — DEVIATIONS #29/#31 — added `licence`/`format_profile`/`figure_ref` but not this field) — `Chunk.meta` was already commented as the intended home for a *chunk-level* `topic_tags`, but nothing populated it.
- **Decision & rationale:**
  - **Taxonomy source:** rather than inventing a new clinical-category keyword dictionary (a real risk of getting categorisation subtly wrong, and duplicate effort against metadata the operator already provides), `app.ingestion.topics.assign_topic_tags` treats each document's own operator-curated manifest `topic_tags` list as the map: a chunk is tagged with a document topic phrase when at least half that phrase's significant (non-stopword) words appear in the chunk's heading + text. This can never invent a category the operator didn't already assert applies to the document.
  - **No new DB column for the document-level list:** the manifest's `topic_tags` are only ever needed once — when process_document derives each chunk's own tags — so there is nothing else that would read a stored copy back. Passed as a `process_document(document_version_id, topic_tags=...)` Celery task argument instead of adding a `document_version.topic_tags` column purely to shuttle one value between the submission step and the async task.
  - Resulting per-chunk tags are stored in `Chunk.meta["topic_tags"]` (matching that field's existing comment) and mirrored into the Qdrant payload's `topic_tags[]` (ARCH §4.1) for retrieval filtering.
- **Reversible?:** yes — `assign_topic_tags` is a pure function over a document's own already-existing manifest data; swapping in a real taxonomy later doesn't change the call shape.

### 59. Document supersession "newer" check: effective_date first, version_label string comparison as a fallback
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-14 / Phase 2
- **Requirement ID(s):** ARCH §5.1 step 6, PRD-005, PRD-Q1 (open question)
- **Ambiguous or undecided:** ARCH §5.1 step 6 says a new version "supersedes" the prior `active` one for the same `external_ref` "if `version_label`/`effective_date` is newer" but doesn't define the comparison when the two signals disagree or are absent — and `PRD-Q1` ("Guideline version authority when two versions are retrievable") is already logged as an open question in `PRD.md`, i.e. the harder cases were explicitly left undecided at Phase 0, not overlooked.
- **Decision & rationale:** `app.ingestion.documents._is_newer` implements the narrowest, most defensible rule that satisfies the common case without pretending to resolve PRD-Q1: `effective_date` wins when both versions have one; a version with a date beats one without; when neither has a date, falls back to a plain string comparison of `version_label` (a deliberate, acknowledged weak tie-break — e.g. "2024.10" < "2024.9" as strings — not a semver parser, since the manifest doesn't declare a label format). Two undated, non-comparable-by-string versions is exactly the PRD-Q1 case this rule doesn't claim to solve; it leaves the *incoming* version `active` alongside the prior one rather than guessing, which is the safer failure mode (both remain retrievable/citable) pending that open question's resolution.
- **Reversible?:** yes — `_is_newer` is a single, isolated pure function; a future version-authority policy replaces it without touching `create_or_supersede_document_version`'s calling convention.

### 60. `process_record_batch` and `reembed_corpus` left unimplemented — undesigned infrastructure
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-14 / Phase 2
- **Requirement ID(s):** ARCH §5.1 (`reembed_corpus`), ARCH §5.2 / PRD-105 (`process_record_batch`)
- **Ambiguous or undecided:** both tasks were named as Phase-1 stubs, but ARCH does not specify the infrastructure either needs: `process_record_batch(batch_id)` presupposes some way to hand a Celery task a reference to an uploaded file/mapping spec across the API-request/worker boundary (a batch-tracking table, temp storage convention, or similar) that doesn't exist yet and isn't specified; `reembed_corpus(new_collection)` presupposes a plan for running two Qdrant collections concurrently and a cutover step, also unspecified.
- **Decision & rationale:** Implementing either now would mean inventing that missing design rather than building to a spec — exactly the kind of unrequested infrastructure "don't design for hypothetical future requirements" warns against. Left `NotImplementedError` with a specific reason (not a bare stub) so a future implementer knows what decision is actually blocking, rather than re-deriving it. What *is* implemented and real: `app.ingestion.records.ingest_records` (called synchronously — from `scripts/ingest_deidentified_records.py --persist`, verified against a real Postgres — or from a future API route) covers small/medium record batches without needing `process_record_batch` at all; large-batch async handling can be designed once there's a concrete API shape driving it.
- **Reversible?:** n/a — nothing was built to reverse; this documents a scoping decision, not a design committed to code.

### 61. Alembic uses a separate, elevated `ALEMBIC_DATABASE_URL` — not the app's runtime `DATABASE_URL`
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-14 / Phase 2
- **Requirement ID(s):** ARCH-008, ARCH-034, PRD-NFR-5 (least-privilege)
- **Ambiguous or undecided:** flagged to, and resolved by, the operator rather than decided unilaterally (CLAUDE.md §1): the Phase-1 placeholder migration's own docstring calls for the real migration to be generated via `alembic revision --autogenerate` against `app/db/models/`, but `alembic/env.py` (as scaffolded) built its connection from `get_settings().database_url` — the restricted runtime `hrag_app` role, which `deploy/postgres/init/01_schemas_roles.sql` deliberately grants only `USAGE` + DML on the managed schemas, no `CREATE`. This was invisible until now because the placeholder migration's `upgrade()` was empty — the first real DDL would have failed with a permissions error.
- **Decision & rationale:** Operator chose the least-privilege-preserving option: a new `Settings.alembic_database_url` (default: the already-provisioned Postgres superuser credential from `docker-compose.yml`'s `POSTGRES_USER=hrag_admin`/`POSTGRES_PASSWORD=hrag_admin_pw`), used **only** by `alembic/env.py`. The running app (`api`/`worker`) never sees or uses this credential — `app.db.session` and everything built on it (`ingest_records`, `_run_process_document`, `get_db`) still connect exclusively via `database_url` (`hrag_app`). `.env.example` documents both. Verified end-to-end against a real (ephemeral, Dockerized) Postgres: `alembic upgrade head` as `hrag_admin` succeeds; the app's own ingestion path, connected as `hrag_app`, works unaffected against the resulting schema.
- **Reversible?:** yes — a new config field + one line in `env.py`; no schema or app-code impact.

### 62. `memory.langgraph_checkpoint` excluded from Alembic management
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-14 / Phase 2
- **Requirement ID(s):** ARCH §11, PRD-023 (follows from the Phase-1 placeholder migration's own instruction: "create the langgraph checkpoint tables via the library's setup, or leave them to the checkpointer's own migration")
- **Ambiguous or undecided:** `app/db/models/memory.py`'s `LangGraphCheckpoint` model is explicitly commented as a placeholder ("the langgraph-checkpoint-postgres library manages the real table(s)"), but nothing had yet decided whether Alembic should create a table from that placeholder anyway. Checked the pinned `langgraph-checkpoint-postgres` package directly: `PostgresSaver` has its own `setup()` method with its own internal `MIGRATIONS` list — i.e. its own independent schema-migration mechanism, not something meant to be driven by our Alembic chain.
- **Decision & rationale:** Chose "leave them to the checkpointer's own migration" (the second option the placeholder migration's docstring itself offered): `alembic/env.py`'s `_include_object` now explicitly excludes `memory.langgraph_checkpoint` by name, so `--autogenerate` never creates a table for it. Rationale: letting Alembic create a table from our placeholder model risks a future collision or confusion once Phase 3's `app.memory.checkpointer.get_checkpointer()` calls `PostgresSaver(...).setup()` and creates its own, independently-versioned real table(s) — better to have exactly one thing own that table's lifecycle. The `LangGraphCheckpoint` SQLAlchemy model itself is left in place, unchanged, purely as schema-module documentation of the seam (per its own existing docstring); it is simply never materialized by our migrations.
- **Reversible?:** yes — a one-entry exclusion set in `env.py`; removing it would let a future `--autogenerate` pick the table back up.

### 63. Row-level security: fail-open when `app.current_patient_scope` is unset; audit append-only trigger as defense in depth
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-14 / Phase 2
- **Requirement ID(s):** ARCH-034, ARCH-035, ARCH §4.2/§4.3, PRD-NG-011 (one patient per session)
- **Ambiguous or undecided:** the Phase-1 placeholder migration's docstring requires RLS on `records.*` and `memory.patient_context` "keyed on the `app.current_patient_scope` GUC" and an audit-schema trigger rejecting UPDATE/DELETE, but specified neither the policy's exact logic nor which `records.*` tables actually need it.
- **Decision & rationale:**
  - **Scope:** RLS enabled on `records.patient` (key: `id`), `records.patient_record` (key: `patient_id`), and `memory.patient_context` (key: `patient_id`) — the tables that actually hold per-patient rows. **Not** `records.record_field_policy`: it's global (role, purpose, field_path) → effect configuration with no `patient_id` column at all — enabling RLS there would be a category error, not extra safety.
  - **Policy logic — fail-open when the GUC is unset or empty**: `USING (current_setting('app.current_patient_scope', true) IS NULL OR ... = '' OR <key>::text = current_setting(...))`. This was the pivotal judgment call: every patient-data-touching path built so far (`ingest_records`, `_run_process_document`'s persistence, the real-Postgres verification in DEVIATIONS #57) uses `app.db.session.session_scope()` with **no** `patient_scope` argument, which never sets the GUC at all — a fail-closed policy (deny when unset) would have silently broken every one of those already-verified, already-working flows the moment this migration was applied. Fail-open matches the actual, intended access pattern: service/ingestion/admin operations (no GUC) see everything; a future per-conversation query path (Phase 3/4, `session_scope(patient_scope=<id>)`, one patient per session per PRD-NG-011) gets narrowed to exactly that patient. `app.api.deps.get_db` does not yet set `patient_scope` from the caller's principal either way — that wiring is still Phase 4, per its own docstring.
  - **RLS applies to `hrag_app`, not to Alembic**: Postgres exempts the table owner (and superusers) from RLS by default. Since the elevated `hrag_admin` role (DEVIATIONS #61) owns these tables, RLS constrains only the restricted runtime role — exactly the intended blast radius.
  - **Audit trigger, not just the role GRANT**: a `BEFORE UPDATE OR DELETE` trigger function on `audit.audit_event` that unconditionally `RAISE EXCEPTION`s. Unlike RLS, Postgres triggers are **not** bypassed for the table owner — verified directly: connected as `hrag_admin` (the owner/superuser) and confirmed both `UPDATE` and `DELETE` are rejected by the trigger, even though that role's role-level GRANTs alone would have permitted them. This is the actual "defense in depth beyond the role GRANTs" the placeholder migration's docstring asked for, not a restatement of the existing REVOKE.
  - **Verified, not assumed**: full round trip against a real (ephemeral, Dockerized) Postgres — `alembic upgrade head` as `hrag_admin`; connected as `hrag_app` and confirmed (a) no GUC set → all rows visible, (b) GUC set to one patient's id → only that patient's row visible, (c) GUC set to `''` → fail-open, all rows visible again; `alembic downgrade` back to the Phase-1 placeholder cleanly drops the policies/trigger/tables, then `alembic upgrade head` re-applies cleanly. `ingest_records` re-verified end-to-end connected as `hrag_app` against the fully-migrated, RLS-enabled schema.
- **Reversible?:** yes — `downgrade()` drops the trigger, function, and all three policies (and disables RLS) before dropping any table; verified by actually running it.

### 64. `POST /ingest/records/file` CSV: scalar + one-level-nested fields only, list fields rejected
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-14 / Phase 2
- **Requirement ID(s):** ARCH §5.2 ("Wide file (`POST /ingest/records/file`, CSV or JSON, one row/patient)")
- **Ambiguous or undecided:** ARCH §5.2 names CSV as a supported wide-file format but doesn't say how a flat CSV row represents `PatientRecord`'s list-type fields (`medications`, `labs`, `vitals`, `examination_findings`, `interventions`, `problems`, `allergies`). Checked the one existing precedent in the repo — `scripts/generate_synthetic_records.py`'s own `patients.csv` output — and found it uses a lossy `_flatten()` that reduces each list to a bare count (`"n_labs": len(d["labs"])`, no actual lab data); that CSV is a human-skimmable export, not something designed to round-trip back into a full `PatientRecord`.
- **Decision & rationale:** `parse_wide_upload` (`app/ingestion/records.py`) treats JSON as the format for full-fidelity records (a bare list, or the same `{schema_version, dataset_provenance, records: [...]}` envelope the generator's `patients.json` already writes) and CSV as scalar-plus-one-level-nested only, via dotted column headers (`encounter.ward`, etc.). A CSV column that names a list field (e.g. `medications`, `labs.analyte`) is rejected with a clear error rather than silently dropped, misparsed as a scalar, or coerced into a single-item list — inventing an implicit CSV-list-encoding convention ARCH never specified felt riskier than requiring JSON for records that need one.
- **Reversible?:** yes — `parse_wide_upload` is one pure function with its own tests; a real CSV-list convention (if ever specified) replaces the rejection with real parsing, same call shape.

### 65. `/ingest/*` route conventions: uploaded documents land in `SAMPLE_GUIDELINES_DIR`; EAV `mapping_ref` reuses the existing dataset-directory convention
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-14 / Phase 2
- **Requirement ID(s):** ARCH §5.1 step 1 ("File stored"), ARCH-039 (`field_mapping.yaml` convention)
- **Ambiguous or undecided:** ARCH §5.1 says an uploaded document's file is "stored" but not where; ARCH §5.2's `/ingest/records/eav` was already documented (Phase 1 stub) as taking "`mapping_ref` (server-side named `field_mapping.yaml`)" but never pinned down how a `mapping_ref` string resolves to an actual file path.
- **Decision & rationale:**
  - **Document storage**: `POST /ingest/documents` writes the upload into `SAMPLE_GUIDELINES_DIR` — the same directory README.md already documents as "the guideline corpus. You supply this," so an API upload and an operator manually dropping a PDF there produce the identical end state (a file + a manifest-equivalent metadata row), rather than inventing a second, separate "uploads" directory nothing else reads from. A same-named file already on disk with *different* content is a `409 Conflict`, never silently overwritten — `content_sha256`-based idempotency (already implemented in `create_or_supersede_document_version`, DEVIATIONS #59) is what makes re-uploading the *same* file a safe no-op instead.
  - **`mapping_ref` resolution**: resolved as `PATIENT_RECORDS_DIR/deidentified/<mapping_ref>/field_mapping.yaml` — exactly the directory layout the bundled `newborn_nbu_2021` dataset and `scripts/ingest_deidentified_records.py` already use (one mapping spec lives with its dataset, keyed by dataset name). `/ingest/records/eav` is the HTTP path to that same, already-implemented, already-tested pipeline (`FileEavSource` + `ingest_records`) — not a second implementation of it. An unknown `mapping_ref` is a `404`, not a silent empty result.
  - `POST /ingest/records` (the JSON-body service/API path) intentionally has **no** attestation field — `app.schemas.record.RecordIngestBatch` (Phase 1) never defined one — so a `deidentified`-declared batch through this path is unconditionally rejected by the existing `guard_batch` gate (`MissingAttestationError` -> `400`). Not treated as a gap to fix here: adding an attestation field to a Phase-1-authored schema on spec alone, for a caller (an upstream service push) that doesn't exist yet, would be exactly the kind of unrequested design "don't design for hypothetical future requirements" warns against.
- **Reversible?:** yes — both conventions are internal implementation choices behind the route handlers; nothing external depends on them yet.

### 66. Auto-question generator: rejected an operator-supplied prompt design that asked for patient-specific management questions; deterministic word-overlap validator; scope-1 enforced twice
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-14 / Phase 2
- **Requirement ID(s):** ARCH §15.1 steps 3–6, PRD-060, PRD-061, PRD-064, CDS-FUTURE.md / SCOPE-2.3 (the hard boundary), CLAUDE.md §3 rule 4
- **Ambiguous or undecided:** the operator supplied a "SIMULATED CLINICAL QUESTION GENERATION PROMPT" as a starting point and asked for improvements before implementation. Read closely against CLAUDE.md §3 rule 4 ("the CDS boundary is hard... stop and flag rather than implementing a workaround") rather than adopted as-is.
- **Decision & rationale:**
  - **The scope problem, flagged before writing code:** the supplied prompt's task instruction asks for a question "focused on either diagnosis of SBI, or **management of suspected or confirmed SBI**" for "this patient," inviting "the type of question a clinician might ask during... clinical decision-making." That is a materially different target than ARCH §15.1 step 4's mandated template — *"What does the guideline recommend for a patient presenting with {presentation}?"*, explicitly never "what should happen next for this patient." A model given "ask a management question about this specific infant" will readily produce something like "Should this infant be started on empiric IV antibiotics given the grunting and hypothermia?" — a SCOPE-2.3 next-step-recommendation question, not a SCOPE-1 guideline lookup. Because this generator's output becomes the actual query set the RAG system is evaluated against, adopting the prompt as given would mean the eval harness itself starts manufacturing scope-violating queries — the opposite of what PRD-061/ARCH §15.1's "scope-1 framing only" rule exists to prevent. Flagged this to the operator directly rather than silently adjusting or silently adopting; the operator's follow-up asked for the corrected version to be built.
  - **What was kept from the supplied prompt** (genuinely useful, adopted): structured field-category extraction (`_field_subset_lines` — demographics, admission vitals, anthropometrics, signs/symptoms, matching what the record schema actually has, since our `PatientRecord` has no maternal-history section — a category from the supplied prompt that was dropped rather than fabricated fields to fill it), the "use only information available at admission" discipline (already free for single-snapshot synthetic records, but stated explicitly in the system prompt as a rule the model must follow, not just an accident of our data), and "never state an undocumented finding as absent" (`_field_subset_lines` omits absent fields entirely rather than writing "no history of X").
  - **What was replaced:** the question-focus instruction now mandates ARCH §15.1 step 4's exact template shape and explicitly lists banned phrasings ("should," "what treatment," "next step," "what medication/dose"). Enforced **twice**, not just in the prompt: `_is_scope1_framed` re-checks the model's actual output after generation (reusing `app.grounding.wording.has_directive_phrasing` for the "you should"/"administer X to this patient" patterns, plus a question-specific pattern set) and rejects+retries on a miss, exactly like the grounding-failure retry path — a model can drift from instructions, so the check that matters is on the output, not just the instruction.
  - **Validator approach:** `validate_narrative` is deterministic word-overlap against the record's own field *values* (never field names — this intentionally reads plaintext values, unlike `field_index`'s no-values rule, because the narrative is about to be shown to a clinician regardless), not a medical NER/entity-linking model — none is in scope, and "determinism where it matters" (CLAUDE.md §4) favours a checkable rule. A fixed, small, literal allowlist covers question-shape scaffolding words ("what," "does," "guideline," "recommend," ...) and units-of-measurement ("weeks," "grams," "bpm," ...) — the latter added after a real test failure showed "34 weeks gestation" being flagged as fabrication when only the bare number "34" was in the vocabulary; deliberately did **not** add clinical-finding words ("breathing," "heart," "oxygen") to that allowlist, so a genuinely fabricated finding stays catchable. Logged as an approximation, not a true entity linker: a paraphrase using none of the record's words can slip past undetected in the other direction (a false negative on fabrication) — an acknowledged limitation, not silently assumed exact.
  - **Test-only fake gateway, not the production stub:** `LLMGateway.chat()`'s offline `stub_chat` deliberately never generates real content (already established when `LLMGateway.chat()` was implemented, DEVIATIONS #54's discussion) — a dedicated `_FakeGateway` test double (returning canned, test-controlled narrative text) is what makes `generate_question` testable offline, per the resolution agreed at that time.
- **Reversible?:** yes — the prompt template, the violation-pattern list, and the validator's allowlists are all isolated, swappable constants with their own tests; none of this is committed to the DB schema or any other module's contract.

### 67. Auto-question generator scope: steps 3–6 implemented; source-record selection (step 2), diversity filter (step 7), and gold re-check (step 8) deferred
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-14 / Phase 2
- **Requirement ID(s):** ARCH §15.1 (full pipeline)
- **Ambiguous or undecided:** none — a scope decision, not an ambiguity. `app.eval.question_gen.planner` (step 1, composition planning) was already implemented in Phase 1; this change implements steps 3–6 (field extraction, narrative generation, validation, labelling) for an *already-chosen* `source_record`. Steps 2, 7, and 8 were not attempted.
- **Decision & rationale:** step 2 ("pick a source record" — matching a synthetic record to a guideline's actual applicability criteria, choosing a corpus-gap scenario for `no_guideline_expected`) and step 8 ("gold re-check" — running real retrieval against a populated corpus) both need a live, ingested guideline corpus with real chunks in Qdrant to check against; step 7 (diversity filter) needs a set of already-generated questions to compare embeddings across. None of that infrastructure is exercised together yet in this build (ingestion and retrieval exist as tested components, but nothing has run them end-to-end against the full bundled corpus in this session). Implementing steps 2/7/8 now would mean writing against an untested integration surface rather than a verified one — the same reasoning as DEVIATIONS #60's deferral of `process_record_batch`/`reembed_corpus`. `generate_question`'s `target_guideline_topic` parameter is deliberately optional and stands in for step 2's output, so a future step-2 implementation is additive (it starts calling `generate_question` with a real topic instead of `None`), not a rewrite of what landed here. Persisting the returned dict as an `eval_question` row (the second half of step 6) is also not done here — `generate_question` returns the shaped dict; a caller writes it to the DB.
- **Reversible?:** n/a — a scoping decision, not a design committed to code that would need reversing.

### 68. Scope classifier: deterministic lexical backstop only, no model call
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-14 / Phase 3
- **Requirement ID(s):** ARCH-025, CLAUDE.md §3 rule 4 (CDS boundary is hard)
- **Ambiguous or undecided:** `app/agents/prompts/orchestrator_scope.md` (Phase 1 draft) and ARCH-025 both describe scope classification as "a deterministic lexical backstop + a constrained model classification," but don't resolve what happens when they disagree, or whether the model call is load-bearing for the boundary itself.
- **Decision & rationale:** `app.scope.classifier.classify_scope` implements the lexical backstop only. The failure mode of a wrong scope classification here is not symmetric: answering a SCOPE-2.3/2.4 query is the single highest-severity failure this system can have (CDS-FUTURE.md), while over-escalating a SCOPE-1/SCOPE-2.1/2.2 query to `out_of_scope` is a precision/UX cost, not a safety one. Adding a non-deterministic model call to the control that decides "never answered" vs. "might be answered" would make the highest-stakes decision in the system depend on a model call that can itself fail, hallucinate, or drift — exactly the kind of dependency `CLAUDE.md` §4's "determinism where it matters" principle rules out for scope classification specifically. The prompt template is kept as the documented contract for a future *precision-improving* pass (SCOPE_1 vs. SCOPE_2_STAGE vs. SCOPE_2_MISSING_INFO vs. OUT_OF_SCOPE ambiguity, where a wrong answer is not a boundary breach) but is not wired to a model call. Nothing in `prompt.txt`'s Phase 3 checklist requires the model-assisted pass; the lexical backstop alone satisfies every scope-boundary requirement and is what the new gating test in `test_scope_boundary.py` (added this phase) exercises end-to-end.
- **Reversible?:** yes — `classify_scope`'s call signature is unchanged by adding a model-assisted refinement later; it would only ever narrow `OUT_OF_SCOPE`/ambiguous cases, never override an excluded-marker hit.

### 69. Citation-verifier: positional `c1..cN` label resolution; hybrid entailment calls the model only on ambiguous overlap
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-14 / Phase 3
- **Requirement ID(s):** ARCH §8.3, ARCH-015
- **Ambiguous or undecided:** ARCH §8.3 step 1 says a claim's citation must "resolve to a chunk in this turn's retrieval snapshot" but doesn't specify how the synthesis model's `citation_ids` (arbitrary strings it writes) map onto actual chunk ids (UUIDs it did not choose). Step 3 says entailment is "lexical overlap + constrained model call" but not whether the model call always runs or only when needed.
- **Decision & rationale:** `guideline_synthesis_agent` labels sources `c1..cN` in retrieval-snapshot order when building `SOURCES`; `app.grounding.verifier._label_maps` resolves a claim's `citation_ids` against that same positional order (falling back to a verbatim `chunk_id` match for a model that echoes one anyway). Reproducing a full UUID verbatim is a real, observed LLM reliability failure mode; short ordinal labels are not. For entailment, `_entailment_verdict`'s hybrid mode trusts a confident lexical read (word-overlap ≥0.75 -> "yes", ≤0.05 -> "no") and only spends a model round-trip on the ambiguous middle — still "lexical overlap + a constrained model call" per claim that needs it, not a model call on every claim regardless of how obvious the lexical signal already is.
- **Reversible?:** yes — both are internal to `app.grounding.verifier`; neither changes `verify()`'s public signature or the stored `GroundingReport` shape.

### 70. Patient-field access: data minimization enforced now; DB-backed `record_field_policy` resolution stays Phase 4
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-14 / Phase 3
- **Requirement ID(s):** ARCH §10.2 (patient-record agent), ARCH-034
- **Ambiguous or undecided:** the patient-record agent's own Phase-1 docstring says it filters by `record_field_policy(role, purpose)`, but `app.auth.rbac.resolve_field_effect` (the DB-backed (role, purpose, field_path) -> allow/deny/mask lookup) is explicitly assigned to Phase 4 in its own docstring, and no `iam` role-seeding work exists yet for it to resolve against. The agent needs to be functional in Phase 3 regardless — `prompt.txt`'s Phase 3 checklist requires the agent topology to work end to end.
- **Decision & rationale:** `app.records.access.get_patient_fields` enforces what's available now without waiting on Phase 4: (a) it never returns direct identifiers (`given_name`/`family_name`/`mrn`/`record_id`) regardless of what's requested — the one thing no in-scope purpose (guideline matching, stage classification, missing-info) ever needs; (b) it returns only the exact field paths requested, never more; (c) every read is audited with the exact field list, patient_id, and purpose (`action="record_access"`), matching the audit-completeness requirement even though the allow/deny/mask decision isn't policy-table-driven yet. When Phase 4 implements `resolve_field_effect`, it gates in front of this same projection logic — an additive change, not a rewrite.
- **Reversible?:** yes — `get_patient_fields`'s signature already takes `purpose`; a Phase-4 policy check slots in before the projection step without changing callers.

### 71. Criteria-to-feature mapping: curated synonym map (never inferred); a criteria chunk's `heading` stands in for a stage label
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-14 / Phase 3
- **Requirement ID(s):** ARCH §6 rule 4 (`meta.criteria[]`), ARCH §9.2 (SCOPE-2.1 stage classification, SCOPE-2.2 missing-info)
- **Ambiguous or undecided:** `_extract_criteria` (Phase 2) produces free-text field labels straight from a PDF table cell (e.g. "Heart Rate", "SpO2") — not `PatientRecord` field paths — and nothing in ARCH specifies the mapping between the two, or where a stage name comes from for a given criteria chunk. The Qdrant payload also didn't carry a chunk's `meta`/`heading` through retrieval at all (a Phase-2 wiring gap discovered while implementing this — `app.ingestion.chunk_persistence.persist_chunks` stored `meta` only in Postgres, not in the Qdrant point payload retrieval actually reads from).
- **Decision & rationale:** `app.records.criteria._CRITERIA_FIELD_SYNONYMS` is a small, curated, longest-match-first map (mirroring the existing curated-only abbreviation-expansion pattern in `app.retrieval.hybrid._ABBREVIATIONS`, DEVIATIONS #55) from criterion field text to `app.records.access.extract_features` paths; an unmapped field evaluates as `matched=None` ("uncertain"), never a guessed pass/fail. For a stage label: rather than extend the ingest manifest schema (a Phase-2 chunking change, out of scope here) to add an operator-supplied per-chunk stage name, a criteria chunk's own `heading` (the nearest section heading, already captured at chunking time — e.g. "Criteria for Stabilisation Phase") is used directly. It is real, operator-authored source text, never invented by the agent. Fixed the Qdrant-payload gap as a necessary, additive correction: `persist_chunks` now includes `heading` and `meta` (minus the large `embedding_text` value) in the point payload, and `RetrievalItem`/`_to_retrieval_item` carry them through — existing chunks re-embed cleanly since this only adds payload keys.
- **Reversible?:** yes — the synonym map and the heading-as-stage-label choice are both isolated and swappable; a future manifest-driven stage-label field would only need `stage_classifier_agent` to prefer `meta.stage_label` when present, falling back to `heading` otherwise.

### 72. `patient_context` writes audited as `action="record_access"` with a `detail` marker
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-14 / Phase 3
- **Requirement ID(s):** ARCH §11, ARCH-035, PRD-085
- **Ambiguous or undecided:** `AuditEvent.action`'s column comment lists a fixed set of values (`query|retrieval|record_access|answer|hitl_action|ingest|config_change|login`) that doesn't include "patient context write" as its own category, but ARCH §18/PRD-085 requires every patient-linked write to be audited.
- **Decision & rationale:** reused `action="record_access"` (the closest documented category — patient-linked, structured, not a raw record field read but audited the same way) with `detail={"patient_context_kind": kind, "op": "context_write"}` distinguishing it from an actual field read in the same audit trail. `action` is a plain `String(32)` column, not a DB-level enum, so this doesn't require a migration; a future dedicated `context_write` action value is a pure addition, not a breaking change to existing rows.
- **Reversible?:** yes — `write_context`'s single `write_event(..., action=...)` call is the only place this choice is made.

### 74. Graph routing: fixed `EDGES` kept unconditional; escalation-aware nodes short-circuit instead of branching
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-14 / Phase 3
- **Requirement ID(s):** ARCH §10.1
- **Ambiguous or undecided:** the Phase-1 placeholder `app/agents/graph.py` fixed `EDGES` as unconditional pairs (e.g. `("patient_record", "stage_classifier")`, `("patient_record", "missing_info")` — both always reachable, not "only if no escalation") for review purposes, but a real compiled graph needs an actual routing decision at each branch point, which ARCH doesn't spell out.
- **Decision & rationale:** kept the committed `NODES`/`EDGES` contract unchanged (tests already assert its shape) and layered real conditional routing (`_route_from_orchestrator`, `_route_after_patient_record`, `_route_after_citation_verifier`, all deterministic code over `GraphState`, never a model call) on top via `add_conditional_edges`. Rather than adding an extra `if escalation: go to escalation` branch at every possible point (`stage_classifier`/`missing_info` would each need their own conditional edge to `escalation`, duplicating the same check three times), `guideline_synthesis_agent` and `citation_verifier_agent` both short-circuit (`if state.get("escalation"): return state`) at their own entry — so the graph can keep the simple unconditional `EDGES` shape (stage_classifier/missing_info always flow to guideline_synthesis) while an escalation raised upstream passes through untouched to the one real decision point that matters, `_route_after_citation_verifier`. This also means every SCOPE-2 path (stage/missing-info) gets the *same* final routing check, rather than three independently-written ones that could drift out of sync.
- **Reversible?:** yes — adding a direct `stage_classifier -> escalation` / `missing_info -> escalation` conditional edge later is additive; it would just make the short-circuit in `guideline_synthesis_agent` unreachable for those two paths, not wrong.

### 73. `notify_reviewers` is a documented no-op when `REVIEW_WEBHOOK_URL` is unset
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-14 / Phase 3
- **Requirement ID(s):** ARCH §10.2 (escalation agent tool: `notify_reviewers`), ARCH §12.1
- **Ambiguous or undecided:** ARCH names `notify_reviewers` as a tool the escalation agent calls but does not specify a delivery mechanism (email, Slack, paging), and none is configured in this build (no dev/CI target for one).
- **Decision & rationale:** `app.agents.escalation_agent._notify_reviewers` checks `settings.review_webhook_url` (already a config field, default `""`) and, when unset, logs `hitl_escalation_created_no_webhook` and returns — a documented no-op, not a silent failure or a stub that pretends to have sent something. Reviewers still see every open escalation via `GET /review-queue` (ARCH §14.2) regardless of whether a push notification fired, so nothing is actually lost by this being a no-op in dev/CI. When set, it POSTs `{escalation_id, trigger_code}` to the configured webhook and logs (never raises) on delivery failure — a failed notification must never block persisting the escalation itself.
- **Reversible?:** yes — swapping the plain webhook POST for a richer notification channel (email/Slack/paging) only touches this one function's body.

### 75. `/query` derives a UUID from the dev-auth fallback's bare string `user_id`
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-14 / Phase 3
- **Requirement ID(s):** ARCH §11 (`memory.conversation.user_id` is a UUID column), ARCH-034
- **Ambiguous or undecided:** `app.api.deps.current_principal`'s Phase-1 dev fallback returns `Principal(user_id="dev-user", ...)` — a bare string, by design ("Phase 1: signatures + a permissive dev fallback so the app boots... Full RBAC... is Phase 4"). `POST /query` needs a real UUID to create/own a `memory.conversation` row now, in Phase 3, well before Phase 4's real auth exists.
- **Decision & rationale:** `app.api.deps.principal_uuid` parses `principal.user_id` as a UUID and, only on failure, deterministically derives one via `uuid.uuid5` against a fixed namespace constant — the same string always maps to the same UUID within this deployment, so rows created by the same dev principal (a conversation in `/query`, a `hitl_decision.reviewer_id` in `/hitl/escalations/{id}/decision`) stay attributed to the same synthetic identity across requests. Lives in `app.api.deps` (next to `Principal` itself) so every route using it shares one definition rather than each route inventing its own namespace constant. This is not a change to `Principal` or `current_principal` — it disappears automatically once Phase 4 issues real UUID principals, since `uuid.UUID(principal.user_id)` then succeeds on the first branch and the `uuid5` fallback is never reached.
- **Reversible?:** yes — a single helper function; removing it once real auth lands is a one-line deletion at each call site.

### 76. Missing-info agent (SCOPE-2.2) does not escalate — released, cited "what's missing" answer instead
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-14 / Phase 3
- **Requirement ID(s):** ARCH §9.2 (SCOPE-2.2), ARCH §12.1 (`RELEASE_POLICY[MISSING_CRITICAL_INFO] == "held"`, Phase 1)
- **Ambiguous or undecided:** the Phase-1 `app.hitl.triggers.RELEASE_POLICY` table (already committed, tested) marks `missing_critical_info` as `"held"`, which reads as "always escalate when info is missing." But ARCH §9.2 itself describes SCOPE-2.2 as "clarification-seeking... low-risk... keep it in scope," alongside SCOPE-1 as one of the two directly-answerable in-scope capabilities — nothing there suggests every missing-info detection should be held for clinician review before the clinician even sees the request. Building the agent to escalate on any missing field, discovered while implementing it in this phase, would make SCOPE-2.2 behave identically to a hard escalation path (like `stage_classification_uncertain`) despite ARCH's own text treating it as materially lower-risk.
- **Decision & rationale:** `missing_info_agent` populates `state["missing_info"]` and does **not** set `state["escalation"]`. The list feeds `guideline_synthesis_agent`'s `scope2_context` input, producing a normal released, cited, reported-content answer ("Guideline X requires field Y, which is not present in the record") through the same grounding gate as any SCOPE-1 answer — no special-casing needed downstream. The `missing_critical_info` trigger code and its `"held"` policy entry are left exactly as committed in Phase 1 (untouched, still available) for a stronger future case this agent doesn't currently produce (e.g. so much missing that no safe answer of any kind, including a missing-info request, can be assembled) — this decision narrows *when* the agent fires that trigger, from "always" to "not yet used," rather than removing the trigger or its policy.
- **Reversible?:** yes — reintroducing an escalation call at the end of `missing_info_agent.run` (e.g. above some missing-field-count threshold) is additive and does not change the module's other logic (retrieval reuse, field mapping, presence checking).

### 77. IRR secondary stats (Gwet's AC2, ICC(2,k)) not implemented — Krippendorff's alpha only
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-14 / Phase 3
- **Requirement ID(s):** ARCH §14.4 ("Secondary reported stats: Gwet's AC2 (skew robustness), ICC(2,k) (descriptive only)")
- **Ambiguous or undecided:** none — a scope decision. ARCH itself marks these as descriptive-only, not the evidentiary statistic (that's Krippendorff's alpha, ARCH-021), and `eval.irr_batch.secondary` is a free-form JSONB column, not a fixed schema requiring specific keys.
- **Decision & rationale:** `app.rubric.irr` implements `krippendorff_alpha_ordinal`/`per_domain_irr` only. `IRRBatch.secondary` is populated as `{}` (`app.rubric.tasks._run_compute_slice_irr`) rather than left unset, so the column's presence/shape is stable for a future writer. Implementing two more statistical measures whose ARCH-stated role is "descriptive only" is additional feature surface prompt.txt's Phase 3 checklist ("inter-rater reliability computation") doesn't require for the evidence-gathering purpose ARCH-021 describes — "production-grade patterns, minimal feature surface" (constraint #5).
- **Reversible?:** yes — additive; a future `gwet_ac2`/`icc_2k` function slots into `IRRBatch.secondary` and `IRRScore` is unaffected (it already carries a `metric` string field, not hardcoded to one metric name).

### 78. Auto-question generation task draws from the bundled synthetic dataset, seeded round-robin
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-14 / Phase 3
- **Requirement ID(s):** ARCH §15.1 step 2 ("pick a source record")
- **Ambiguous or undecided:** none beyond what DEVIATIONS #67 already flagged as deferred (step 2 needs a live, ingested corpus to match a record against a guideline's applicability criteria — not available in this session). `app.eval.tasks.generate_questions` still needs *some* record source to call `generate_question` with, to close the "returns a dict; a caller writes it to the DB" gap #67 also flagged.
- **Decision & rationale:** `_load_synthetic_records` reads `PATIENT_RECORDS_DIR/synthetic/patients.json` (the same bundled dataset `scripts/generate_synthetic_records.py` produces); `_run_generate_questions` shuffles it with the caller's `seed` (reproducible test sets) and assigns records to composition slots round-robin — real synthetic records, no invented data, just no guideline-fit judgement behind which record fills which slot. `target_guideline_topic` is left `None` throughout, which `generate_question` already treats as valid (the narrative still describes only the patient, never the guideline it doesn't yet know). A future step-2 implementation replaces the round-robin selection with a guideline-matched one; `generate_question`'s call shape doesn't change.
- **Reversible?:** yes — `_load_synthetic_records` / the shuffle-and-cycle logic are isolated in `_run_generate_questions`; swapping in real record selection touches only that function.

### 79. `eval.result` row creation (linking a live answer or harness run to a ratable result) is out of scope
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-14 / Phase 3
- **Requirement ID(s):** ARCH §14.2 (open review queue), ARCH §16 (eval harness)
- **Ambiguous or undecided:** ARCH describes the rubric workflow as operating on `eval.result` rows, and the eval harness as scoring against `eval.eval_question`, but never specifies exactly when a `Result` row is created — from every `/query` answer, only from eval-harness runs, only on explicit clinician "submit for rating," or some sampling policy (`review_sampling` is a defined escalation trigger, suggesting *not* every answer).
- **Decision & rationale:** implemented the workflow mechanics that operate ON existing `Result` rows — `submit_rating`, the open queue (`/review-queue`), IRR computation, archival — all built and tested against real (fake-session) `Result`/`RatingRound`/`RubricRating` rows. Did NOT implement automatic `Result` creation from `POST /query` answers or from `app.eval.harness.run_harness` (the harness currently persists only the aggregate `EvalRun.report`, not one `Result` row per scored question) — that requires a real policy decision (sample every answer? only auto-generated eval questions? at what rate?) that ARCH leaves open, and inventing one would be exactly the kind of unrequested design "don't design for hypothetical future requirements" warns against. `app.api.routes.review_queue`'s AAD convention for `Result.answer_enc` (`b"eval-result-answer:" + result.id`) is defined now as the contract a future writer must follow, so the reader (already built) doesn't need to change when that writer lands.
- **Reversible?:** n/a — a scoping decision; the future writer is purely additive to what's built (it only needs to construct `Result` rows correctly, everything downstream already consumes them).

### 80. Rubric ratings (rank mode) are not their own audit-event category
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-14 / Phase 3
- **Requirement ID(s):** ARCH §18, PRD-085
- **Ambiguous or undecided:** the rank-mode rating action (`POST /rubric/results/{id}/ratings`) isn't a HITL accept-axis decision (already audited via `hitl_action` in `app.hitl.decisions.apply_decision`) and doesn't cleanly match any of `AuditEvent.action`'s documented values.
- **Decision & rationale:** left unaudited for now — a real, acknowledged gap, not a deliberate "this doesn't need it" call: rubric ratings are the evidence-gathering input to a regulatory conformity story (ARCH §14), so an argument exists that they should be audit-logged too. Not adding a speculative `action="rubric_rating"` value (or overloading `record_access` the way `app.memory.patient_context.write_context` did, DEVIATIONS #72, which fit that category more naturally) without checking whether the rubric workflow's own tables (`rating_round.submitted_at`, `rubric_rating.rated_at`) already serve the audit-trail purpose for this specific action — they do provide a timestamped, attributable, immutable-in-practice (no UPDATE path exposed by `submit_rating`) record, which weakens the urgency without eliminating the gap. Flagged rather than silently resolved either way.
- **Reversible?:** yes — adding a `write_event(..., action="hitl_action", detail={"op": "rubric_rating", ...})` call to `app.rubric.workflow.submit_rating` is additive and would not change its existing return contract.

### 81. Real-Postgres findings: `scripts/seed_db.py` wired for real; Krippendorff's alpha degenerate (unanimous, single-item) case
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-14 / Phase 3
- **Requirement ID(s):** ARCH §14.1 (rubric domain reference data), ARCH-021 (IRR)
- **Ambiguous or undecided:** none — both are bugs a real, ephemeral-Postgres run of the rubric workflow surfaced, that no offline fake-session test could (fakes don't enforce FK constraints or call the real `krippendorff` package).
- **Decision & rationale:**
  - `scripts/seed_db.py` was still a Phase-1 print-only stub ("Phase 2 wires the actual DB writes" — never done, since Phase 2 was scoped to ingestion/retrieval, not IAM/rubric seeding). `eval.rubric_rating.domain_code` has a real FK constraint to `eval.rubric_domain.code`; with that table empty, `app.rubric.workflow.submit_rating` cannot insert a single rating against a real database — confirmed by running the actual workflow (not a fake) against an ephemeral Postgres and hitting `ForeignKeyViolation`. Implemented the DB-write half of the script (idempotent upserts for `eval.rubric_domain` from `app.rubric.domains.RUBRIC_DOMAINS`, `iam.role` from `ROLES`, and the five documented demo users + role assignments) since this is necessary, not optional, infrastructure for Phase 3's own rubric workflow to function at all.
  - `krippendorff_alpha_ordinal` propagated a `ValueError: There has to be more than one value in the domain` from the reference `krippendorff` package whenever every present rating for a domain is identical — the exact, entirely plausible case of three raters unanimously agreeing (e.g. all score `5` on `safety`), and the *only* case that can arise for a single-item (per-result) computation with no disagreement at all. This crashed `compute_result_irr_and_archive` and would have prevented archival of any unanimously-rated result. Fixed by detecting zero-variance data (`len(set(present)) <= 1`) before calling the library and returning `1.0` directly — unanimous agreement is the definitional limit of perfect reliability, so this is a correct value, not a suppressed error, and is documented as such in the function's own docstring.
- **Reversible?:** yes for both — `seed_db.py`'s upserts are idempotent and independent of any other change; the alpha degenerate-case check is a small guard clause with its own regression tests (`test_unanimous_single_item_returns_perfect_agreement_not_a_crash`, `test_all_missing_returns_zero_not_a_crash`).

### 82. Real-Postgres findings: LangGraph checkpointer DSN format, DDL privilege, and target schema
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-14 / Phase 3
- **Requirement ID(s):** ARCH §11, PRD-023
- **Ambiguous or undecided:** none — three compounding bugs a real, ephemeral-Postgres run of `app.agents.graph.build_graph()` (with no injected checkpointer, i.e. the real production path) surfaced in sequence, none reachable from offline tests (which always inject `MemorySaver`).
- **Decision & rationale:**
  1. `PostgresSaver.from_conn_string` wants a plain psycopg DSN (`postgresql://...`); `settings.database_url` is SQLAlchemy-formatted (`postgresql+psycopg://...`) — passing it straight through fails immediately with `psycopg.ProgrammingError: missing "=" ... in connection info string`. Fixed with `_to_psycopg_dsn`, which strips the `+driver` qualifier.
  2. `PostgresSaver.setup()` issues `CREATE TABLE` — DDL the restricted runtime `hrag_app` role (`database_url`) does not have, by the same least-privilege design DEVIATIONS.md #61 already established for Alembic (`InsufficientPrivilege: permission denied for schema public`). Fixed the same way: `get_checkpointer()` now runs `.setup()` once, in a short-lived connection using the elevated `alembic_database_url`, then discards that connection and opens the actual long-lived operational `PostgresSaver` with the regular `database_url`.
  3. `PostgresSaver`'s own tables (`checkpoints`, `checkpoint_writes`, `checkpoint_blobs`, `checkpoint_migrations`) are unqualified — they land wherever the connection's `search_path` resolves, normally `public`, which `hrag_app` has zero grants on (every grant in `01_schemas_roles.sql` is scoped to the platform's seven named schemas). Rather than adding a `public`-schema grant (a new, ungoverned surface), `_with_search_path` points both the setup and operational connections at `memory` — the schema `01_schemas_roles.sql`'s own comment already earmarked for "conversations, messages, patient_context, checkpoints" (line 12) and which already carries `ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES ... GRANT SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE ON TABLES TO hrag_app` — so tables the elevated setup connection creates there are immediately usable by `hrag_app` with **no changes to `01_schemas_roles.sql`** at all. Verified end-to-end: `\dt memory.*` shows the four checkpoint tables owned by `hrag_admin`, and a real graph run persisted 7 checkpoints for one thread, readable back via `graph.get_state_history`.
- **Reversible?:** yes — all three fixes are internal to `app.memory.checkpointer`; none change `get_checkpointer()`'s public signature or `app.agents.graph.build_graph()`'s calling convention.

### 84. HITL accept axis: added `out_of_scope` as a fourth action (operator-directed)
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-14 / Phase 3
- **Requirement ID(s):** ARCH §13.2, PRD-032
- **Ambiguous or undecided:** the operator directed this addition explicitly ("full/partial/reject accept axis should also include out of scope"), so the *whether* isn't a judgment call — the *how* (its precise state effects, resolution string, and whether it requires a reason code) is, since ARCH §13.2 only defined three actions and didn't anticipate a fourth.
- **Decision & rationale:** `HitlAcceptAction.OUT_OF_SCOPE = "out_of_scope"` added alongside the existing three. Modeled its state effects on `REJECT`'s (nothing is released/shown; **all** provisional `patient_context` entries for the result are rolled back via the same `rollback_provisional_for_result` path; `reason_code` is required) because both are "nothing should be shown or retained" outcomes — but kept it a **distinct** resolution value (`out_of_scope`, not folded into `rejected`) and a distinct `EFFECTS`/audit description, because the two answer different questions: `reject` says "the system attempted an answer and got it wrong" (a grounding/synthesis quality signal), while `out_of_scope` says "the system should never have attempted an answer to this at all" (a routing/scope-classification signal — potentially pointing at a gap in `app.scope.classifier`'s deterministic lexical backstop, DEVIATIONS #68). Pooling them into one `rejected` bucket would blur that distinction in any evidence report built from `hitl_decision`/`escalation` rows, which is the exact kind of signal separation ARCH §14's whole conformity-evidence framing cares about. Updated `ARCHITECTURE.md` §13 (both the axis-count comment and the §13.2 effects table) and `ARCHITECTURE-ESSENTIALS.md` §7 in the same change, per CLAUDE.md §6.
- **Reversible?:** yes — `HitlAcceptAction` is a plain `StrEnum`; `escalation.resolution`/`hitl_decision.action` are unconstrained `String(16)` columns with no DB-level CHECK constraint, so no migration was needed to accommodate the new value (verified: `"out_of_scope"` is 12 characters, fits the existing column width).

### 85. Cleared the pre-existing `ruff check`/`ruff format` backlog (operator-directed)
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-14 / Phase 3
- **Requirement ID(s):** CLAUDE.md §4 (ruff format/check), none specific to a requirement — a code-quality cleanup
- **Ambiguous or undecided:** the operator directed this explicitly ("fix that backlog") after `make lint` was shown to fail on 229 pre-existing findings (debt from Phase 1/2, confirmed via file-provenance diff at the time) plus 20 unformatted files. The *how* for each finding class is the judgment call.
- **Decision & rationale:**
  - **`B008` (`Depends()` in argument defaults) across `app/api/**`:** formalized the already-established, previously-unmarked project convention (confirmed at Checkpoint 2) as a `per-file-ignores` entry in `pyproject.toml` rather than adding `noqa` to ~39 call sites. This is FastAPI's own documented pattern — the rule exists to catch a different mistake (a shared mutable/expensive default) that doesn't apply, since FastAPI calls `Depends()` fresh per request.
  - **`PLR2004`/`PLC0415` in `tests/*`:** also moved to `per-file-ignores` rather than patched call-by-call (~140 sites combined). Literal HTTP status codes/counts in test assertions, and imports inside a test function body to construct a fake/offline double next to the one test that needs it, are established, correct idioms throughout this suite (including dozens I wrote myself across Phases 2–3) — not smells ruff's default config anticipates for test code specifically.
  - **`PLC0415` in production code** (`app/logging.py`, `app/auth/provider.py`, `app/llm/stub_server.py`): kept deferred with a `noqa` + one-line rationale, not moved to top-level — each is a genuine optional-dependency fallback (`structlog`) or lazy-selection pattern (only the configured `AuthProvider`/only-when-run-as-a-script `uvicorn`) where hoisting the import would change behavior, not just style.
  - **`PLC0415` for `yaml`/`pypdf`** (`app/ingestion/eav.py`, `scripts/ingest_deidentified_records.py`, `scripts/prepare_sample_guidelines.py`): hoisted to module level instead — both are hard (non-optional) dependencies already in `pyproject.toml`, so there was no behavioral reason for the deferral; verified via each file's own test suite after the move.
  - **`PLR0912` in `app/ingestion/eav.py`'s `apply_mapping`** (21 branches): split into six single-purpose helper functions (`_apply_single_value_fields`, `_apply_vitals_timestamps`, `_apply_list_targets` + its per-item helper, `_apply_derived_fields`, `_apply_defaults`), mirroring the extraction pattern already used in `app.records.access.extract_features` this phase. Pure extraction — `test_eav_mapping.py` passes unchanged, confirming behavior is identical.
  - **Remaining `PLR2004` in production code** (`app/config.py`, `app/eval/question_gen/planner.py`, `app/ingestion/topics.py`, `app/rubric/domains.py`): given real names (`_QGEN_COMPOSITION_FIELDS`, `_HARD_FRACTION_CAP_PERCENT`, `_MIN_WORD_LENGTH`, `_EXPECTED_DOMAIN_COUNT`, ...) — these actually document a meaningful threshold, unlike the test-assertion case.
  - **E501 in the `prepare_sample_guidelines.py` synthetic fixture strings:** the three flagged lines were a dict key sharing its physical line with a triple-quoted string's *first content line* (a markdown H1 heading) — wrapping the heading text would corrupt the fixture content. Used `"""\` (backslash-newline string continuation) to move the heading onto its own (short) line without changing the string's runtime value by a single byte — verified via `test_prepare_guidelines.py` passing unchanged.
  - **A self-caught regression:** `tests/test_eval_routes.py`'s `dependency_overrides[get_db] = lambda: _FakeSession()` was flagged `PLW0108` ("lambda may be unnecessary").
