  - **Reduced marker/line/font sizes** (e.g. chart 1/3 markersize 7-9→4-5, linewidth 1.5-2.5→1-1.8, tick/legend fontsize→7, title fontsize 12→9) since each panel's usable area shrank from the original standalone ~9.5in×5.5in to roughly 18cm×7cm (≈7in×2.75in) minus legend margin — the original sizes were tuned for the larger, single-chart canvas and would have been disproportionate/crowded at panel scale.
  - **Chart 2's legend shortened**: `"k={lbl} (dotted = RRF baseline at that k)"` (9 long entries) → `"k={lbl}"` with a short legend `title="dotted = RRF at that k"` instead — the reserved legend margin is now shared across all three panels' full figure width, not sized per-chart, so a verbose per-entry format that fit the old standalone 9.5in-wide chart 2 no longer reliably fits.
  - **Deleted the three old per-chart output files** (`chart1_k_vs_recall_by_alpha.png`, `chart2_alpha_vs_recall_by_k.png`, `chart3_alpha_vs_mrr.png`) from the repo and the live container's `reports/` directory; new single output is `retrieval_tuning_report.png`.
- **Verified**: offline — `ruff check`/`ruff format --check` clean, `tests/test_retrieval_tuning.py` (10 passed — no test touches `report.py`, confirmed by grep before this change; only `scripts/run_retrieval_weight_sweep.py` calls `generate_reports`, and its usage — iterate `paths`, print each — is unchanged by the one-vs-three-element list). Live — re-ran the real sweep inside the live `api` container against the same 67-question corpus, regenerated the combined PNG, copied it out and inspected it directly: three legible panels, correct A/B/C labels, legends fully inside the canvas (no clipping), exact target pixel dimensions confirmed via `PIL.Image.open(...).size` (4251×4960 @ 600dpi).
- **Reversible?:** yes — a rendering-layer refactor (how the existing computed data is drawn/saved); no change to `sweep.py`'s computation, no schema/API/production-path change. Reverting to three separate `Figure`s per chart is a `report.py`-only change.

### 130. Panel letters moved outside the plot area; "MRR" spelled out as "Mean Reciprocal Rank" on chart 3
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-17 / Phase 6, immediately following #129, same live stack
- **Requirement ID(s):** PRD-109/ARCH-040
- **What actually happened:** two follow-up requests on the combined report from #129: (1) move the A/B/C panel letters outside the plot area (they were inside each axes' top-left corner, with a backing patch to stay legible over data — #129), and (2) spell out "MRR" as "Mean Reciprocal Rank" on chart 3 (panel C)'s title and y-axis label.
- **Decision & rationale:**
  - `_panel_label`: repositioned from inside the axes (`(0.015, 0.97)`, `va="top"`, with a `_SURFACE`-colored `bbox` backing patch) to outside it, above and slightly left of the top-left corner (`(-0.03, 1.16)`, `va="bottom"`). Outside the plot area, no data line can ever pass under the label, so the backing patch is no longer needed and was removed.
  - This needed more vertical room per panel than the #129 layout reserved (which only left space for the existing title above each axes, not a label above *that*): `generate_reports`'s `fig.subplots_adjust` changed `top` 0.965→0.93 (more margin above the topmost panel, A, which has no panel above it to borrow space from) and `hspace` 0.55→0.65 (more room between panels B and C's letters and the panel above them). Confirmed by re-rendering and visually inspecting: both the letter and the panel's own title fit fully inside the canvas on all three panels, no clipping at the figure's top edge.
  - `chart_alpha_vs_mrr`: `ax.set_ylabel(f"MRR@{MRR_K}")` → `f"Mean Reciprocal Rank@{MRR_K}"`; chart title likewise. Left `MRR_K`, the `mrr()` metric function, and other internal identifiers unchanged — the request was about the rendered chart text, not the code's own naming.
- **Verified**: offline — `ruff check`/`ruff format --check` clean, `tests/test_retrieval_tuning.py` (10 passed, unchanged — no test touches chart rendering or label text). Live — re-ran the real sweep inside the live `api` container, regenerated the combined PNG, copied it out and visually confirmed: all three panel letters sit clearly above and outside their respective plot areas with no overlap or clipping, and panel C reads "Mean Reciprocal Rank@24" in both its title and y-axis label.
- **Reversible?:** yes — a label-position change (with matching margin adjustments) and a display-string change; no schema, API, computation, or production-path change.

### 131. Biomedical embedding ablation (SapBERT / MedCPT vs. BM25) implemented and run for real — `PHASE2-EMBEDDING-ABLATION-PROPOSAL.md` approved and executed; one real bug found and fixed; real result recorded
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-17 / evidence-gathering for a possible future Phase 2 reopening (not itself a phase), approved same day
- **Requirement ID(s):** PRD-110, ARCH-041
- **What was implemented, matching the approved proposal:** `app/eval/model_ablation/` (`encoders.py` — SapBERT/MedCPT loading + `[CLS]`-pooling, with a `stub` mode for offline tests; `ablation.py` — brute-force in-memory cosine ranking over the full corpus + client-side RRF combine across three arms (`sapbert_bm25`, `medcpt_bm25`, `sapbert_medcpt_bm25`) plus a freshly-recomputed production-RRF reference; `report.py` — a 2-panel PNG, mirroring the Phase 6 report's palette/layout conventions), `scripts/run_model_ablation.py`, `tests/test_model_ablation.py` (9 tests, stub backend, no network). One additive `QdrantVectorStore` method, `scroll_all` (full corpus dump, payload-only, no vectors) — used only by this harness, not the `VectorStore` Protocol, same justification pattern as Phase 6's `single_vector_search`. New config, read only by this tooling, never by production: `MODEL_ABLATION_BACKEND`, `SAPBERT_MODEL_ID`/`_VERIFIED`, `MEDCPT_QUERY_MODEL_ID`/`MEDCPT_ARTICLE_MODEL_ID`/`_VERIFIED`.
- **Judgment calls made while implementing** (per proposal §10's commitment to log these the moment they're made):
  1. **RRF constant reused from `settings.rrf_k` (60)**, not a separately-invented value — matches `vectorstore.py`'s own documented assumption that 60 is "Qdrant's own internal constant" (DEVIATIONS #49). The client-side RRF here is not expected to reproduce Qdrant's fused scores number-for-number (there is no Qdrant collection to compare against for the two new channels), only to combine all three ablation arms consistently with each other and with the same constant production nominally uses.
  2. **`_bm25_rank`'s zero-score tail is `sorted()`, not left in whatever order Qdrant/`set` iteration happens to return.** A chunk with no lexical overlap with the query gets no score from the sparse query at all; those chunks must still get *some* deterministic rank for the RRF sum to be reproducible run-to-run.
  3. **The production RRF reference is recomputed fresh per question** (a real `store.hybrid_search` call), not copied from Phase 6's historical numbers as the original proposal (§5) suggested might be sufficient. Decided during implementation that reusing already-existing code for an always-current, always-consistent reference was simpler and more robust than embedding a specific historical number that could go stale — a refinement of the proposal, not a deviation from its intent (it explicitly called this "no new computation," which recomputing via an already-existing method still satisfies in spirit).
- **Real bug found only by real data, not the stub-backend tests:** `Encoder.encode`'s tokenizer call had no explicit `max_length`, relying on the tokenizer's own default — for at least one real guideline chunk (4481 tokens), that default did not truncate, and the forward pass crashed (`RuntimeError: size of tensor a (4481) must match size of tensor b (512)`) instead of silently truncating as intended. Fixed with an explicit `max_length=512` (the standard BERT-family position-embedding limit, matching the existing `reranker_max_length` convention elsewhere in this codebase) and batched encoding (`batch_size=16`, avoiding one single giant 314-text forward pass). The tiny fixture texts in `test_model_ablation.py` never exercised this path — same class of gap as several earlier "only real data found this" entries in this file (#122's gold-chunk gap, #122's chart y-range/legend bugs).
- **Model repo ids confirmed to exist; pooling method still not independently re-verified.** Both `SAPBERT_MODEL_ID` and the two `MEDCPT_*_MODEL_ID` placeholders resolved successfully against the real HuggingFace Hub (tokenizer + weights loaded without error) — the proposal's "best knowledge, unverified" ids turned out to be correct as repo ids. `[CLS]`-token pooling (this implementation's choice) was not independently re-checked against either model's current reference usage in this session; `*_VERIFIED` flags remain `false` until that specific check happens, per the proposal's own §6 standard (repo-id existence and "verified against the model's current card" are not the same claim).
- **Real result (informational, not a production change; 67 calibration questions, the same live 3-document guideline corpus, single run):** on MRR@24, **SapBERT+BM25 leads** (≈0.18) over the production RRF baseline (≈0.16, recomputed fresh this run — close to, not identical to, the ≈0.157 figure recorded in DEVIATIONS #123 from a different code path, as expected for two independently-computed RRF baselines); **MedCPT+BM25 underperforms it** (≈0.10); the 3-way **SapBERT+MedCPT+BM25 combination sits close to, slightly below, RRF** (≈0.14) — MedCPT's weaker showing pulls the 3-way blend down from where SapBERT alone lands. Recall@k: all three arms track closely with RRF through the swept range and converge to the same ≈0.68 ceiling by k≈50 (the corpus's own ceiling, per DEVIATIONS #123 — not a fusion-strategy effect). **Reading against proposal §8's bar**: SapBERT is the more promising of the two candidates on this single run, but N=67 over one small corpus, with no repeated splits or significance check, is not "robustly improves ... over the current RRF baseline" — this does **not** trigger a Phase-2-reopening proposal on its own. A natural next step, if this line of investigation continues, would be re-running with `[CLS]`-pooling independently verified and/or a mean-pooling alternative, and checking stability across a couple of random splits of the eval-question pool (the same robustness bar Phase 6's own §7 set) — not undertaken here, out of scope for this approved proposal.
- **Verified**: offline — `ruff check`/`ruff format --check` clean, `tests/test_model_ablation.py` (9 passed), full `make test` equivalent (547 passed, up from 538 — no existing test touched or broken), `mypy` at the same pre-existing baseline (only the already-accepted `pandas`/`seaborn` missing-stubs findings and the unrelated `app/logging.py` finding — no new findings). Live — real run against the live `dev` stack described above; one real PNG produced and visually reviewed (2 legible panels, panel letters outside the plot area matching the Phase 6 report's established convention, no data/legend collision).
- **Reversible?:** yes — everything here is new, additive code (`app/eval/model_ablation/`, one new `QdrantVectorStore` method, new config read only by this tooling, one new Makefile target, one new optional-dependency combination already covered by existing extras). Nothing in `app.retrieval.hybrid`/`vectorstore.hybrid_search` (the production path) was changed, no new Qdrant vectors were persisted, and no new `audit.audit_event` writes were added.

### 132. Panel B (MRR@24 by arm) changed from a plain bar chart to a dot plot with 95% bootstrap CI error bars (user request) — revealed the four arms' point estimates aren't actually distinguishable
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-17 / immediately following #131, same live stack
- **Requirement ID(s):** PRD-110/ARCH-041
- **What actually happened:** follow-up request to add 95% bootstrap confidence intervals to panel B and switch its mark from bars to a dot plot with error bars. This needed `run_ablation` to retain something it previously discarded: `mrr_rows` only ever stored each arm's *mean* MRR, not the per-question scores the mean was computed from — no CI is possible without them.
- **Decision & rationale:**
  - **Percentile bootstrap, not a normal-approximation interval.** `ablation._bootstrap_ci` resamples each arm's per-question MRR scores with replacement `_BOOTSTRAP_N=10,000` times, takes each resample's mean, and reports the [2.5, 97.5] percentiles of that distribution. Chosen over a normal/t-approximation because MRR scores are a bounded, discrete-ish, right-skewed quantity (mostly `0` or `1/rank` for small rank) — exactly the kind of distribution a normal approximation fits poorly, while the bootstrap makes no shape assumption.
  - **Fixed seed (`_BOOTSTRAP_SEED=1234`), not left to numpy's default entropy source.** Without it, regenerating the same report from the same underlying data would jitter the CI bounds slightly on every run — a report whose interval width changes for no reason other than re-running it would undermine trust in the number itself. Both the seed and the resample count are arbitrary-but-fixed judgment calls, not derived from anything.
  - **`AblationResult.mrr_rows` gained two fields** (`ci_low`, `ci_high`) rather than a parallel structure — kept the one-row-per-arm shape callers already expect, additive only.
  - **Chart mark**: `chart_mrr_by_arm` now draws `ax.errorbar` (the interval) plus `ax.scatter` (the point estimate) per arm, replacing `ax.bar` — same categorical color assignment and RRF-as-diamond/gray convention as the bar version, just a different mark for the same identity encoding. Title updated to name the interval (`"... (95% bootstrap CI)"`) so the chart is self-describing without needing the caption/DEVIATIONS entry to know what the whiskers are.
- **Real finding, only visible once uncertainty was shown (informational, not a production change):** all four arms' 95% CIs overlap substantially — SapBERT+BM25 ≈0.18 [≈0.13, ≈0.24], MedCPT+BM25 ≈0.10 [≈0.07, ≈0.14], SapBERT+MedCPT+BM25 ≈0.14 [≈0.10, ≈0.19], RRF production ≈0.16 [≈0.12, ≈0.22] (bounds read off the rendered chart, real run, same 67 questions and corpus as #131). No arm's interval excludes another's point estimate. This makes explicit, with an actual interval rather than just a caution in prose, what DEVIATIONS #131 already flagged qualitatively: SapBERT's higher point estimate on this run is not statistically distinguishable from the RRF baseline at this sample size — reinforcing, more rigorously than before, that this ablation does **not** meet proposal §8's "robustly improves" bar and does not trigger a Phase-2-reopening proposal.
- **Verified**: offline — `ruff check`/`ruff format --check` clean, `tests/test_model_ablation.py` (13 passed, up from 9 — 4 new: CI-bracketing check added to the existing aggregation test, plus determinism/degenerate/empty/realistic-n tests for `_bootstrap_ci` directly), full suite (551 passed, up from 547), `mypy` unchanged from #131's baseline. Live — re-ran the real sweep inside the live `api` container against the same 67-question corpus and regenerated the PNG; panel B visually reviewed (4 legible dot+whisker points, correct colors/markers, RRF still the gray diamond, no overlap with panel A, title names the interval).
- **Reversible?:** yes — two additive dataclass fields, one new pure function, and one chart function's mark; no schema, API, computation-of-existing-metrics, or production-path change.

### 133. Phase 5 (React frontend) reopened for a WCAG 2.2 AA contrast/accessibility pass — analysis only in this entry, no code changed yet
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-17 / Phase 5 reopened (originally closed, Checkpoint 5 approved 2026-09-14)
- **Requirement ID(s):** PRD-107 (query/citation UI), PRD-031/PRD-032/PRD-040 (rank mode + accept axis)
- **What happened:** operator explicitly asked to reopen Phase 5 and requested a contrast/accessibility assessment (WCAG 2.2 AA/AAA) of `frontend/src`, plus mobile Likert-rubric UX guidance referencing `frontend/mobile_mockup.html` (a design-intent fragment, not wired into the app — its `var(--surface-1)` etc. tokens are undefined anywhere in the repo).
- **Scope interpretation (judgment call):** treated "reopen Phase 5" as reopening the existing, already-approved React frontend for a contrast/accessibility remediation pass — not a rebuild. Delivered a code-grounded audit (real computed contrast ratios against colors actually in `frontend/src`, e.g. `#888`/`#999` text-on-white failures in `CitationList.tsx`, `EscalationQueue.tsx`, `ReviewQueue.tsx`, `AnswerView.tsx`; the native-radio-table anti-pattern in `RubricForm.tsx`/`AcceptAxisControls.tsx`; the total absence of any hover/focus-visible/active styling or CSS file in the frontend) plus concrete fixes, but did **not** apply any code changes or add new dependencies (Tailwind/Radix) in this pass — those were offered as recommendations pending operator confirmation, since introducing a CSS framework and a component-primitives library is a stack decision beyond a pure contrast-value fix and outside what "evaluate contrast and visibility" alone authorizes.
- **New checkpoint required**: Phase 5's status is now "reopened," not "complete," until the operator reviews the audit, decides which fixes (and whether Tailwind/Radix) to apply, the changes are implemented and tested, and a new Checkpoint 5 approval is given — per CLAUDE.md §2, never chaining into implementation/Phase 6 work off an old approval.
- **Verified**: N/A — no code changed in this entry (audit/analysis only).
- **Reversible?:** yes — this entry and the README status-row edit are documentation only.

### 134. Wired up Tailwind CSS v4 + Radix UI primitives; rebuilt the rank-mode rubric as a one-domain-per-screen wizard; fixed two real correctness bugs found only by driving the actual rendered wizard, not by inspection
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-17 / Phase 5 (reopened, DEVIATIONS #133), operator approved "go ahead" on the Tailwind/Radix proposal
- **Requirement ID(s):** PRD-031, PRD-032, PRD-040, ARCH §13.2
- **What was implemented:** `frontend/package.json` gained `tailwindcss` + `@tailwindcss/vite` (dev) and `@radix-ui/react-radio-group`, `@radix-ui/react-collapsible`, `@radix-ui/react-progress` (runtime, though the progress bar itself is a plain styled `div` — Radix's `Progress` wasn't actually needed once built, left installed rather than pulled back out for a one-line diff). `vite.config.ts` adds the `@tailwindcss/vite` plugin. `frontend/src/index.css` (new) declares the audited color tokens (DEVIATIONS #133 — `ink`/`ink-muted` replacing the old `#666`/`#888`/`#999`, `accent`/`accent-strong` teal, `danger` red reserved for `reject`/`out_of_scope`, the disclaimer banner's proven-AAA colors kept byte-for-byte) via Tailwind v4's `@theme`, plus a global `:focus-visible` rule. Every component that previously used inline `style={}` (`App.tsx`, `DisclaimerBanner.tsx`, `QueryPanel.tsx`, `AnswerView.tsx`, `AnswerSegments.tsx`, `CitationList.tsx`, `EscalationQueue.tsx`, `ReviewQueue.tsx`, `LoginPage.tsx`, `QueryPage.tsx`, `ReviewPage.tsx`, `EscalationsPage.tsx`) was converted to Tailwind utility classes against these tokens; a shared `components/ui/Button.tsx` centralizes hover/active/disabled/focus styling so it can't drift per call site again. `RubricForm.tsx` was rebuilt as a stepper (one domain per screen, collapsible definition + all 5 anchors, a collapsible "view answer and citations" panel holding `candidateAnswer`, a segmented 1–5 `RadioGroup` at 52px targets, a sticky bottom Back/Next bar with an "N/11 rated" count) ending in a dedicated, larger-format accept-axis step. `AcceptAxisControls.tsx` (shared with `EscalationsPage.tsx`'s standalone decision form) was rebuilt as large `RadioGroup` cards, `reject`/`out_of_scope` the only ones styled with `--color-danger`.
- **Two real bugs found and fixed by actually driving the rendered wizard** (a throwaway Puppeteer-over-real-Chrome harness against `vite dev`, seeded with the real 11 domains from `backend/app/rubric/domains.py`; not present in the final diff — this is process, not a shipped artifact):
  1. **Domain-rating `RadioGroup` silently stopped recording scores from the 2nd domain onward.** Passing `value={value?.toString()}` (`undefined` for an unrated domain) made Radix's `RadioGroup` fall back to *uncontrolled* mode the first time an unrated domain rendered; uncontrolled mode then *kept* the previously-checked item's value internally, so the next domain visually rendered with the *prior* domain's rating pre-"checked" (with no matching `scores` entry) — and clicking that already-"checked"-per-Radix item fired no `onValueChange`, since Radix saw no value change. A rater would appear to select a rating and nothing would happen, with no error. Fixed by always passing a defined string (`""` for unrated) — see the comment at `RubricForm.tsx`'s `DomainStep` `RadioGroup.Root`.
  2. **Clicking "Next →" on the 11th (last) domain silently auto-submitted the rating with the untouched default `full_accept` value, before the rater ever saw the accept-axis step.** The Next/Submit button occupies the same JSX tree position across the `onAcceptStep` ternary, so React patches the *same* underlying `<button>` DOM node's `type` attribute (`"button"` → `"submit"`) in place rather than remounting it. The browser resolves a click's native default action against the element's type *after* all JS handlers finish running, so the click that advanced `step` to the accept-axis screen — synchronously re-rendering that same node as `type="submit"` — had its own default action resolved against the *new* type, submitting the form immediately. Confirmed via a debug build: `onSubmit` fired with `step=11` (i.e. immediately after the transition) on every 11th-domain "Next" click, before any accept-axis interaction. This would have contaminated every multi-rater IRR submission with a spurious, never-chosen `full_accept` decision. Fixed by making the submit button `type="button"` with an explicit `formRef.current?.requestSubmit()` call, so it no longer depends on native submit-button click-dispatch timing.
  - A third suspected issue (accept-axis cards appearing to show a stale selected color in screenshots) was investigated with a computed-style dump and ruled out: it was `transition-colors`'s ~150ms CSS transition caught mid-animation by a zero-delay scripted screenshot, not a real bug — confirmed by re-sampling after a 300ms settle (exact expected token colors, one card selected at a time). Recorded here so a future session doesn't re-chase the same non-bug from a similarly fast script.
- **Verified**: offline — `npx tsc -b` clean, `npx eslint src --max-warnings 0` clean (0 errors/warnings), `npm run build` succeeds (73 modules, 13.80kB CSS / 219.75kB JS bundle). Live — real interaction testing against `vite dev` (real Chrome, CDP-driven, no mocks beyond stubbing the one `/rubric/domains` network call with the real seed data) covering: all 11 domains ratable and advancing correctly post-fix, definition/answer collapsibles opening, accept-axis card selection (all 4 options, including the danger-styled pair), partial_accept's edited-answer textarea, focus-visible keyboard navigation, and a correct final submit payload. Not run against the live backend/full docker stack (out of scope for a frontend-only accessibility pass; `getRubricDomains`/`submitRating` themselves are untouched pass-through calls in `api/client.ts`).
- **Known pre-existing, unrelated `npm audit` findings**: `esbuild`/`vite` (dev-server-only CORS advisory) and `react-router`/`react-router-dom` (open-redirect / SSR-hydration advisories) — both predate this change, both require a breaking major-version bump (`vite@8`, `react-router-dom@7`) to fix, and neither was introduced by the packages added here (`tailwindcss`, `@tailwindcss/vite`, `@radix-ui/*`, none of which `npm audit` flagged). Left as-is; flagging rather than silently upgrading unrelated deps beyond this task's scope.
- **Reversible?:** yes — additive dependencies and a rewrite scoped to `frontend/`; no backend, schema, or API contract change. `RatingSubmission`/`AcceptAxisValue` payload shapes are unchanged (verified via the real submit payload above), so `api/client.ts` and the backend routes it calls needed no changes.

### 135. Live-verified the #134 rewrite against the real `dev`-profile docker stack (real Postgres/Qdrant/gateway) — closes the "not run against the live backend" gap #134 left open; two real findings, one already-safe, one flagged not fixed
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-18 / Phase 5 (reopened), operator asked to "spin up the backend stack and test it live"
- **Requirement ID(s):** PRD-031, PRD-032, PRD-040, PRD-107
- **What was done:** `make up` (dev profile) against the existing, previously-migrated-and-seeded `pg-data`/`qdrant-data` volumes from an earlier session (11 rubric domains, seeded demo users, a populated `eval.result` review queue from a prior eval run already present — nothing new seeded); rebuilt only the `frontend` image (`docker compose build frontend`) to pick up the Tailwind/Radix changes and recreated that container. Confirmed `EMBEDDING_BACKEND=local` and `LLM_GATEWAY_URL=https://host.docker.internal:8443` point at the operator's real self-hosted gateway (already running, unrelated to this repo) — so this pass exercises real synthesis, not the offline stub. Drove the real app through the real `proxy` container (`https://localhost`, real TLS, real nginx) with the same real-Chrome/CDP approach as #134, this time against the actual running stack: dev-login as `reviewer1@example.dev`, a real `/query` submission, then a real `/review` queue item rated end-to-end through the rebuilt wizard and submitted for real (persisted a real `rating_round` + `accept_action` row).
- **Real findings:**
  1. **A real query returned `escalated`** (`"Synthesis output could not be parsed into valid answer segments: model output is not valid JSON: Unterminated string starting at: line 36 column 14 (char 1857)"`, `trigger_code: grounding_failure`) for a longer question. This is the grounding safety net working as designed — PRD-NFR-2/CLAUDE.md §3's "never a general-knowledge answer" held under a real, messy LLM failure mode (the real gateway's output was truncated mid-JSON), not a frontend bug; `AnswerView.tsx`'s escalation card rendered it correctly (danger-red border, trigger code, outcome, all legible). Root cause (likely a `max_tokens` ceiling on the real gateway call cutting off a longer synthesis) is a backend/gateway tuning question, out of scope for this frontend pass — not investigated further here, flagged for whoever next touches `LLMGateway`/synthesis prompt sizing.
  2. **A second, shorter real query returned `no_guideline`** rather than a citation-backed answer — also correct, conservative grounding behavior for a real corpus/gateway combination, not a bug.
  3. **`ReviewQueue.tsx`/`EscalationQueue.tsx`'s queue-list `<table>` is not mobile-responsive** — confirmed live at 390px width against a real, multi-row queue (12 real rows): the 5-column table (`Result` id / `Provenance` / `Expected outcome` / `Observed outcome` / action button) overflows the viewport and requires horizontal scroll; some columns render off-screen with no visual affordance that more content exists sideways. This is pre-existing table markup (unchanged in shape by #134's Tailwind conversion, which only swapped inline `style` for equivalent utility classes) — not a regression introduced this session, but a real gap in the same "mobile-first clinical tool" spirit as #133/#134's wizard work. **Fixed same-day, operator approved**: see #136.
- **Verified**: live, against the real stack described above — `alembic current` at head, dev-login working for `reviewer1@example.dev` (`clinician`+`reviewer` roles), `GET /rubric/domains` returning the real 11 seeded domains through the real proxy, a full rate-all-11-domains-then-accept-axis-then-submit pass against a real `eval.result` row succeeding with the correct payload and UI confirmation ("Rating submitted (accept axis: full_accept)."), and both query outcomes above rendering correctly. Stack left running (not torn down) at the operator's implicit continuation of this session; no repo files changed by this entry beyond this log (all test scripts lived under the session scratchpad, not the repo).
- **Reversible?:** yes — this entry is verification only; no application code changed. The stack can be stopped with `make down` at any time.

### 136. Fixed the #135 queue-list mobile overflow — `ReviewQueue.tsx`/`EscalationQueue.tsx` now render a stacked card list below `sm`, the existing table at `sm` and up
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-18 / Phase 5 (reopened), same session as #133–#135, operator approved fixing it now
- **Requirement ID(s):** PRD-042, PRD-047, PRD-107
- **What was implemented:** both components now render two markups gated by Tailwind's `sm:` breakpoint rather than one: a `sm:hidden` stacked-card list (id + provenance on one line, expected/observed — or trigger/opened for escalations — stacked below, a full-width action button in the thumb-reach zone, matching the wizard's card language from #134) and a `hidden sm:table` version of the original table, unchanged in shape. Both markups render off the same `items` array and the same `onSelect` callback — no duplicated data-fetching or state, just two presentations of it, same pattern as any other responsive-by-CSS component in this codebase (nothing is mounted/unmounted by JS on resize, so there's no state-loss risk crossing the breakpoint).

Plain text label, not a rephrasing — "AI LLM response" is the operator's own wording, kept verbatim rather than editorialized (e.g. to "Answer" or "Synthesized response"), since it's their own review-tool vocabulary, not user-facing product copy elsewhere in the app.

### 138. Fixed a real bug in `compute_field_index`: repeating-group fields (`vitals`, `labs`, `examination_findings`, `interventions`, `medications`) never got indexed per-concept keys, so `missing_info_agent` (SCOPE-2.2) could report a criterion field as missing even when it was actually recorded
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-18 / Phase 2 (reopened 2026-09-18 for the `newborn_nbu_2021` EAV export update — new `field_name`/`context` values, explicit `NA` sentinel for not-assessed values), operator asked to fix it as part of investigating how "assessed-negative" (`FALSE`) vs "not assessed" (`NA`/absent) is captured
- **Requirement ID(s):** PRD-080, PRD-084, ARCH-034, ARCH-039, SCOPE-2.2 (PRD-052)
- **What was found:** `app.records.criteria.field_present_in_index` (and its own docstring) has always assumed `field_index` contains indexed per-item keys for repeating groups, e.g. `vitals.0.heart_rate_bpm` — falling back to a `{prefix}.*.{leaf}` scan when the flat concept path isn't a direct key. `app.ingestion.records.compute_field_index`/`_flatten_presence` never actually produced those keys: a list value collapsed to a single `{prefix}: len(value) > 0` flag (e.g. `"vitals": true`) with no per-item breakdown. This was undetected by tests because `test_records_access.py` and `test_missing_info_agent.py` both hand-construct `field_index` dicts already in the *expected* indexed shape, rather than deriving them from a real `PatientRecord` via `compute_field_index`. Net effect in `missing_info_agent.run`: `field_present_in_index(field_index, "vitals.heart_rate_bpm")` was always `False` for a real ingested record, even when heart rate was recorded — every vitals-sourced criterion field was reported as missing.
- **Also directly relevant to the operator's assessed-negative-vs-not-assessed question**: `examination_findings`/`interventions`/`medications` are `{name, <flag>}`-shaped (not "wide" per-concept like `vitals`), so a generic recursive flatten would have produced useless `.name`/`.present` keys, losing which concept (e.g. "apnoea") was assessed. The concept identifier (the `name`/`analyte` field's *value*) had to become part of the index *key*, never the index *value* — kept strictly to PRD-080's "names + null-ness only, never a value": the index says a member was *assessed* (an item exists at all — `ExamFinding.present`/`Intervention.active`/`Medication.active` are non-nullable bools, so an item only exists when TRUE/FALSE was recorded, never for `NA`/absent per `eav.py`'s existing `_build_list_target_item`), never whether it was positive or negative.
- **Fix**: `_flatten_presence` (`app/ingestion/records.py`) now, for list values: (a) always keeps the existing flat `{prefix}: any-recorded` flag (backward compatible — three existing tests assert this), and (b) additionally emits indexed keys — `_NAME_VALUE_LIST_FIELDS = {"examination_findings": ("name","present"), "interventions": ("name","active"), "medications": ("name","active"), "labs": ("analyte","value")}` drives `{prefix}.{idx}.{concept_name}: <value_key> is not None` for those four families; `vitals` (no name/value shape) falls through to generic recursive flattening, naturally producing `vitals.0.heart_rate_bpm`-style keys since `Vitals` fields are already per-concept.
- **Verified**: `backend/tests/test_ingest_records_persistence.py` — new `test_field_index_distinguishes_assessed_negative_from_not_assessed` (an assessed-negative `ExamFinding(present=False)` indexes as `True`/assessed; a member never in the list has no key at all; the literal `False` clinical value never appears among `examination_findings.*` index values), `test_field_index_indexes_vitals_by_concept_name`, and a direct regression test `test_field_present_in_index_resolves_real_vitals_field_index` proving `field_present_in_index` now actually resolves against a `compute_field_index`-derived index (previously only ever tested against hand-written fixtures). All 3 pre-existing `compute_field_index` tests plus the full backend suite still pass (554 passed).
- **Not fixed here (flagged, not actioned)**: `app.records.access._list_and_flag_features` still collapses `interventions`/`medications` to an `{family}.active` list of *active-only* names — an assessed-and-not-given intervention/medication (`active=False`) is still indistinguishable from "never assessed" at the *decrypted-feature* layer (unlike `examination_findings`, which already preserves `present=True/False` per-name in `extract_features`). This is a separate, larger change (feature-shape, not field_index) and wasn't part of what was asked; left for a future session if the operator wants that same distinction available to `get_patient_fields` callers, not just the field-index presence check.
- **Reversible?:** yes — additive indexed keys alongside the unchanged flat flag; no schema/migration change (`field_index` is a `JSONB` column), no change to what `ingest_records` persists structurally, no API contract change (`GET /records/{id}/fields` now returns more granular names, which is the intended fix, not a breaking change to its shape).

### 139. `newborn_nbu_2021` EAV export update: explicit `NA` sentinel for not-assessed values, and a new `maternal_risk_factors` context (`maternal_infection`, `prom`) — schema **v1.4.0**
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-18 / Phase 2 (reopened — README §Status), operator updated the dataset export and specified the new fields
- **Requirement ID(s):** PRD-002, PRD-006, PRD-080, ARCH-039
- **What changed in the export:** (1) rows with `field_value == "NA"` are now included explicitly for a field not assessed on a given patient, where previously such fields were simply absent from that patient's rows. (2) two new `field_name` values, `maternal_infection` and `prom`, both TRUE/FALSE, under a new `context` value `maternal_risk_factors`.
- **Decision & rationale:**
  1. **`NA` needed no mapping change.** `app/ingestion/eav.py`'s `_NULL_LITERALS` already includes `"na"` (case-insensitive) and every transform (including `_bool_truthy`, which backs list-target members) already treats it as null — identical to how a fully-absent row was always handled. Confirmed by a new test (`test_na_sentinel_means_not_assessed_distinct_from_false`, `tests/test_eav_mapping.py`) exercising an actual `"NA"` row through the real pivot + mapping path, not just the transform function in isolation.
  2. **`maternal_infection`/`prom` are maternal-history signs, not the newborn's own examination findings** — folding them into `examination_findings[]` would have been semantically wrong (that list is documented as "history/examination sign" of the patient, i.e. the newborn) and would have made a maternal risk factor indistinguishable from a neonatal sign in any downstream code keying off `examination_findings.<name>`. Added a new, additive, `schema_version`-gated field instead: `PatientRecord.maternal_risk_factors: list[ExamFinding]` (schema **v1.4.0**) — reuses `ExamFinding`'s exact shape (`name`, `present`, `recorded_at`) rather than defining a near-identical new class, since the shape and "present/absent sign" semantics are genuinely identical; only the list it lives in differs. `field_mapping.yaml` gained a `maternal_risk_factors` `list_targets` group with `members: [maternal_infection, prom]`, following the exact same declarative pattern as `examination_findings`/`interventions`/`medications` — no new code needed in `eav.py` itself.
  3. **`prom` is assumed to mean "premature/prolonged rupture of membranes"** (a standard obstetric risk factor for neonatal infection) — this was not confirmed by the operator, only inferred from clinical context (paired with `maternal_infection` under `maternal_risk_factors`). Flagged explicitly in `DATASET.md`'s variable table as an open assumption for operator confirmation; if wrong, only the human-readable label is affected — `field_mapping.yaml`'s `members` entry is just the literal source string `prom`, so no mapping code would need to change, only the documentation.
  4. **Every place that already special-cased `examination_findings` for the same "name lives in a value, not a key" reason got the parallel entry**, so the new field doesn't silently fall through to generic (and wrong) handling: `app.ingestion.records._NAME_VALUE_LIST_FIELDS` and `_CSV_LIST_FIELD_PREFIXES`, `app.records.criteria._LIST_FIELD_PREFIXES`, `app.records.access._list_and_flag_features` (features as `maternal_risk_factors.<name>`, mirroring `examination_findings.<name>`) — this is exactly the seam DEVIATIONS #138's fix introduced, applied to the field that motivated checking it.
  5. `data/record_schema.json` (hand-maintained mirror of `PatientRecord`'s schema, per DEVIATIONS #23/#32/#35/#39's precedent) updated to match: `schema_version: "1.4.0"`, new `maternal_risk_factors` property.
- **Verified**: full backend suite (557 tests) passes, including new/updated coverage — `test_na_sentinel_means_not_assessed_distinct_from_false` and a `maternal_risk_factors`-mapping assertion in `tests/test_eav_mapping.py`; `test_field_index_indexes_maternal_risk_factors_separately_from_exam_findings` in `tests/test_ingest_records_persistence.py`; `test_extract_features_keeps_maternal_risk_factors_distinct_from_exam_findings` in `tests/test_records_access.py`; `test_ingest_script_runs_with_complete_attestation`'s real-dataset `schema_version` assertion updated `1.3.0` → `1.4.0` and re-verified against the real 40,871-patient file. `ruff check`/`ruff format --check` clean on every changed file.
- **Reversible?:** yes — additive schema field + additive mapping group; no migration (patient_record.payload_enc is a JSON blob tagged with its own `schema_version`, older 1.3.0 snapshots still validate under `PatientRecord`).

### 140. Operator confirmed #139's open assumption: `prom` = premature/prolonged rupture of membranes
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-18 / Phase 2 (reopened), operator confirmed on request
- **Requirement ID(s):** PRD-006 (dataset documentation accuracy)
- **What happened:** DEVIATIONS #139 flagged `prom`'s meaning as an inferred, unconfirmed reading rather than an operator-stated fact. Operator (Timothy Tuti, the attesting PI on `DATASET.md`) confirmed it means premature/prolonged rupture of membranes.
- **What changed:** `DATASET.md`'s variable table and `field_mapping.yaml`'s comment updated from "assumed ... operator should confirm" to "confirmed by operator, 2026-09-18." No code or mapping logic changed — `field_mapping.yaml`'s `members: [maternal_infection, prom]` already used the literal source string, unaffected by the label's confirmation.
- **Reversible?:** yes — documentation-only.

### 141. Fixed `_list_and_flag_features`'s active-only aggregate for `medications`/`interventions` — an assessed-and-not-given item is now a real feature, not indistinguishable from never-assessed
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-18 / Phase 2 (reopened), operator asked to close the gap DEVIATIONS #138 flagged but left unfixed
- **Requirement ID(s):** PRD-052 (SCOPE-2.2), PRD-084, ARCH-034
- **What was found:** `app.records.access._list_and_flag_features` produced `medications.active` / `interventions.active` as a list of *only* `active=True` names. An `active=False` medication/intervention (assessed, confirmed not given — the same clinical meaning as `examination_findings`' `present=False`) never appeared in `extract_features`'s output at all, so `get_patient_fields("medications.gentamicin", ...)`-style per-concept lookups (and any future criteria matching against a medication/intervention, mirroring how `examination_findings.<name>` already works) could not tell "assessed and not given" from "never assessed" — the exact distinction the operator raised for `examination_findings` in DEVIATIONS #138, but left open here.
- **Fix:** kept the existing `medications.active`/`interventions.active` aggregates unchanged (nothing in the codebase reads them, but removing a working feature un-asked wasn't this task), and added a per-name feature for every medication/intervention regardless of `active` value — `features[f"medications.{name}"] = medication.get("active")` / same for interventions — via a small shared helper (`_name_value_features`, driven by `_NAME_VALUE_FEATURE_FIELDS`) that also now backs `examination_findings`/`maternal_risk_factors`'s per-name features, replacing the four near-identical inline loops (ruff `PLR0912` flagged the un-factored version at 14 branches; the helper brought it back under the limit and removed the duplication in the same move).
- **Verified**: new test `test_extract_features_distinguishes_assessed_negative_meds_and_interventions` (`tests/test_records_access.py`) — an assessed-negative medication/intervention is a real `False` feature; the aggregate `.active` lists are unchanged; a never-assessed medication has no key at all. Full backend suite (558 tests) passes; `ruff check`/`ruff format --check` clean.
- **Reversible?:** yes — additive features alongside unchanged aggregates; no schema, API contract, or policy-table change (the existing `WILDCARD_FIELD_PATH` clinician/reviewer policy already allows any field path, so no new `record_field_policy` rows are needed for the new feature keys to be requestable).

### 142. Correction: `medications`/`interventions`' `active` flag is not clinically equivalent to `examination_findings`/`maternal_risk_factors`'s `present` flag
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-18 / Phase 2 (reopened), operator correction
- **Requirement ID(s):** PRD-052 (SCOPE-2.2), documentation accuracy only — no behavior changed
- **What was wrong:** DEVIATIONS #138 and #141 described `Medication.active`/`Intervention.active`'s `False` value as carrying "the same clinical meaning" as `ExamFinding.present`/`present=False` (both framed as "assessed-negative"). Operator corrected this: `medications`/`interventions`' TRUE/FALSE records whether an **action was taken** (True = given, False = not given) — a clinical staff decision/event — not an **assessment of a sign in the patient** (True/False = sign present/absent on exam), which is what `examination_findings`/`maternal_risk_factors` actually record. These are different kinds of clinical facts that happen to share the same TRUE/FALSE/NA/absent data shape in the source export and the same `{name, value}` list-item shape in `record.py` — the shape is shared, the clinical meaning is not.
- **What changed:** no code or behavior change (the `True`→given/`False`→not-given passthrough implemented in DEVIATIONS #141 was already correct — only its written characterization was wrong). Updated: `app/records/access.py`'s `_NAME_VALUE_FEATURE_FIELDS` comment (now states the given/not-given vs present/absent distinction explicitly, per family), `tests/test_records_access.py`'s renamed `test_extract_features_distinguishes_not_given_meds_and_interventions` (was `..._assessed_negative_meds_and_interventions`) and its docstring/comments, and `TRACEABILITY.md`'s PRD-084 row.
- **Still holds, not affected by this correction:** the not-assessed / never-documented case (`NA` or a fully absent EAV row → no list item at all → no feature key) means the same structural thing for both families — "there is no record either way" — only the *documented* True/False value's clinical meaning differs by family. Nothing about DEVIATIONS #138/#139/#141's actual code fixes was wrong; only their prose equated two different clinical concepts that happen to share a data shape.
- **Reversible?:** yes — documentation/comment correction only; full backend suite (558 tests) still passes unchanged.

### 143. Flagged new scope (not implemented): clinical-concept extraction from patient-record field values
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-18 / raised during Phase 2 (reopened), not assigned to a phase — **nothing in this entry has been built**
- **Requirement ID(s):** none yet — no `PRD-###`/`ARCH-###` exists for this; needs one assigned at a checkpoint before any implementation starts (CLAUDE.md §2: "if a requirement... conflicts with something you discover during implementation... stop and flag it," applied here pre-emptively to a capability that was never in `prompt.txt`/PRD.md at all)
- **What was requested:** derive labeled clinical concepts from recorded field values during ingestion/retrieval — e.g. `vitals.resp_rate_bpm > 60` (for the patient's age) → a "tachypnoea" concept tag — to make patient-record content more directly usable by retrieval/synthesis, rather than raw numeric values only.
- **Why this is logged as new scope rather than started:** it was never in `prompt.txt`'s phase plan or `PRD.md`. Per CLAUDE.md §2, a scope change discovered mid-build gets flagged, not quietly absorbed into whatever phase happens to be open.
- **CDS-boundary analysis (must be resolved *before* implementation, not after):** substantively distinguishable from the two hard-excluded capabilities in `CDS-FUTURE.md` — it labels a recorded value against a fixed, deterministic threshold ("this vital, at this value, matches a named clinical concept"), which is closer to structured inference (already in scope — see CLAUDE.md §1) than to `SCOPE-2.3` (autonomous next-step recommendation) or `SCOPE-2.4` (guideline adjustment for local constraints). It must stay a **label**, never a suggested action, or it crosses into `SCOPE-2.3`. This reading needs operator/checkpoint sign-off, not just this session's judgment, before code is written.
- **Non-negotiable constraints if/when this is built** (from CLAUDE.md §3 rule 5 / §4 "Determinism where it matters," and this session's own findings):
  1. **Deterministic thresholds, not an LLM call** — a curated, never-inferred rules table, the same pattern as `app/records/criteria.py`'s `_CRITERIA_FIELD_SYNONYMS` (never a model guessing what counts as "tachypnoea").
  2. **Must not fire on a `None`/not-assessed value.** This directly depends on the tri-state (assessed-positive / assessed-negative-or-not-given / never-documented) fixes from DEVIATIONS #138/#139/#141/#142 in this same session — a concept rule must only evaluate a field that is actually present in `field_index`/`extract_features`, never treat a missing vital as if it were a normal/negative one.
  3. Needs its own test coverage per requirement (CLAUDE.md §5) before being considered started, including a case proving it does NOT fire on missing data.
- **Where it would live (not yet built):** most likely `backend/app/records/` alongside `criteria.py` (e.g. a new `concepts.py`), consumed wherever `app.records.access.extract_features` output currently feeds an agent — exact seam not yet chosen, needs review against the live `patient_record_agent`/`missing_info_agent` call sites first.
- **Related, also not started:** an eval ablation (single-stage vs. multi-step orchestration, where "multi-step" runs this concept-extraction step before embedding/synthesis) was separately proposed as a `PHASE7-PROPOSAL.md` candidate, analogous to `PHASE6-PROPOSAL.md`. It depends on this feature existing first.
- **Reversible?:** n/a — no code changed; this entry exists so the scope question is on record before anyone (including a future session) starts building it without a checkpoint decision.

### 144. Drafted requirement IDs for #143's proposed concept-extraction feature: PRD-057, SCOPE-2.6, ARCH-041
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-18 / drafted at operator's request — **still not approved, not implemented**
- **Requirement ID(s):** PRD-057, SCOPE-2.6, ARCH-041 (all newly drafted by this entry)
- **What was done:** added `PRD-057` to `PRD.md` §4.6 (Capability scope), `SCOPE-2.6` to `ARCHITECTURE.md` §9.2's in-scope-Scope-2 table, and a new `ARCHITECTURE.md` §9.4 documenting `ARCH-041` as the *proposed* labeling mechanism (curated rule table, `app.records.criteria`-style, home `app/records/concepts.py`) — all three explicitly marked `PROPOSED — not approved, not implemented` in their own text, not written as settled/in-scope requirements. `TRACEABILITY.md` gained matching rows for all three, status `not started — proposed only, pending checkpoint approval`.
- **Why marked proposed rather than approved:** this is ID/text drafting only, done because the operator asked for it — it is not itself a checkpoint approval to build the feature. CLAUDE.md §2's phase-checkpoint protocol still requires an explicit go-ahead before any code under `app/records/concepts.py` (or any agent/prompt wiring for it) is written.
- **Numbering chosen:** `PRD-057` was the next free ID in the §4.6 capability-scope block (`PRD-050`–`056` used, `057`–`059` open before `PRD-060` starts §4.7 — no renumbering needed). `SCOPE-2.6` follows `SCOPE-2.1`/`2.2` (the in-scope Scope-2 half) rather than being sequenced after `SCOPE-2.5`, since it's a proposed *addition to* the in-scope table, not part of the out-of-scope/exception block `2.3`–`2.5` occupy. `ARCH-041` is the next free `ARCH-###` after `ARCH-040` (Phase 6's fusion-sweep harness).
- **Reversible?:** yes — documentation only; no code, test, or behavior change. Full backend suite unaffected (not re-run for this entry — no `.py` file touched).

### 145. Drafted `PHASE7-PROPOSAL.md` for the single-stage vs. multi-step orchestration ablation — redesigned to *not* depend on `PRD-057`/`SCOPE-2.6`/`ARCH-041`, correcting #143
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-18 / drafted at operator's request — **still not approved, not implemented**
- **Requirement ID(s):** `PRD-111` (proposed, see the document); no dependency on `PRD-057`/`SCOPE-2.6`/`ARCH-041`
- **Correction to #143:** that entry said the ablation "depends on this feature existing first," referring to the fixed-threshold concept-labeling capability. On drafting the actual proposal, redesigned "multi-step pre-processing" to mean **re-using the already-implemented, already-grounded `app.records.criteria.evaluate_criteria`** (matched retrieved criteria as query-expansion clauses) rather than a new independent labeling vocabulary — avoiding the need for this session (or any coding session) to invent clinically-authoritative thresholds with no guideline citation behind them. This makes the ablation buildable from already-approved primitives, with **no dependency on `PRD-057`/`SCOPE-2.6`/`ARCH-041`** at all. See `PHASE7-PROPOSAL.md` §2 for the full reasoning.
- **What was done:** wrote `PHASE7-PROPOSAL.md` (proposal document only, no code) at the repo root, matching `PHASE6-PROPOSAL.md`'s section structure and rigor: motivation, the design refinement above, judgment calls to log at implementation start, deliverables, experiment design/metrics, CLAUDE.md §3 compliance, out-of-scope boundary, testing plan, docs to update on approval, and a checkpoint stop-line.
- **Also noted in the proposal:** `PHASE6-PROPOSAL.md` §7 had provisionally earmarked "Phase 7" for a *different*, untriggered proposal (production fusion-weight adoption, conditional on Phase 6's sweep beating RRF — it didn't, DEVIATIONS #123). That name was unclaimed and is used here for an unrelated topic; flagged explicitly in the new document so it isn't mistaken for the fusion-weight follow-up.
- **Reversible?:** yes — one new proposal document; no code, test, schema, or requirement-table change (the `PRD-111` ID is proposed text inside the document itself, not yet added to `PRD.md`/`TRACEABILITY.md` — that happens on approval, per the document's own §9).

### 146. Revised the concept-extraction proposal: an operator-authored, attestation-gated vocabulary is a legitimate design (not something a coding session should invent) — added as a third arm to `PHASE7-PROPOSAL.md`, and `PRD-057`/`SCOPE-2.6`/`ARCH-041` revised to specify it
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-19 / drafted at operator's request — **still not approved, not implemented**
- **Requirement ID(s):** `PRD-057`, `SCOPE-2.6`, `ARCH-041`, `PRD-111` (all still proposed only)
- **What prompted this:** operator pushed back on #143/#145's framing that a fixed clinical-threshold vocabulary was necessarily out — correctly pointing out that the actual objection was "this coding session shouldn't invent thresholds," not "a fixed vocabulary is unsafe." An **operator-authored, attestation-gated** vocabulary resolves that objection completely, the same governance pattern `DATASET.md`'s attestation and `field_mapping.yaml` already use for other clinically-authored artifacts.
- **What changed:**
  1. `ARCHITECTURE.md` §9.4 (`ARCH-041`) rewritten: proposes `data/clinical_concepts.yaml` (schema-level, alongside `data/record_schema.json`) with a required `authored_by`/`authored_date` header and a required non-empty `source` per concept; a loader modeled on `app.ingestion.records.guard_batch`'s attestation gate fails closed on any placeholder entry. Made explicit that a label from this vocabulary is **internal retrieval-signal only** — it can perturb query augmentation but can never appear in or support answer text, because it carries no citation the way a `criteria`-chunk-derived fact does; the existing §8.3 grounding/quote check still gates every answer segment regardless.
  2. **Also fixed a real section-ordering bug from DEVIATIONS #144**: that entry's edit had inserted `### 9.4` *before* `### 9.3` in `ARCHITECTURE.md` (numerically out of order in the actual file, not just visually) — corrected by moving §9.4 to after §9.3's content, immediately before the `---`/§10 divider, in the same change as the content rewrite above.
  3. `PRD.md` (`PRD-057`) and the `TRACEABILITY.md` rows for `PRD-057`/`SCOPE-2.6`/`ARCH-041` updated to match.
  4. `PHASE7-PROPOSAL.md` restructured from two arms (single-stage, criteria-reuse) to three: Arm A (single-stage, unchanged), Arm B (criteria-reuse, unchanged from the first draft), and new **Arm C (operator-vocabulary)** — one retrieval call, query augmented *before* retrieval from `data/clinical_concepts.yaml` matches, no dependency on Arm B's first pass having found the right guideline. This is a real scope increase from the first draft (DEVIATIONS #145 said Phase 7 "does not need or build" the `SCOPE-2.6` capability) — Arm C now implements a bounded slice of it. The document's "Relationship to PRD-057/..." section was rewritten to say so plainly rather than silently letting the scope creep past what #145 told the operator.
  5. Added the hard blocking dependency explicitly: Arm C's *real* report cannot be produced until the operator authors real `data/clinical_concepts.yaml` content; the test suite instead uses an obviously non-clinical fixture vocabulary (e.g. `value: 999999`, `source: "unit-test fixture, not a real clinical value"`) to exercise the loader/evaluator mechanics only.
- **Reversible?:** yes — documentation only (`ARCHITECTURE.md`, `PRD.md`, `TRACEABILITY.md`, `PHASE7-PROPOSAL.md`); no code, test, or schema change. Nothing in `data/clinical_concepts.yaml` or `app/records/concepts.py` exists yet.

### 147. Added operator-curated `synonyms` to the proposed concept vocabulary (raised before Checkpoint 7 approval)
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-19 / clarification requested before approving Checkpoint 7 — **still not approved, not implemented**
- **Requirement ID(s):** `PRD-057`, `SCOPE-2.6`, `ARCH-041` (still proposed only)
- **What was asked:** whether the vocabulary design (DEVIATIONS #146) could allow spelling variants (e.g. "tachypnoea"/"tachypnea") and synonymous phrases (e.g. "fast breathing") for the same concept, so query augmentation isn't defeated by wording differences between the vocabulary and a guideline's actual text.
- **Decision & rationale:** added an optional `synonyms:` list per concept in `data/clinical_concepts.yaml`, operator-curated in the **same** attested file and gate as the threshold — not split into "assistant-decided spelling rules" vs. "operator-decided phrase synonyms" (a lighter-weight auto-normalization option was offered; operator chose the single-file design for one audit trail). The expansion-term builder appends a matched concept's synonyms parenthetically after its primary name, reusing `app.retrieval.hybrid._expand_abbreviations`'s exact technique (original term preserved for exact/BM25 matching, synonyms added for recall) — a third instance of the codebase's existing curated-vocabulary pattern (alongside `_ABBREVIATIONS` and `_CRITERIA_FIELD_SYNONYMS`), not a new mechanism.
- **What changed:** `ARCHITECTURE.md` §9.4 (`ARCH-041`), `PRD.md` (`PRD-057`), and `PHASE7-PROPOSAL.md` (§2 Arm C, §3 judgment calls, §4 schema example, §9 testing plan) updated to include `synonyms` in the schema and mechanism description. Also fixed a stray "§9.4.1" cross-reference in `ARCHITECTURE.md` §9.4 left over from DEVIATIONS #146 (no such subsection exists) — repointed to `PHASE7-PROPOSAL.md` §2.
- **Reversible?:** yes — documentation only; no code, test, or schema change.

### 148. Fixed a real ID collision: `ARCH-041` was already assigned (and implemented) for the embedding-ablation harness — the concept-extraction proposal's `ARCH-041` renumbered to `ARCH-042`
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-19 / caught while starting Phase 7 implementation, before any code was written — **corrects #144/#146/#147, which all used the wrong ARCH id**
- **Requirement ID(s):** `ARCH-042` (was incorrectly `ARCH-041` in #144/#146/#147); no change to the real `ARCH-041` (embedding ablation, `PHASE2-EMBEDDING-ABLATION-PROPOSAL.md`, approved 2026-09-17)
- **What happened:** DEVIATIONS #144 assigned `ARCH-041` to the (still-proposed) concept-extraction mechanism by checking `ARCHITECTURE.md`/`TRACEABILITY.md`'s highest `ARCH-###` reference (`ARCH-040`, Phase 6) and picking the next number. That check missed a real assignment: `ARCH-041` was already used — in shipped code, not just docs — for the embedding-ablation harness approved the day before Phase 6 closed (`app/eval/model_ablation/*.py`, `app/retrieval/vectorstore.py`'s `scroll_all`, `scripts/run_model_ablation.py`, `tests/test_model_ablation.py`, `app/config.py` all cite it in their docstrings). `TRACEABILITY.md`'s `ARCH-###` table never got a row for it (the same kind of gap `PRD-109`/`PRD-110` had in `PRD.md` — a real, pre-existing documentation debt, not something introduced this session), which is exactly why grepping the docs alone gave a wrong answer. **Cause:** verified the next-free ID against `ARCHITECTURE.md`/`TRACEABILITY.md` text only, not against the actual codebase — the memory-verification discipline of grepping real code before treating a recalled/derived fact as current wasn't applied to a *newly derived* fact (an ID assignment), only to old ones.
- **What changed:** every occurrence of `ARCH-041` written for the concept-extraction proposal (`ARCHITECTURE.md` §9.2's `SCOPE-2.6` row and §9.4's heading/body, `TRACEABILITY.md`'s `PRD-057`/`SCOPE-2.6` rows, `PHASE7-PROPOSAL.md` throughout) renumbered to `ARCH-042`. Added the missing `TRACEABILITY.md` row for the real `ARCH-041` (embedding ablation) in the same change, closing the documentation gap that caused this. `PRD.md` needed no fix — `PRD-057`'s text never cited `ARCH-041`.
- **Verified**: `grep -rhoE "ARCH-[0-9]+" --include=*.py --include=*.md` across the whole repo (code and docs together, not docs alone) confirms `ARCH-041` now resolves only to the embedding-ablation harness and `ARCH-042` only to the concept-extraction proposal, with no other gaps in between.
- **Reversible?:** yes — documentation only; no code, test, or schema change. No implementation had started under the wrong number.

### 149. Implemented Phase 7 (`PHASE7-PROPOSAL.md`, Checkpoint 7 approved 2026-09-19): `app/records/concepts.py` (ARCH-042, bounded to eval-only use) + `app/eval/orchestration_ablation/` (PRD-111)
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-19 / Phase 7, Checkpoint 7 approved same session
- **Requirement ID(s):** PRD-057, SCOPE-2.6, ARCH-042, PRD-111
- **What was built** (all as specified in `PHASE7-PROPOSAL.md`, no scope changes beyond the judgment calls below): `app/records/concepts.py` (`load_vocabulary` — attestation gate, fails closed on any `TODO_CONFIRM`; `evaluate_concepts` — never fires on a feature absent from `extract_features`'s output; `build_expansion_term` — primary term + parenthetical synonyms, same technique as `app.retrieval.hybrid._expand_abbreviations`); `data/clinical_concepts.yaml` (the template, unattested — no real threshold decided by this session); `app/eval/orchestration_ablation/{augment.py,ablation.py,report.py}`; `backend/scripts/run_orchestration_ablation.py`; `make orchestration-ablation-report`; `tests/test_concepts.py` (16 tests) + `tests/test_orchestration_ablation.py` (17 tests). Full suite: 591 passed (up from 558); `ruff check`/`ruff format --check` clean; `mypy app` clean at the pre-existing baseline (new yaml/pandas/seaborn missing-stub findings match the already-accepted pattern for `eav.py`/`retrieval_tuning/report.py`/`model_ablation/report.py`).
- **Judgment calls made while implementing (logged now, per CLAUDE.md §6):**
  1. **Source-record resolution: reverse-hash against the synthetic JSON only, never a `records.patient`/`records.patient_record` read.** Investigating how to resolve `EvalQuestion.source_record_id` back to a patient record surfaced that it's populated by **two different pipelines** with different meanings: `app.eval.tasks.generate_questions` sets it to `uuid5(record["record_id"])` for a bundled `synthetic-generator-v1` record (no DB row behind that UUID at all), while `app.eval.auto_seed` sets it to a real `records.patient.id` for an attested **de-identified** record. `PHASE7-PROPOSAL.md` §7 committed to "no PHI... no de-identified data touched" — so `app.eval.orchestration_ablation.augment.resolve_source_record` resolves **only** via the first path (`load_synthetic_record_index` reverse-hashes every bundled synthetic record and looks up by UUID; a de-identified-sourced question's ID simply never matches, and is deliberately never looked up any other way). This makes the compliance claim mechanically true rather than a stated intention: this module contains no `records.patient`/`decrypt_record` call at all.
  2. **Retrieval-call accounting resolved in favor of §2's mechanism over §6's stated count.** The proposal's Arm B algorithm (§2 step 4) says a second retrieval call happens "if any matched; otherwise Arm B == Arm A" (implying 1 call when nothing matched), while §6's cost-accounting line flatly states "Arm B = 2." Implemented per the more specific §2 mechanism: `retrieval_calls` is 1 when Arm B doesn't fire, 2 when it does; `SliceReport.avg_retrieval_calls` reports the mean, which is the more accurate and more informative number anyway (real cost, not worst case).
  3. **Eval-set filter implemented exactly as §3 specified**: `well_supported` requires non-empty `gold_relevant_chunks` (primary); `missing_info_expected` does not (secondary/exploratory) — both via one `fetch_ablation_questions(require_gold=...)` parameter, not two separate functions.
  4. **`fetch_ablation_questions`/`run_full_ablation` (plain SQLAlchemy queries) are not offline-tested**, same precedent as `app.eval.retrieval_tuning.sweep.fetch_calibration_questions` (that module's own test file: "needs a real Postgres for a meaningful test"). Everything downstream of a fetched `AblationQuestion` (`run_question`, `aggregate`, both `augment.py` builders, `concepts.py` end to end) is tested offline against `:memory:` Qdrant + stub embedding/reranker backends and hand-built `AblationQuestion`/`QuestionResult` fixtures.
  5. **3-arm chart palette**: used the dataviz skill's categorical slots 1–3 (blue/orange/aqua) directly — the only three slots that clear every CVD/normal-vision gate under *all-pairs* comparison (not just adjacent pairs), per `references/palette.md`; no sequential-ramp reasoning needed since arm identity, unlike Phase 6's `alpha`/`k`, is not an ordered dial.
- **Not run for real**: this sandbox has no running Qdrant/Postgres/seeded `eval.eval_question` table, so `make orchestration-ablation-report` has not produced an actual report — same gap Phase 6's first entry (#121) and the embedding ablation's first entry left open before a later live run. Additionally, Arm C's real-data report specifically cannot be produced until the operator attests `data/clinical_concepts.yaml` (still every field `TODO_CONFIRM`).
- **Verified**: offline only, as described above (591 tests, ruff, mypy). `TRACEABILITY.md` (new `PRD-111` row; `PRD-057`/`SCOPE-2.6`/`ARCH-042` rows updated from "proposed" to "implemented, bounded to eval-only use"), `PRD.md`, `ARCHITECTURE.md` §9.2/§9.4, `README.md` (Status table + Phase 7 narrative + Data section + repo layout), and `Makefile` (`orchestration-ablation-report` target) all updated in the same change.
- **Reversible?:** yes — additive modules and docs only; no production code path (`app/agents/graph.py`, `hybrid.py`, any route) touched; `app/records/concepts.py` has no caller outside `app.eval.orchestration_ablation`.

### 150. Real run (operator: "attest data/clinical_concepts.yaml with real thresholds and run it live") found a real path bug, then a real null result with an identified root cause: this deployment's eval-question pool has zero synthetic-sourced questions, so Arms B/C never fire
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-19 / Phase 7, immediately following Checkpoint 7's approval and implementation (DEVIATIONS #149), against the live `dev`-profile stack (persisted `pg-data`/`qdrant-data`/`hf-model-cache` volumes from prior sessions — real 314-point 3-document guideline corpus, 101 existing `eval.eval_question` rows, 67 `well_supported` with `gold_relevant_chunks`, 2 `missing_info_expected`)
- **Requirement ID(s):** PRD-111, PRD-057, SCOPE-2.6, ARCH-042
- **Operator attestation**: `data/clinical_concepts.yaml` filled in directly by the operator (`authored_by: "Timothy Tuti"`, `authored_date: "19 Sep 2026"`, `tachypnoea`: `vitals.resp_rate_bpm > 59`, `source: "operator-curated"`, `synonyms: ["fast breathing", "high respiratory rate", "tachypnea", "rapid breathing"]`) — not written or suggested by this session, per the governance design in #146/#149.
- **Infra steps to run it live**: added a `docker-compose.yml` bind mount for `data/clinical_concepts.yaml` on the `api` service (it lives directly under `data/`, not under the already-mounted `data/sample_guidelines`/`data/patient_records`, so it wasn't visible in the container without one — same rationale as those two existing mounts, an operator-editable attested artifact that shouldn't need a rebuild to update). `docker compose up -d postgres redis qdrant`, `docker compose build api` (picked up all of Phase 7's new backend code — cached pip layer, fast rebuild), `docker compose up -d api`, confirmed `alembic current` already at head, confirmed 314 Qdrant points and the real question counts above, `pip install -e ".[retrieval-tuning]"` inside the container, then `python -m scripts.run_orchestration_ablation`.
- **Real bug #1, found on first live run**: `scripts/run_orchestration_ablation.py`'s `_DEFAULT_CONCEPTS_PATH` (`Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent.parent / "data/clinical_concepts.yaml"`) computed `/data/clinical_concepts.yaml` (one `.parent` too many) instead of `/app/data/clinical_concepts.yaml` — Arm C was silently skipped as "not attested" with a misleading `FileNotFoundError` message, even though the file *was* attested. Root cause: the script's `__file__`-relative math assumed the host's `backend/scripts/` → `backend/` → repo-root → `data/` layout, but the real invocation environment (confirmed by inspecting the running container: `pwd` → `/app`, `ls /app` → `app/ data/ scripts/ ...`) has `scripts/` and `data/` as *siblings* directly under `/app`, one level shallower. `_DEFAULT_OUT_DIR` uses the same `__file__`-relative style but happened to compute correctly for the container layout by coincidence (the package itself is nested as `/app/app/...`, matching `run_model_ablation.py`'s pre-existing identical pattern) — not touched. **Fix**: both defaults changed to plain relative paths resolved against the process's actual `cwd` at runtime (`data/clinical_concepts.yaml`, `app/eval/orchestration_ablation/reports`), overridable via `CLINICAL_CONCEPTS_PATH`/`ORCHESTRATION_ABLATION_OUT_DIR` env vars — same pattern `scripts/prepare_sample_guidelines.py`'s `SAMPLE_GUIDELINES_DIR` already uses, robust to being invoked from either the container's `/app` or (with an explicit override) the host. Verified via `ruff`/full offline suite (591 passed, unchanged) before rebuilding the image and re-running.
- **Real result, after the fix (all three arms ran, including Arm C for real)**: `well_supported` (n=67): recall@5 = 0.0149 (1/67), recall@8 = recall@24 = 0.6866 (46/67) — **identical across all three arms**; MRR@8 = 0.1057 — **identical across all three arms**. `missing_info_expected` (n=2): all zero (too small/no gold to be meaningful). **`fired_rate` = 0.0 for both `criteria_reuse` and `vocabulary`, on every single question in both slices** — the three arms produced byte-identical rankings throughout; the 3-panel report (`orchestration_ablation_report.png`) shows this directly (flat, zero bottom panel; identical bar heights across all three colors in the top two panels).
- **Root cause, confirmed diagnostically, not assumed**: both augmentation builders (`build_arm_b_query`/`build_arm_c_query`) short-circuit to "no fire" whenever `resolve_source_record` returns `None` (§7's no-PHI design: it only ever reverse-hashes against the bundled synthetic `patients.json`, never touching `records.patient`/`records.patient_record`). Queried directly: **0 of all 101 `eval.eval_question` rows in this deployment** (not just the 67 scored ones) resolve to any of the **6** bundled synthetic records — every real question here was generated by `app.eval.auto_seed` (de-identified-record-sourced, per that module's own docstring: "already-ingested de-identified patient records (never synthetic ones)"), never by `app.eval.tasks.generate_questions` (the synthetic-sourced path this ablation's compliance boundary requires). This is **not a bug in the ablation** — it is the correct, intended consequence of the no-PHI design decided in DEVIATIONS #149 — but it means **this specific real deployment currently has zero statistical power for Arms B/C**: the null result reflects "no synthetic-sourced question existed to augment," not "augmentation doesn't help."
- **Also confirmed**: the operator's real LLM gateway (`LLM_GATEWAY_URL=https://host.docker.internal:8443`) *is* reachable from this environment (`GET /health` → `200 {"status":"healthy","model":"qwen3.6:35b",...}`) — so generating synthetic-sourced questions for a real comparison is technically possible, but `app.eval.tasks`'s question generator has a separately-documented, pre-existing gap (never populates `gold_relevant_chunks`, PRD-070's `TRACEABILITY.md` note, surfaced in DEVIATIONS #122) that would need addressing first to get any of those questions into this ablation's primary (`require_gold=True`) slice — a real scope decision, not something to build unasked as a side effect of "run it live."
- **Verified**: live, as described above. Report PNG copied out and visually confirmed (flat fired-rate panel, identical bar triplets). Offline suite unaffected (591 passed) after the path fix.
- **Docker/infra state left behind**: `postgres`/`redis`/`qdrant`/`api` containers running (dev profile, reusing pre-existing named volumes — no data created or destroyed); `docker-compose.yml` gained the one new bind mount described above; `hospital_rag_platform-api` image rebuilt (new backend code baked in). Not torn down, matching the DEVIATIONS #135 precedent of leaving a live stack up at the operator's implicit continuation of the session.
- **Reversible?:** yes — the script-path fix and compose mount are both plain, additive fixes; the live run created no persistent data (read-only against existing Postgres/Qdrant state) beyond the report PNG file inside the container's ephemeral filesystem.

### 151. Fixed `app.eval.tasks._run_generate_questions` to populate `gold_relevant_chunks` (closes the gap DEVIATIONS #122 identified and deliberately left open, PRD-070)
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-19 / fixing the specific gap #150's live run surfaced, at operator's request
- **Requirement ID(s):** PRD-070, PRD-111 (unblocks a real synthetic-sourced comparison for Phase 7)
- **What was wrong:** `app.eval.auto_seed._generate_one_scenario` invokes the real pipeline per generated scenario and writes back its own grounding-verified citations as `gold_relevant_chunks` (DEVIATIONS #122). `app.eval.tasks._run_generate_questions` — the harness's separate fixed-testset generator, and the *only* path that produces synthetic-sourced `EvalQuestion` rows — never invoked the pipeline at generation time at all, so its rows' `gold_relevant_chunks` stayed permanently `None`. #122 diagnosed this precisely and deliberately left it unfixed as "a structurally different, larger change." #150's live run showed the concrete cost: 0 of 101 real questions in the deployment resolved to a synthetic record, because none had ever been generated through this path with usable gold data — Phase 7's Arms B/C had nothing to compare against.
- **Fix**: added `_invoke_pipeline`/`_INVOKE_PIPELINE_FN` (same lazy-import-`invoke_graph` pattern as `app.eval.harness`/`app.eval.auto_seed`'s own indirection points, `patient_id=None` — these are scope-1-framed narratives with no live patient context, same reasoning `auto_seed._invoke_pipeline`'s docstring already gives) and `_capture_gold_relevant_chunks`, called once per generated question right after `generate_question` succeeds, before `session.add(row)`. Unlike `auto_seed`, **no `eval.Result` row is persisted** — this generator's fixed-testset questions are scored fresh by `app.eval.harness` on every actual run, so the extra pipeline call here exists solely to capture the gold set at creation time, not to cache an answer. A transient gateway error (`httpx.HTTPError`/`LLMGatewayError`) during that capture is logged and swallowed, not raised: the narrative already generated successfully via `generate_question` and is still a valid question without a gold set, exactly the same "one bad step doesn't fail the whole batch" discipline the surrounding loop already applies to `QuestionGenerationFailed`.
- **Verified**: offline — 5 new tests in `tests/test_eval_tasks.py` (gold set populated + deduped + sorted from real citations; unset on escalation; unset when citations are empty; the question is still created and gold stays unset on a transient gateway error during capture, not on the whole batch; the capture helper itself swallows `LLMGatewayError`), mirroring `test_eval_auto_seed.py`'s established fixture pattern (`_INVOKE_PIPELINE_FN` monkeypatched to canned pipeline-output dicts). Full backend suite: 596 passed (up from 591); `ruff check`/`ruff format --check` clean; `mypy app/eval/tasks.py` clean at the pre-existing baseline.
- **Reversible?:** yes — additive change to one existing function; no schema change (`gold_relevant_chunks` already existed); `add_to_fixed_testset` behavior and every other field on the created row are unchanged.

### 152. Generated real synthetic-sourced questions live and re-ran Phase 7 with them; still 0% fired rate for both arms, now root-caused all the way down (not a resolution problem this time — a real corpus gap for Arm B, a real threshold boundary for Arm C)
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-19 / Phase 7, immediately following DEVIATIONS #151's fix, at operator's request ("fix app.eval.tasks..., then generate synthetic questions")
- **Requirement ID(s):** PRD-070, PRD-111
- **What was done, live**: rebuilt the `api` image with #151's fix, then ran `_run_generate_questions(count=18, composition="60,20,20", seed=7, add_to_fixed_testset=True)` directly against the live stack (real LLM gateway narrative generation + a real full-pipeline invocation per question for gold-chunk capture). 9 of 18 attempts produced a valid question (the other 9 rejected by the existing narrative validator, `QuestionGenerationFailed` — expected, not investigated further); of those 9, all 9 correctly resolve to one of the 6 bundled synthetic records (confirming #151's fix and #150's resolver both work end to end), and 3 `well_supported` questions got real, non-empty `gold_relevant_chunks` from real grounding-verified citations (the other 6 escalated — `grounding_failure`/`safety_filter` — so correctly got no gold set, per #151's design).
- **Re-ran the Phase 7 ablation** against the now-larger pool (69 `well_supported` + 4 `missing_info_expected`, including the new synthetic-sourced rows). **Still 0% fired rate for both `criteria_reuse` and `vocabulary`, on every question** — recall/MRR numbers unchanged from #150's first run (recall@8/@24 = 0.6957, recall@5 ≈ 0.015, MRR@8 = 0.1075, identical across all three arms).
- **This time root-caused past the resolver, to two independent, real, corpus/data-specific causes — confirmed directly, not inferred:**
  1. **Arm B: the real ingested guideline corpus has zero `criteria`-type chunks.** Queried the live Qdrant collection directly: `count(chunk_type="criteria")` = **0 of 314 points**. Not a per-question retrieval-ranking miss — there is structurally nothing of that type for Arm B (or, by extension, `stage_classifier_agent`/`missing_info_agent`, SCOPE-2.1/2.2) to ever match against in this specific real corpus. Confirmed across all 3 synthetic `well_supported`+gold questions' Pass-1 retrieval: every single top-8 item was `chunk_type=prose`.
  2. **Arm C: the two synthetic records with an assessed `vitals.resp_rate_bpm` both fall at or below the operator's threshold.** Values were 59.0 and 40.0; the attested rule is `> 59` (strict). 59.0 does **not** satisfy strict `>59` — it's exactly at the boundary, not above it.
