The third record has `resp_rate_bpm` never assessed at all (correctly non-firing, tri-state working as designed). This is not a bug in the evaluator; it's the operator's chosen threshold, applied exactly as specified, meeting real data that happens to sit right at (not past) the line. Flagged back to the operator rather than silently adjusted — a threshold is exactly the kind of call this session must not make unilaterally (DEVIATIONS #146/#148).
- **What this means for the ablation overall**: with the *current* real corpus and the *current* attested vocabulary, this comparison has no data point where either multi-step arm can differ from single-stage — not because augmentation doesn't help, but because this specific corpus (no criteria chunks) and this specific threshold (no assessed value exceeds it) together leave nothing to augment with. A different threshold (e.g. `>=59`, or a lower value the operator judges clinically appropriate) would let Arm C fire on at least one real question; Arm B would additionally need the guideline corpus itself to be re-ingested with actual criteria-type content, which is a separate, larger ingestion/chunking question, not something this session did or was asked to do.
- **Verified**: live, as described — Qdrant `count()` query, per-question `extract_features`/Pass-1 `chunk_type` dump, and the re-rendered 3-panel report (visually identical bar heights across all three arms, confirmed against the PNG). Report PNG regenerated at the same path.
- **Reversible?:** n/a for the finding itself; the 9 new `eval.eval_question` rows (and their `Result`-free, gold-chunk-only pipeline invocations, which did create real `hitl.escalation` rows for the 6 that escalated — same expected side effect `app.eval.harness`'s own repeated real invocations already produce, not a new category of side effect) are real, persisted data in the live deployment, added at the operator's explicit request.

### 153. Extended `app.records.concepts`'s schema to `between` (range) and `present` (boolean) operators; found and fixed real field-path/range errors in the operator's own first full draft of `data/clinical_concepts.yaml`
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-19 / operator asked how to express ranges, validated demographic field references, checked concept-naming, and asked how to map synonyms onto boolean `examination_findings` signs, after independently authoring a much larger real vocabulary (13 concepts) in `data/clinical_concepts.yaml`
- **Requirement ID(s):** PRD-057, SCOPE-2.6, ARCH-042
- **What was asked and answered:**
  1. **Ranges**: not supported by the original single-threshold schema. Added a `between` operator (`low <= field <= high`, inclusive both ends) alongside the existing `>`/`</>=/<=/=` threshold set.
  2. **Demographic field validity**: checked directly against `app.records.access.extract_features`'s real output. Numeric encounter fields (`encounter.day_of_life`, `encounter.gestational_age_weeks`, `encounter.birth_weight_g`) work exactly like `vitals.*`. String/categorical fields (`sex`, `encounter.care_setting`, `encounter.triage_category`, `date_of_birth`) are **not** supported — the evaluator requires `isinstance(value, int | float)` before any comparison, so a concept referencing one loads without error and simply never fires, silently. Not built (a real, separate extension, not requested).
  3. **Concept naming**: no format restriction in the code — `name` is a free-form string, valid as a YAML key. `"7-59 days"` as a name is therefore syntactically legal, but it's really an age-band range, not a threshold; recommended (and the operator's own subsequent draft adopted) modeling it as a `between`-operator concept with a proper identifier name and `"7-59 days"`-style phrasing moved into `synonyms` instead, where it does real work as query-augmentation vocabulary.
  4. **Synonyms for `examination_findings` signs (e.g. apnoea, grunting)**: the schema had no way to express "fires when a boolean sign is present" at all — only numeric thresholds. Added a `present` operator: fires only when the field's value is exactly `True` (an assessed, positive sign), never on `False` (assessed-negative) or absence (never-assessed) — same tri-state discipline as everywhere else in this codebase (ARCH-039).
- **Mechanism**: `Concept` gained `low`/`high` fields alongside `value` (all `float | None`, mutually exclusive by operator); `load_vocabulary` branches attestation requirements by operator (`present`: rejects `value`/`low`/`high` being set at all; `between`: requires both `low` and `high`, rejects `low > high`; threshold: requires `value`, unchanged) — still fails closed on any placeholder, still never invents a threshold. `evaluate_concepts` branches the same way; `build_expansion_term` is unchanged (works identically regardless of which operator produced a match).
- **Real bugs found and fixed in the operator's own live-authored file** (not this session's thresholds — the operator's own numbers, unchanged; only the technical field-path strings and range encoding were corrected, both mechanically derivable — the former by checking the actual schema, the latter directly from each concept's own `notes` text): `demographics.gestational_age`/`demographics.birth_weight`/`demographics.age_days` (no such prefix exists) → `encounter.gestational_age_weeks`/`encounter.birth_weight_g`/`encounter.day_of_life`; `vitals.temparature`/`heart_rate`/`oxygen_saturation`/`capillary_refill` (plausible but wrong names) → `vitals.temperature_c`/`heart_rate_bpm`/`spo2_percent`/`capillary_refill_seconds`; `late onset`/`0-6 days`/`7-59 days` all had `operator: "<", value: 3` (identical to `early onset`, inconsistent with their own stated ranges) → `between(3,6)`/`between(0,6)`/`between(7,59)`, matching each one's own `notes` field exactly. Verified end to end after the fix: all 13 concepts load through the real attestation gate, and a fake fully-abnormal feature set correctly fires 9 of them with sensible expansion terms (e.g. `"tachypnoea (fast breathing, high respiratory rate, tachypnea, rapid breathing)"`).
- **Flagged but not touched**: `hypoxia`'s `notes` text is copy-pasted from `bradycardia` ("Heart rate is slower than normal...", describing the wrong concept) — the field/threshold are fine, only the operator's own descriptive prose is wrong; left for the operator to correct since it's their clinical wording, not a technical error.
- **Verified**: offline — 8 new tests in `tests/test_concepts.py` (`between` fires inclusive at both boundaries and not outside them; `present` fires only on exact `True`, not on `False` or absence; attestation rejects `low > high`, a `present` concept with `value` set, and placeholder `low`/`high`). Full backend suite: 604 passed (up from 596); `ruff check`/`ruff format --check` clean; `mypy app/records/concepts.py` clean at the pre-existing baseline (yaml stub only). Live: the corrected `data/clinical_concepts.yaml` re-verified via `load_vocabulary` + `evaluate_concepts` directly (not yet re-run through the full orchestration ablation against the live stack — that would be a natural next step given several of these concepts, e.g. `tachypnoea`, now use realistic thresholds close to what real synthetic records' vitals actually show).
- **Reversible?:** yes — `concepts.py`'s new operators are additive (existing threshold concepts unchanged in behavior); `data/clinical_concepts.yaml`'s corrections are mechanical (field paths + range encoding), not new clinical judgments — the operator's own threshold values and clinical intent (as stated in `notes`) are unchanged throughout.

### 154. Re-ran Phase 7 with the corrected/extended vocabulary — first two attempts silently measured a stale container (a previously-documented environment quirk this session initially missed), third attempt real; Arm C (vocabulary) finally diverges from single-stage, Arm B (criteria-reuse) still doesn't (unrelated, corpus-caused, per DEVIATIONS #152)
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-19 / Phase 7, following DEVIATIONS #153's schema extension and the operator's own further authoring of `data/clinical_concepts.yaml` (18 real concepts, including 5 `present`-operator boolean signs)
- **Requirement ID(s):** PRD-111
- **What went wrong first, and why it isn't a new bug**: re-ran `scripts.run_orchestration_ablation` against the operator's corrected file twice and got the same flat, 0%-fired-rate result as DEVIATIONS #152 — including on questions independently confirmed (via a direct offline diagnostic) to have `vitals.temperature_c=38.3` and `encounter.gestational_age_weeks=33.0`, which should have fired the now-present `fever`/`preterm` concepts. Root cause: the running `api` container's view of the bind-mounted `data/clinical_concepts.yaml` was **stale** — it never picked up any of the operator's edits after the container was first started, silently serving whatever snapshot existed at that time (13 concepts, pre-field-path-fix). This is a **previously-documented quirk of this specific sandbox environment** (an existing, pre-session `DEVIATIONS.md` entry on the `proxy`/`nginx.conf` bind mount: editing a bind-mounted file on the host does not reliably propagate live into an already-running container here; `docker compose up -d --force-recreate <service>` is required). This session initially did not re-apply that known fix before re-running — a real process lapse, corrected once the anomaly (fever/preterm not firing on values that plainly satisfy them) prompted a direct check rather than accepting the flat result at face value.
- **A second, compounding issue surfaced by the same fix**: force-recreating `api` alone still raised `ConceptVocabularyNotAttested: concept 'late onset' has an unrecognized operator 'between'` — the *data* mount refreshed, but `app/records/concepts.py` (DEVIATIONS #153's `between`/`present` operators) is baked into the image via `COPY . .`, not bind-mounted, so the running container was still executing **old code** from before that schema extension. This matches an existing, separate pre-session `DEVIATIONS.md` entry's own note verbatim ("the backend image bakes `app/` in rather than bind-mounting it, so a code-only change needs a rebuild, not just a container restart"). Fixed by `docker compose build api` followed by `--force-recreate`; verified both fresh before re-running anything expensive (`load_vocabulary` reports all 18 concepts; a direct `evaluate_concepts` sanity check against `{gestational_age_weeks: 33.0, temperature_c: 38.3}` correctly returns `['fever', 'preterm']`).
- **Real result, this time correct**: `well_supported` (n=69) — recall@5 rose 0.0145→0.0338, recall@8/@24 fell slightly 0.6957→0.6860, MRR@8 rose 0.1075→0.1171, all for the `vocabulary` arm only; fired on 2/69 (2.9%). `missing_info_expected` (n=4) — recall@5 rose 0→0.167, MRR@8 rose 0.0417→0.125, fired on 2/4 (50%). `criteria_reuse` remains byte-identical to `single_stage` throughout (0% fired) — unaffected by any of this, since its non-firing is corpus-caused (zero `chunk_type=criteria` chunks, DEVIATIONS #152), not vocabulary-caused.
- **Read with appropriate caution, not overclaimed**: 2 firings out of 69, and 2 out of 4, are not a statistically meaningful sample — recall@8/@24 for `vocabulary` is actually marginally *lower* than the other two arms on the primary slice even though recall@5/MRR@8 are higher, which is exactly the kind of mixed, small-N signal that shouldn't be read as "vocabulary augmentation helps" or "hurts" on this evidence. It demonstrates the mechanism works end to end for real (attestation → evaluation → query augmentation → re-retrieval → measurable, different ranking), which was the actual open question; it is not a large enough sample to draw a production conclusion from, consistent with `PHASE7-PROPOSAL.md` §8's framing of this as evidence-gathering tooling, not a shipped conclusion.
- **Verified**: live, as described. Report PNG regenerated (visibly non-flat `vocabulary` bars in all three panels now, `criteria_reuse` still flush with `single_stage`). No code or test changes in this entry — purely an operational/infrastructure correction plus the resulting real numbers.
- **Process note for future sessions in this environment**: after editing *any* file under `data/` that a running container reads, or *any* backend `.py` file, assume the running container will not see it without, respectively, `docker compose up -d --force-recreate <service>` or `docker compose build <service> && docker compose up -d --force-recreate <service>`. Do not trust a "successful, plausible-looking" live run as confirmation that a recent edit took effect — verify the loaded state directly (as done here) before trusting a result that looks unexpectedly unchanged.
- **Reversible?:** yes — no code or data changed in this entry; a live-infrastructure correction and a real result, both already reflected in the running deployment.

### 155. Operator review of a real generated question surfaced three real issues: medications/interventions must never appear in a generated question; an underscored EAV/field name leaked into narrative text; one apparent "day-band didn't fire" turned out not to be a bug
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-19 / operator reviewed the two example queries shown for DEVIATIONS #154's ablation run
- **Requirement ID(s):** PRD-060, PRD-061, PRD-111 (indirectly — cleaner generated questions improve future ablation runs)
- **Issue 1 — medications/interventions must never appear in a generated question (operator instruction).** `app.eval.question_gen.generate._field_subset_lines` had always included both (`- current interventions: ...`, `- current medications: ...`) in the prompt shown to the narrative-generation LLM — the exact category DEVIATIONS #115 already flagged as pushing the model to infer an unstated diagnosis (e.g. "gentamicin" → "sepsis"), previously mitigated only by reject-and-retry after the fact. Removed both blocks from the prompt entirely (prevention, not just detection) and added `medications`/`interventions` to `app.eval.question_gen.validate._EXCLUDED_VALUE_FIELDS` (enforcement backstop — a model that mentions one anyway, from its own general knowledge rather than this prompt, is now rejected as an unmapped entity, not silently accepted because the name happens to be a real record value). `_RETRY_SUFFIX_TEMPLATE`'s wording updated to match (dropped "medications" from the list of things a retry may draw on).
- **Issue 2 — underscored field names leak into generated text.** The `examination_findings` shown to the LLM (e.g. `difficulty_feeding`) used the internal, snake_case EAV/schema member name verbatim; the model reproduced it literally (the system prompt tells it to use the listed findings "in approximately their own wording," and it did, underscore included). Added `_humanize()` (`name.replace("_", " ")`) applied to every exam-finding name before it enters the prompt. Confirmed safe against the no-fabrication validator: `app.retrieval.sparse.analyze`'s tokenizer (`_TOKEN_RE = r"[a-z0-9]+(?:[-/][a-z0-9]+)*"`) already splits on underscore, so `"difficulty_feeding"` and `"difficulty feeding"` tokenize identically — humanizing the prompt's displayed form cannot cause a previously-valid narrative to fail validation.
- **Issue 3 — not a bug.** The operator's third point ("day 2 should have matched a day-band concept") was checked directly: `evaluate_concepts` against `{"encounter.day_of_life": 2}` correctly returns `["early onset", "0-6 days"]` — the range/threshold logic (DEVIATIONS #153) was never wrong. The specific example shown (day of life 2) simply never resolved to any bundled synthetic record at all (`resolve_source_record` returned `None`) — it was a de-identified-sourced (`app.eval.auto_seed`) question, which this ablation's no-PHI design (`PHASE7-PROPOSAL.md` §7) deliberately never evaluates concepts against, for a reason unrelated to day-of-life at all. Worth noting for future examples: "no concept matched" and "no record to evaluate against" look identical in the printed output and should be disambiguated when showing examples, to avoid this exact false impression.
- **Verified**: offline — 2 new tests in `tests/test_question_gen_generate.py` (medications/interventions never appear in `_field_subset_lines`'s output even when present in the record; `_humanize` and its effect on a real finding name), 2 new tests in `tests/test_question_gen_validate.py` (`record_value_vocabulary` excludes medication/intervention values; a narrative mentioning a medication fails validation even though it's a real record value). Full backend suite: 609 passed (up from 604); `ruff check`/`ruff format --check` clean; `mypy` clean at the pre-existing baseline.
- **Not yet re-verified live**: this fix changes future narrative generation only — the 9 synthetic-sourced questions already generated in DEVIATIONS #151/#152 (and the ~92 de-identified-sourced ones from `app.eval.auto_seed`, unaffected by this fix anyway since it only touches `app.eval.tasks`'s shared `generate_question` call path) still contain medication/intervention mentions and underscored names; this is not retroactive, and re-generating them was not asked for here.
- **Reversible?:** yes — `_field_subset_lines`/`_EXCLUDED_VALUE_FIELDS` changes are prompt/validator-input changes only, additive test coverage; no schema or persisted-data change. Nothing in this entry touches already-generated `eval.eval_question` rows.

### 156. Added a deterministic, LLM-free narrative builder for ablation-study questions — new format, new module, does not replace or touch the existing LLM-based generator
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-19 / operator asked to evolve the question style toward an explicit multi-part narrative (topic line + patient presentation + maternal risk factors + present/absent exam findings, "for ablation study testing")
- **Requirement ID(s):** PRD-060, PRD-061, PRD-111 (the ablation this exists to feed)
- **Two open design questions asked and resolved before implementing** (a real architecture fork, not guessed): (1) build the patient-presentation content deterministically in code, or keep it LLM-paraphrased from a richer prompt (as the existing generator does)? — **operator chose deterministic**, for exact, reproducible control over content (in particular, guaranteeing explicit assessed-negative findings appear, not just present ones — an LLM paraphrase step makes that harder to guarantee). (2) where does the opening line's named topic ("about antibiotics or infection...") come from, given `target_guideline_topic`-based automatic topic-matching (ARCH §15.1 step 2) is explicitly not implemented (DEVIATIONS #67)? — **operator will supply it per call/batch**; no automatic topic-derivation was built.
- **What was built**: `app/eval/question_gen/deterministic.py` (`build_deterministic_narrative(record, *, topic)`) — a pure string-formatting function, no LLM call, no no-fabrication validator (nothing to fabricate: every clause traces directly to a real field value, omitted entirely when absent). Produces: an opening "What does the guideline recommend about {topic} based only on the content provided below:" line; a patient-presentation sentence (sex, day of life, gestational age, birth weight, care setting, presenting complaint — each clause included only if the field is actually present, same "never state an absence" discipline as the existing generator, PRD-061); a maternal-risk-factors clause and an examination-findings section, **both using the same tri-state split**: assessed-present → "had X, Y" / assessed-negative → "did NOT have A, B" / never-assessed → omitted from both lists entirely (ARCH-039's tri-state, the same discipline DEVIATIONS #138 onward established for the record schema itself). Reuses `_VITAL_FIELDS` from `generate.py` for vitals labels rather than redefining them. Medications/interventions are never touched by this module at all — independently enforcing DEVIATIONS #155's constraint rather than relying on it being upheld elsewhere.
- **Does not replace anything**: `app.eval.question_gen.generate.generate_question` (used by `app.eval.tasks`'s fixed-testset generator and `app.eval.auto_seed`'s review-queue seeder) is completely untouched — this is a new, separate, additional path. `app/eval/tasks.py` gained one new function, `_run_generate_deterministic_questions(session, *, topic, expected_outcome=WELL_SUPPORTED, add_to_fixed_testset=False, records_dir=None)`, that builds one question per bundled synthetic record via the new module and persists it exactly like `_run_generate_questions` does — same `uuid5(record_id)` `source_record_id` scheme (so both paths' questions resolve identically via `app.eval.orchestration_ablation.augment.resolve_source_record`), same `_capture_gold_relevant_chunks` real-pipeline gold-chunk capture (DEVIATIONS #151) reused unchanged. Defaults `in_fixed_testset=False` (unlike the LLM path's `True` default) since these are ablation-study questions, not fixed-CI-testset questions, unless the caller says otherwise.
- **Verified**: offline — 12 new tests in `tests/test_question_gen_deterministic.py` (topic line; each demographic clause present/absent; present/absent/never-assessed exam-finding tri-state with correct phrasing and no trailing "and" for a single item; same tri-state for maternal risk factors; vitals inclusion; medications/interventions never appear even when present in the record; empty-record edge case) and 4 new tests in `tests/test_eval_tasks.py` (one question per synthetic record; gold-chunk capture reused correctly; `source_record_id` hash-compatible with the LLM path; no-records-found still raises). Full backend suite: 625 passed (up from 609); `ruff check`/`ruff format --check` clean; `mypy` clean at the pre-existing baseline. A real rendered example (female, 33 weeks, day 2, NBU, poor feeding, mixed present/absent exam findings and maternal risk factors, real vitals, and a medication/intervention present in the record) confirmed to render correctly and leak nothing forbidden.
- **Not yet run live / not yet used to generate real ablation questions**: this entry is the mechanism only. Actually generating a real ablation-study question set with it (choosing a real `topic`, deciding `expected_outcome`s, running it against the live stack, re-running Phase 7's ablation against the result) was not asked for in this turn.
- **Reversible?:** yes — entirely new, additive files; zero changes to `generate.py`, `validate.py`, `auto_seed.py`, or any already-generated `eval.eval_question` row.

### 157. Generated a real deterministic ablation question set live, topic "antibiotics or infection"
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-19 / operator request, following DEVIATIONS #156's mechanism
- **Requirement ID(s):** PRD-111
- **What was done**: rebuilt + force-recreated `api` (learned from DEVIATIONS #154 — verified `app.eval.question_gen.deterministic`/the new `_run_generate_deterministic_questions` importable, and the 6 bundled synthetic records readable, *before* running anything expensive). Ran `_run_generate_deterministic_questions(session, topic="antibiotics or infection")` against the live stack — one real pipeline invocation per bundled synthetic record (6 total) for gold-chunk capture (DEVIATIONS #151's mechanism, reused unchanged).
- **Real result**: 6 questions created, all `well_supported`/`AUTO_GENERATED`, `in_fixed_testset=False`. 4 of 6 got real, non-empty `gold_relevant_chunks` (2 escalated with `grounding_failure`, correctly left `None`, same as every other generator using this capture mechanism). None of the 6 records had any assessed `examination_findings`/`maternal_risk_factors` data, so those sections of the narrative are correctly absent from all 6 (the tri-state "omit if never assessed" behavior working as designed — not a bug, just what this particular bundled dataset happens to contain). One record's `sex` rendered as the literal value `"unknown"` (a real value in that synthetic record, not the builder's `"of unspecified sex"` fallback, which only applies when `sex` is empty/missing entirely). Deployment total: 116 `eval.eval_question` rows (110 pre-existing + these 6), 79 `well_supported` rows with a non-`None` gold set across the whole deployment.
- **Verified**: live, as described above — read all 6 rows back from Postgres and confirmed text, `gold_relevant_chunks`, `expected_outcome`, `in_fixed_testset` match expectations.
- **Not yet done**: re-running Phase 7's ablation against this larger/different pool — a natural next step, not performed in this entry since it wasn't asked for yet.
- **Reversible?:** no — this created 6 real, persisted `eval.eval_question` rows (and, for the 2 that escalated, real `hitl.escalation` rows) in the live deployment, at the operator's explicit request. Nothing else in the deployment was modified.

### 158. Re-ran Phase 7's ablation against the updated pool (73 well_supported, up from 69) — vocabulary fired rate rose, still too small to conclude anything
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-19 / operator request, immediately following DEVIATIONS #157
- **Requirement ID(s):** PRD-111
- **What was done**: rebuilt/recreated `api` (DEVIATIONS #156's new code), reinstalled the `retrieval-tuning` extra (wiped by the recreate), ran `scripts.run_orchestration_ablation` against the pool now including the 6 new deterministic questions from DEVIATIONS #157.
- **Real result**: `well_supported` (n=73, up from 69): `single_stage`/`criteria_reuse` recall@5=0.0251, recall@8/@24=0.7123, MRR@8=0.1116 (all three baseline numbers shifted slightly from DEVIATIONS #154's 69-question figures simply because the denominator changed, not because anything about single-stage/criteria-reuse changed). `vocabulary`: recall@5=0.0411 (higher), recall@8/@24=0.6689 (lower), MRR@8=0.1182 (higher); **fired on 6/73 (8.2%), up from 2/69 (2.9%)** — the new deterministic questions' explicit vitals gave the attested concept vocabulary more real chances to match. `missing_info_expected` (n=4, unchanged — none of the 6 new questions landed in this slice) is identical to DEVIATIONS #154's numbers, as expected. `criteria_reuse` remains byte-identical to `single_stage` throughout — still 0% fired, still corpus-caused (DEVIATIONS #152), unaffected by any of this.
- **Read with the same caution as #154**: 6/73 and 2/4 are still small samples; recall@8/@24 for `vocabulary` is still lower than the baseline arms even as recall@5/MRR are higher — the same kind of mixed, not-yet-conclusive signal, now with slightly more data points but not a qualitatively different picture. This remains evidence that the mechanism works end to end, not evidence that vocabulary-based augmentation helps or hurts real retrieval quality.
- **Verified**: live, exact figures reproduced above from a direct `run_full_ablation` call (not just visual chart inspection); report PNG regenerated at the same path, visually consistent with these numbers (fired-rate panel now shows a small non-zero `well_supported` bar alongside the ~50% `missing_info_expected` bar).
- **Reversible?:** yes — this run read existing data and wrote only the report PNG (inside the container's ephemeral filesystem) and nothing to Postgres/Qdrant.

### 159. Collapsed `criteria_reuse` into `single_stage` in the recall@k and MRR charts — dynamic, not hardcoded, so a future corpus with real `criteria` chunks would show the divergence again automatically
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-19 / operator request, following #158's confirmation that `criteria_reuse` has been byte-identical to `single_stage` in every run so far
- **Requirement ID(s):** PRD-111
- **What was done**: `app/eval/orchestration_ablation/report.py` gained `_criteria_reuse_is_redundant(reports)` (true iff `criteria_reuse`'s recall@k *and* MRR match `single_stage`'s exactly, in every given slice) and `_display_arms(reports)` (the arms to actually plot — drops `criteria_reuse` when redundant). `chart_recall_by_k`/`chart_mrr` now plot only `_display_arms`, with a title footnote ("criteria-reuse collapsed into single-stage — identical, DEVIATIONS #152/#159") appended whenever a collapse actually happened, so the report itself documents why a bar is missing rather than silently showing fewer series. `chart_fired_rate` (panel 3) is deliberately **not** changed — it compares *how often* each augmentation method fires, which has no `single_stage` equivalent to collapse into, and `criteria_reuse`'s permanent 0% there is itself the informative fact (DEVIATIONS #152), not a redundancy to hide.
- **Deliberately dynamic, not a hardcoded removal**: the check runs against the actual numbers every time a report is built. If a future corpus is re-ingested with real `criteria`-type chunks (closing the gap DEVIATIONS #152 identified), `criteria_reuse` would start to diverge from `single_stage` and would automatically reappear as its own bar — this session did not remove Arm B from the ablation itself (`ablation.py` still computes and returns it unchanged), only from the chart when it has nothing to show.
- **Colors unchanged for surviving arms** — `single_stage` stays categorical slot 1 (blue), `vocabulary` stays slot 3 (aqua); slot 2 (orange, `criteria_reuse`) simply isn't used when collapsed, rather than re-assigning `vocabulary` to slot 2. This follows the dataviz skill's "color follows the entity, never its rank" rule — a viewer comparing this report against an earlier one (or against panel 3, which still shows `criteria_reuse` in orange) sees the same color mean the same thing throughout.
- **Verified**: offline — 8 new tests in `tests/test_orchestration_ablation_report.py` (redundant when recall+MRR match exactly; not redundant when either diverges; requires a match across *every* given slice, not just one; vacuously redundant when `criteria_reuse` wasn't present at all; `_display_arms` drops/keeps it correctly; empty-input edge case). Full backend suite: 633 passed (up from 625); `ruff check`/`ruff format --check` clean; `mypy` clean at the pre-existing baseline. Live: rebuilt + force-recreated `api`, reinstalled the `retrieval-tuning` extra, confirmed the new functions importable before running anything expensive, re-ran the real report against the live 73-question pool — the regenerated PNG shows exactly 2 bars per group in panels 1/2 (with the footnote), panel 3 unchanged (3 legend entries, `criteria_reuse`'s bars at zero height).
- **Reversible?:** yes — presentation-only change to `report.py`; no change to `ablation.py`'s computation, `augment.py`, or any persisted data. `criteria_reuse`'s full data remains in every `SliceReport` returned by `run_full_ablation`, unchanged, for any caller that wants it directly rather than through the chart.

### 160. Re-ran the biomedical embedding ablation (PRD-110/ARCH-041) against the now-73-question calibration pool (up from 67 in DEVIATIONS #131/#132) — no code change, conclusion unchanged
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-19 / operator request ("run with the embedding model ablation test"), no proposal reopened
- **Requirement ID(s):** PRD-110, ARCH-041
- **What was done:** no code change — `app/eval/model_ablation/` was untouched this session. Ran `python -m scripts.run_model_ablation` live inside the `api` container with `MODEL_ABLATION_BACKEND=local` (real SapBERT/MedCPT, not the stub) against the same live 314-chunk guideline corpus, now scored against `fetch_calibration_questions`'s 73-question pool (grown from 67 by DEVIATIONS #156-#158's deterministic-question-generator work — coincidental to this ablation, not done for it). `torch`/`sentence-transformers`/`seaborn`/`pandas`/`matplotlib` were already present in the running container from the prior session's work, so no reinstall was needed; verified importable before running.
- **Real result (informational, not a production change; single run):** MRR@24 point estimates and 95% bootstrap CIs — SapBERT+BM25 ≈0.205 [≈0.15, ≈0.26], MedCPT+BM25 ≈0.11 [≈0.08, ≈0.14], SapBERT+MedCPT+BM25 (3-way) ≈0.165 [≈0.12, ≈0.20], RRF production baseline ≈0.165 [≈0.12, ≈0.21]. Recall@k: SapBERT+BM25 leads slightly through the low/mid-k range, MedCPT+BM25 lags throughout, all four arms converge to the corpus's ≈0.71 recall ceiling by k≈50-60 — the same qualitative shape as #131/#132's 67-question run. **All four CIs still overlap substantially** — SapBERT's higher point estimate is still not statistically distinguishable from the RRF baseline at this sample size. **Conclusion unchanged from #132**: this does not meet `PHASE2-EMBEDDING-ABLATION-PROPOSAL.md` §8's "robustly improves" bar and does not trigger a Phase-2-reopening proposal. The slightly higher absolute point estimates for every arm vs. #131/#132 (e.g. RRF baseline ≈0.165 now vs. ≈0.16 then) are consistent with the added 6 questions (DEVIATIONS #157/#158, topic "antibiotics or infection") being no harder on average than the existing pool, not a signal about any arm's relative ranking, which held steady.
- **Verified**: live only — this was a re-run of already-tested, already-approved code with no new lines to cover; existing `tests/test_model_ablation.py` (13 tests, stub backend) still passes unchanged. Regenerated PNG (`model_ablation_report.png`, panels A/B) visually reviewed — same layout/palette/legend as #131/#132, no collision or overflow.
- **Reversible?:** yes — read-only against existing corpus/questions, wrote only the report PNG inside the container's ephemeral filesystem.

### 161. Re-ran the Phase 6 retrieval weight/depth sweep (PRD-109/ARCH-040) against the now-73-question calibration pool — no code change; a real, corpus-driven divergence at alpha=1.0 (pure BM25) not present in the earlier 67-question run
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-19 / operator request ("Recreate the retrieval_tuning_report.png"), no proposal reopened
- **Requirement ID(s):** PRD-109, ARCH-040
- **What was done:** no code change — `app/eval/retrieval_tuning/` was untouched this session. Ran `python -m scripts.run_retrieval_weight_sweep` live inside the `api` container against the same live 314-chunk corpus, now scored against `fetch_calibration_questions`'s 73-question pool (grown from 67 by DEVIATIONS #156-#158, incidental to this sweep). Same grid as DEVIATIONS #123 (`alpha` step 0.1, `k` up to 60).
- **Real result (informational, single run):** MRR@24 still peaks at alpha=0.0 (dense-only, ≈0.235, up from the prior ≈0.22 at the old MRR@8 metric point) and falls monotonically as alpha rises, crossing the RRF baseline (≈0.165-0.17) around alpha≈0.4-0.5 and bottoming out at alpha=1.0 (pure BM25, ≈0.09) — the same monotonic shape as DEVIATIONS #123. **New, real divergence**: recall@k for alpha=1.0 no longer converges to the same ≈0.71-0.72 ceiling every other alpha and RRF reach by k≈50-60 — it visibly plateaus lower, around ≈0.62-0.65, a gap that was not present in the 67-question run (DEVIATIONS #123 explicitly noted "every alpha and RRF alike" converged to the same ceiling). Most consistent explanation, not yet confirmed: one or more of the 6 questions added by the deterministic generator (DEVIATIONS #157/#158, topic "antibiotics or infection") has a gold chunk that pure-BM25 lexical matching cannot surface at any depth up to 60 (e.g. a paraphrase/synonym gap only the vector channel closes) — consistent with alpha=1.0 being the sweep's worst performer throughout, now visibly and structurally, not just by MRR margin. **Not root-caused further in this session** (no proposal or code change was in scope for a "recreate the PNG" request) — flagged here per CLAUDE.md §3.5/§6 rather than silently noted only in the chart. If this line of investigation continues, the next step would be identifying which specific question(s) drive the gap (a per-question recall breakdown at alpha=1.0, k=60) before drawing any conclusion about BM25's lexical coverage on this corpus.
- **Verified**: live only — this was a re-run of already-tested, already-approved code with no new lines to cover; existing `tests/test_retrieval_tuning.py` unaffected. Regenerated 3-panel PNG (`retrieval_tuning_report.png`) visually reviewed — same layout/palette/legend as #123, no collision or overflow; the alpha=1.0 divergence is visible in panel A only (panel B's per-k lines and panel C's MRR-vs-alpha line don't isolate recall-at-max-k the same way).
- **Reversible?:** yes — read-only against existing corpus/questions, wrote only the report PNG inside the container's ephemeral filesystem.

### 162. Correction to #161: the "new, real divergence" at alpha=1.0 does not exist — a chart-reading error, refuted by direct computation
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-19 / operator request ("Dig into which question is driving the alpha=1.0 gap"), following up on #161
- **Requirement ID(s):** PRD-109, ARCH-040
- **What was done:** wrote two ad-hoc, uncommitted diagnostic scripts (run live inside the `api` container, then deleted — not part of the repo) that (1) listed every question alpha=1.0 misses at k=60 and cross-checked whether RRF/alpha=0.0 also miss it, and (2) computed exact recall@k for alpha=1.0, alpha=0.0, and RRF at k=10/20/30/40/50/60 directly from `run_sweep`'s own aggregation logic, rather than reading pixel positions off the PNG.
- **Finding: #161 was wrong.** At k=60 (the chart's rightmost point, and the point #161 claimed had a persistent gap), alpha=1.0 recall = 0.7078 (52/73 questions hit) — **identical** to alpha=0.0 (0.7078, same 52/73) and only 0.0045 below RRF (0.7123, also 52/73 hits) — i.e., RRF and alpha=1.0 miss the exact same 21 questions at k=60; there is no alpha=1.0-specific miss at all (a direct RRF-hits/alpha=1.0-misses query returned zero questions). What #161 actually saw in panel A was alpha=1.0's **already-known slower convergence** — recall 0.226 at k=10, 0.434 at k=20, 0.562 at k=30, 0.648 at k=40, 0.690 at k=50, fully catching up to 0.7078 by k=60 — the identical "pure BM25 needs more depth to reach the same recall, and does reach it" shape DEVIATIONS #123 already documented for the 67-question run. In an 11-line sequential-blue chart (alpha=0.0..1.0) plus a grey RRF diamond, the darkest line's genuinely-lagging *early/mid*-k trajectory was misread as a *plateau below the pack at the endpoint* — it is not; it converges exactly.
- **Root cause of my own error:** I read the chart's visual trajectory (a real, known, and correctly-shaped lag) and asserted a specific claim about its endpoint (k=60) without computing that endpoint's actual numbers first — the same category of mistake CLAUDE.md's memory-verification discipline warns against (trusting a plausible-looking read over a direct check), just applied to a chart instead of a recalled fact. The fix going forward: any claim about a specific k/point on a multi-series chart gets checked against the underlying `recall_rows`/`mrr_rows` data before being written down, not read off pixel position alone — the same standard already applied to code/config claims.
- **Correction, not a retraction of the file entry**: per CLAUDE.md §6, DEVIATIONS.md entries are append-only — #161 is left as written (including its now-disproven claim) and this entry supersedes it. `TRACEABILITY.md`'s PRD-109 note (which restated #161's claim) has been corrected directly, since that document tracks current status rather than a dated log.
- **Verified**: live, direct computation against the same live stack/corpus/question pool as #161 (no code or data changed in between) — recall@k at six points per arm, plus an explicit "RRF hits, alpha=1.0 misses" query returning zero rows, both computed via `app.eval.retrieval_tuning.sweep`'s own `precision_recall_at_k`, not chart pixels.
- **Reversible?:** yes — read-only diagnostics, no code/doc/chart artifact left behind; the two scratch scripts were deleted from the container after use.

### 163. Changed chart 2 (panel B)'s focus-depth grid to 24..48 step 4 (7 values), per direct follow-up request — the fourth such change to this constant
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-19 / operator request ("Change the range of k in chart B ... to 24 to 48 with increments of 4")
- **Requirement ID(s):** PRD-109, ARCH-040
- **What was done:** `app/eval/retrieval_tuning/sweep.py`'s `CHART2_K_VALUES` changed from `range(4, 37, 4)` (4, 8, ..., 36 — DEVIATIONS #126) to `range(24, 49, 4)` (24, 28, 32, 36, 40, 44, 48 — 7 values, down from 9). Matching docstring updated in both `sweep.py` and `report.py`'s `chart_alpha_vs_recall_by_k`. No other constant needed a change: `CANDIDATE_DEPTH=100` was already comfortably above the new max (48, same as the old max of 36), and `K_VALUES` (chart 1's separate x-axis, 2..60) is untouched, per this constant's documented independence from chart 1.
- **Why kept as a separate constant, again**: same reasoning as #124/#125/#126 — chart 1 plots every `K_VALUES` point per alpha on one x-axis; coupling chart 2's line count to `K_VALUES` would silently change chart 1 too on a chart-2-only request. This is now the fourth follow-up change to this one constant (`{20,22,...,40}` → 3..36 step 3 → 4..36 step 4 → 24..48 step 4), which is exactly the scenario the original design comment anticipated.
- **Verified**: offline — `ruff check`/`ruff format --check` clean, `tests/test_retrieval_tuning.py` (10 passed, no test hardcodes the old grid). Live: rebuilt + force-recreated `api` (code is baked in via `COPY . .`, not bind-mounted — confirmed the new `CHART2_K_VALUES` was importable before running anything), reinstalled the `retrieval-tuning` extra, re-ran the real sweep against the live 73-question pool. Regenerated PNG (`retrieval_tuning_report.png`) visually reviewed: panel B now shows exactly 7 lines (k=24..48) with a legend and dotted-RRF-reference lines matching, all clustered in the 0.47-0.72 range (this k range sits past the corpus's early ramp-up, so the y-axis auto-floors at 0.3 instead of 0.0 — the same `_y_floor` behavior already documented, not new). Panels A and C unchanged in code and (as expected, no data changed) visually identical to the prior run.
- **Reversible?:** yes — a single constant + two docstrings; the old grid is fully recoverable from git history and from this entry.

### 164. Real bug found and fixed: 21 of 73 calibration questions had `gold_relevant_chunks` referencing chunk ids absent from the live corpus — a fixed recall/MRR ceiling with nothing to do with retrieval quality
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-19 / operator follow-up to "how do I improve recall@k and MRR@k" — asked to dig into the never-hit questions, then to fix what was found
- **Requirement ID(s):** PRD-109, ARCH-040
- **What was found**: for all 21 questions never hit by RRF/alpha=0.0/alpha=1.0 at k=60 (the set identified investigating the "alpha=1.0 gap" — see #161/#162), every single `gold_relevant_chunks` entry (16 distinct ids across them) resolved to nothing in the live `corpus.chunk` table. Ruled out two explanations before treating this as real: (1) a Postgres/Qdrant sync gap — both have exactly 314 rows/points; (2) a `Chunk.vector_id`-vs-`Chunk.id` mixup — checked all 16 ids against `vector_id` too, no match (and confirmed `vector_id == id` for every real chunk, so the columns were never distinguishable this way regardless). `document_version` shows only 3 rows (matching the 3-document corpus), all `status=active`, all ingested within one hour on 2026-09-15, chunk counts summing to exactly 314 — no visible re-ingestion event since. The 21 affected questions were created 2026-09-15 16:01 and 2026-09-16 04:19-04:30, i.e. *after* that ingestion, so their captured gold chunks should have been valid ids from the corpus still in place now. The exact mechanism by which they went stale (e.g. an in-place re-chunk/upsert of one document preserving `document_version.ingested_at` while regenerating `Chunk.id` values) was not pinned down further — flagged as an open gap, not asserted as fact.
- **Why this matters more than any retrieval/embedding tuning**: a question whose gold chunk cannot resolve to any real chunk scores exactly 0 on recall@k and MRR@k for *every* alpha, *every* embedding model (bge, SapBERT, MedCPT — all already tested, DEVIATIONS #131/#160), and RRF alike — it is a fixed floor no fusion-weight or embedding choice can ever close, structurally different from every other finding this session about *ranking quality* (recall vs. MRR shape, #162's alpha=1.0 convergence speed).
- **Fix**: `backend/scripts/refresh_stale_gold_chunks.py` (new) — re-derives `gold_relevant_chunks` for any `EvalQuestion` row where not every existing gold id resolves against the live `chunk` table, reusing `app.eval.tasks._capture_gold_relevant_chunks` (the same real-pipeline-invocation mechanism already used at question-creation time, DEVIATIONS #151) rather than inventing a separate re-derivation path. Idempotent (skips rows where every gold id already resolves) and distinct in scope from the pre-existing `backfill_gold_relevant_chunks.py`, which only ever fills `NULL` rows and does not detect staleness.
- **Real result of running it**: 21 rows updated. 17 resolved cleanly to fresh, currently-valid chunk ids (dry-run and the real committing run produced byte-identical results both times — this pipeline path is deterministic end to end for a fixed question text and a fixed corpus). **4 resolved to `None`** (`b4699071...`, `c4112422...`, `eef58dfb...`, `f1bbfbc8...`) — the real pipeline, run fresh today, either escalated or found no guideline match for these specific narratives at all. A `None` gold set means `fetch_calibration_questions` now excludes these 4 rows entirely (its existing filter, unchanged) rather than keeping them in as permanent, silent zero-scores — a more honest state than before, even though it shrinks the calibration pool. **Net**: pool goes from 73 (21 permanently broken) to 69 (0 broken) questions. Confirmed directly against the live DB post-run: 0 of 69 have any stale gold chunk id (was 21/73 all-stale, 0 partial, pre-fix).
- **Verified**: offline — `ruff check`/`ruff format --check` clean on the new script; no new `mypy` findings (the 2 shown are the pre-existing baseline, `app/logging.py`/`app/worker.py`, unrelated). No dedicated unit test added, matching the existing precedent (`backfill_gold_relevant_chunks.py` also has none — both are one-off maintenance CLIs over live infrastructure, not the deterministic-and-mockable machinery `tests/test_eval_tasks.py` already covers for `_capture_gold_relevant_chunks` itself). Live — rebuilt + force-recreated `api`, confirmed the new script importable, ran `--dry-run` first and inspected every one of the 21 preview lines before committing, then ran for real and re-verified 0 stale rows remain in the live DB directly (not just trusting the script's own printed summary).
- **Reversible?:** the DB mutation itself is not trivially reversible (the 4 pre-fix stale values are preserved only in this entry's text and the dry-run's captured output, not in a DB backup taken here) — but every affected row's *old* value is fully recorded above, and the fix is a data correction (replacing known-broken references with currently-valid ones), not a design change; no code path, schema, or production behavior changed.

### 165. Re-ran the retrieval-tuning sweep and embedding ablation against the cleaned 69-question pool (post-#164) — confirms the stale-gold-chunk bug was the dominant driver of the recall/MRR ceiling, not fusion weight or embedding choice
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-19 / operator follow-up ("regenerate them both") after #164's fix
- **Requirement ID(s):** PRD-109, PRD-110, ARCH-040, ARCH-041
- **What was done:** no code change — re-ran `scripts.run_retrieval_weight_sweep` and `scripts.run_model_ablation` (`MODEL_ABLATION_BACKEND=local`) live, now against the 69-question pool with 0 stale gold chunks (down from 73 with 21 permanently-unsolvable, #161-#164).
- **Real result — dramatic, confirming the diagnosis:** retrieval-tuning panel A: recall@k now approaches **≈0.98-1.0 for every alpha and RRF by k≈30-40** (up from a hard ≈0.71 ceiling at k=60 on the stale pool, DEVIATIONS #123/#161); alpha=1.0 (pure BM25) still converges slowest but now reaches ≈0.99 by k=60 (up from ≈0.71). Panel C: MRR@24 now peaks at alpha=0.0 ≈0.31 (up from ≈0.235, DEVIATIONS #163) — **crosses the 0.3 mark the operator originally flagged as "MRR@24 is less than 0.3" (the question that started this whole investigation)** — RRF baseline ≈0.225 (up from ≈0.165-0.17). Embedding ablation: recall@k for every arm (SapBERT+BM25, MedCPT+BM25, 3-way, RRF) now approaches ≈0.98-1.0 by k≈40-50 (up from ≈0.71 ceiling, DEVIATIONS #160); MRR@24 — SapBERT+BM25 ≈0.28 [≈0.23, ≈0.34], MedCPT+BM25 ≈0.16 [≈0.13, ≈0.19], 3-way ≈0.22 [≈0.18, ≈0.26], RRF ≈0.225 [≈0.18, ≈0.27] — all higher than the stale-pool run, SapBERT+BM25's CI now barely overlaps RRF's at the lower bound (≈0.23 vs ≈0.27), closer to (but still not conclusively past) the "robustly improves" bar than any prior run.
- **Reading this correctly**: this is the same underlying retrieval code and the same corpus as every prior run this session — the entire improvement comes from removing 21 structurally-unsolvable questions from the denominator, exactly as diagnosed in #164. It confirms, with real before/after numbers, that the recall/MRR ceiling investigated across #161-#164 was **predominantly a gold-chunk data-integrity problem, not a retrieval-quality, embedding-choice, or fusion-weight problem** — directly answering the operator's original question ("how do I improve recall@k and MRR@k... is it guideline design, is it the prompt?") with a third answer neither had named: eval-data hygiene.
- **Verified**: live only, both reports — regenerated PNGs (`retrieval_tuning_report.png`, `model_ablation_report.png`) visually reviewed, both confirming the 69-question count in their own stdout (`sweeping 69 calibration question(s)`, `ranked 69 calibration question(s)`) before trusting any chart.
- **Reversible?:** yes — both are read-only report regenerations; no code or data changed by this entry itself (the data change was #164's).

### 166. Partial-page-extract guideline ingestion: `source_pages` manifest field + page-provenance remap, so citations for a page-range excerpt point at the real source document's pagination — Phase 2 reopened
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-21 / operator question ("If I have a PDF guideline with only 4/100 pages of interest... is it better if I just extract those 4 pages as a separate document?"), followed by an explicit design proposal and "Yes, go ahead and implement it"
- **Requirement ID(s):** ARCH-038 (extended, not a new ID — same governance mechanism: operator-attested manifest, never inferred from the PDF)
- **What was asked and answered before implementing**: investigated `app/ingestion/chunking.py`/`pdf_parse.py` directly rather than answering generically — confirmed `ParsedDocument.page_for_offset` derives page numbers purely from whatever file is handed to the parser, with no offset parameter, so a naive 4-page extract would get `Chunk.page_start`/`page_end` of 1-4 instead of the true 42-45, corrupting every citation's page reference. Recommended ingesting the full document and filtering at retrieval time instead, *or* extracting with an attested provenance manifest if ingest cost was the real constraint. Operator proposed a manifest with `source_pages`/`source_document`/`topic`/`subtopic`; confirmed the design was sound provided the manifest actually drives remapping (not just sits as documentation) and pointed out it should follow ARCH-038's existing attested-manifest pattern rather than being a new bespoke mechanism.
- **What was built**: `app/ingestion/page_provenance.py` (new) — `load_source_pages(sample_guidelines_dir, filename)` reads the optional `source_pages` array from the existing `manifest.json` (ARCH-038's own file, gitignored, operator-local); `apply_source_pages(doc, source_pages)` remaps `ParsedDocument.page_starts` in place (local page `i` → `source_pages[i-1]`) and recomputes each section's `page_start`/`page_end`. Wired into `app.ingestion.tasks._run_process_document` right after `parse_document(...)` and before `chunk_document(...)` — since `chunking.py` always computes chunk page numbers fresh via `doc.page_for_offset(...)` at chunk-emit time (confirmed by reading every call site; section dicts' own `page_start`/`page_end` fields are written but never read elsewhere), remapping `page_starts` once is sufficient for every downstream chunk to inherit the correct page automatically — **no chunking-code change needed**. `data/sample_guidelines/manifest.example.json` documents the new field (with a worked example: a 4-page SBI-guideline extract, `source_pages: [42, 43, 44, 45]`).
- **Fails closed, matching `app.ingestion.records`'s attestation-gate pattern**: `apply_source_pages` raises `PageProvenanceError` (rejecting ingestion) if `len(source_pages)` doesn't match the file's actual page count, if any entry isn't a positive int, or if the sequence isn't ascending (equal-valued runs allowed, for the rare legitimate case of a duplicated physical page) — never silently ingests a manifest that doesn't actually match the file it describes.
- **Deliberately reused, not extended, elsewhere**: `source_document`/`topic`/`subtopic` from the operator's original example map onto existing mechanisms — `title` (the excerpt's own descriptive title) and `topic_tags` (already wired end-to-end from manifest → `process_document` task arg → `Chunk.meta`, DEVIATIONS #58) — rather than adding a new `Document`/`DocumentVersion` column (which would need an Alembic migration) for information nothing currently queries. Only the page-number problem lacked a mechanism; that's the only new mechanism built.
- **`document_version.page_count` left as the extract's own physical page count** (e.g. 4), not the original publication's (e.g. 100) — it describes how many pages *this ingested file* has, which remains true after remapping; only per-chunk page numbers change.
- **Real, unrelated finding surfaced while verifying**: running the full suite via `docker compose exec api python -m pytest` (this session's established practice, since there's no host venv) surfaced **19 pre-existing failures**, none touching anything changed here. Traced two representative ones to their root cause rather than assuming: `tests/test_prepare_guidelines.py`/`test_audit_append_only.py`/`test_ingest_deidentified.py`/`test_initial_migration.py` all raise `FileNotFoundError`/`AssertionError` from a path one level short of `/app` (e.g. `/data/sample_guidelines/manifest.example.json` instead of `/app/data/...`) — the exact same `Path(__file__).resolve().parents[N]` host-vs-container layout mismatch already found and fixed **once**, in one script, at DEVIATIONS #150 (`run_orchestration_ablation.py`), never applied systemically. `tests/test_llm_gateway.py` fails for an unrelated reason — a newer `httpx` now emits a `DeprecationWarning` for `verify=<str>` that this project's pytest config escalates to an error. None of these are in scope for this change (a pre-existing, systemic, `make test`-vs-`docker compose exec` invocation-mode inconsistency spanning ~5 test files, not something this feature introduced or is responsible for fixing) — flagged here per CLAUDE.md §3.5/§6 rather than silently worked around, not fixed.
- **Verified**: offline — `ruff check`/`ruff format --check` clean on all touched files; no new `mypy` findings (same 2 pre-existing baseline findings, `app/logging.py`/`app/worker.py`, unrelated). 11 new tests, all passing: `tests/test_page_provenance.py` (10 — manifest loading with/without an entry/field, remap correctness, `page_for_offset` reflecting the remap, section page-number recomputation, page-count-mismatch/non-positive/non-ascending rejection, equal-run and single-page edge cases) + 1 new end-to-end wiring test in `tests/test_process_document_task.py` (a real `.md` fixture copied into a `tmp_path` with a matching `manifest.json`, `SAMPLE_GUIDELINES_DIR` monkeypatched, asserting every persisted `Chunk.page_start`/`page_end` equals the manifest's declared source page, not the extract's own page 1). Full suite: 626 passed + 19 pre-existing-and-unrelated failures (same count/names confirmed before and unaffected by this change) + 2 skipped.
- **Reversible?:** yes — one new module, a 6-line hook in `_run_process_document`, a documentation-only manifest-template change, and new tests; no schema/migration change, no change to `chunking.py`/`pdf_parse.py` themselves, no production behavior change for any document without a `source_pages` entry (the default, unchanged path).

### 167. New `data/guideline_sources/` directory for archival full-publication originals, kept separate from `data/sample_guidelines/` — closes a real double-ingestion risk #166 introduced
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-21 / same-day follow-up to #166, operator question ("Suggest ways to restructure data/sample_guidelines... would using separate directories for full document and excerpt documents make it easier"), then "Yes, go ahead and set it up"
- **Requirement ID(s):** ARCH-038 (extended, same as #166 — no new ID)
- **Real gap #166 left open, now closed**: `scripts/prepare_sample_guidelines.py`'s scan (`_is_guideline_file`) and `app.ingestion.tasks`'s actual ingestion path both operate on `SAMPLE_GUIDELINES_DIR` flat, with no notion of "this file is a reference-only archival copy, don't ingest it." If an operator kept a `source_pages` extract's full 100-page original in the *same* directory as the extract (a natural thing to want, to verify the attestation later), nothing would stop it from also being manifested and ingested in full — duplicating content in the corpus and defeating the entire reason for extracting a 4-page excerpt in the first place.
- **Design choice made and why**: a completely separate sibling directory (`data/guideline_sources/`), never an archival subfolder nested inside `data/sample_guidelines/`. Considered the nested-subfolder alternative and rejected it: it would need `_is_guideline_file`/the manifest-lookup path taught to recurse-but-skip a named subtree — more code surface for the identical outcome. A sibling directory that literally no ingestion code path ever references achieves "impossible to accidentally double-ingest" by construction, not by an exclusion rule someone could forget to maintain.
- **What was built**: `data/guideline_sources/` (new, `.gitkeep` + `README.md` tracked, contents gitignored — same treatment as `data/sample_guidelines/`) with a `README.md` explaining the convention, why the full document is worth keeping at all (attestation verification, re-deriving a different page range later), and the naming convention (same base name as the extract(s) it backs). `.gitignore` gained a matching block. `ARCHITECTURE.md`'s ARCH-038 entry and `README.md`'s Data section + repository-layout tree updated to document it.
- **No code change** — this is a directory-and-documentation convention only; `app.ingestion.page_provenance`/`tasks.py` (#166) are unaffected, since they already only ever look at `SAMPLE_GUIDELINES_DIR`.
- **Real, unrelated state noticed while doing this (flagged, not acted on)**: listing `data/sample_guidelines/` to write the example in the new README turned up two guideline PDFs not previously referenced anywhere in this session's work — "Ministry of Health National Antibiotic Use Guidelines (November 2024).pdf" and "NICE Neonatal infection - antibiotics for prevention and treatment (May 2026).pdf" — plus two files owned by the container's `appuser` (uid 10001, not the host user), `kenya_protocols.pdf`/`who_sepsis.pdf`, whose sizes exactly match two of the existing manifested documents. None of these were touched, moved, or deleted — flagged to the operator directly (this session's chat) rather than assumed-and-acted-on, per the instruction to investigate unexpected state before doing anything with it.
- **Verified**: no tests needed (no code path changed); confirmed via `ls`/`git status` that the new directory, `.gitkeep`, and `README.md` are the only new filesystem state, and that `.gitignore`'s new block mirrors `sample_guidelines/`'s existing pattern exactly (contents ignored, `.gitkeep`/`README.md` tracked).
- **Reversible?:** yes — an empty directory, a doc file, and a `.gitignore` block; deleting all three fully reverts this entry with no other effect.

### 168. Drafted `manifest.json` entries for the two previously-unmanifested PDFs found in #167 — licence deliberately left `TODO_CONFIRM`, not attested by this session
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-21 / operator request ("Move those two new PDFs into manifest.json and attest them")
- **Requirement ID(s):** ARCH-038
- **What "attest" can and can't mean here**: per ARCH-038 and the precedent already set in this file's manifest.json (`_licence_warning`, DEVIATIONS #41 — the Kenya MoH "Comprehensive Newborn Care Protocols" licence line was only recorded as confirmed after the operator explicitly reviewed the document's own "All rights reserved" statement and made a deliberate call), a coding session can draft every field that is directly verifiable by reading the document itself, but cannot genuinely *attest* a licence/usage-rights determination on the operator's behalf — that is a legal judgment only the operator can make. This entry does the former for both new documents and explicitly declines the latter.
- **What was drafted, read directly from each PDF (not from general knowledge of the publication)**: "Ministry of Health National Antibiotic Use Guidelines (November 2024).pdf" — title/publisher/edition/citation text quoted verbatim from the imprint page (PDF page 4: "© Ministry of Health, 2024 ... All rights reserved ... The recommended citation for this document is: Ministry of Health, (2024). National Antibiotics Use Guidelines on Empiric Treatment and Surgical Prophylaxis, Nairobi, Kenya. November 2024 First Edition."); `format_profile: clinical_protocol` (same style as the other Kenya MoH document — empiric-treatment/surgical-prophylaxis tables by clinical scenario, not GRADE recommendation statements); `topic_tags` from the printed table of contents (PDF pages 7-8). "NICE Neonatal infection - antibiotics for prevention and treatment (May 2026).pdf" — title/publisher/`external_ref` (`NG195`) read from the PDF's own embedded document metadata (`/Title`, `/Author`, `/Keywords`), cross-checked against page 1's cover text and URL (`nice.org.uk/guidance/ng195`) — both agree; `version_label`/`effective_date` follow the WHO-SBI-entry precedent for a since-updated document (original publish date, "20 April 2021", kept as `effective_date`; "last updated 13 May 2026" recorded in `version_label` rather than replacing it); `format_profile: grade_recommendations` (numbered recommendation statements, matching the WHO documents' style); `topic_tags` from the table of contents (PDF pages 3-6).
- **Licence deliberately left `TODO_CONFIRM` for both, with the specific document text quoted rather than a guess**: the Antibiotic Use Guidelines' imprint page states only "All rights reserved" — the **same words**, but explicitly **not the same document**, as the Comprehensive Newborn Care Protocols entry the operator already confirmed a `CC BY-NC-SA 4.0` override for (DEVIATIONS #41) — that confirmation is per-document and was not carried over here, since a shared publisher and a shared boilerplate rights phrase are not evidence of a shared licence decision. The NICE document states "© NICE 2026. All rights reserved. Subject to Notice of rights (https://www.nice.org.uk/terms-and-conditions#notice-of-rights)" — a named NICE policy document, not a Creative Commons licence, and not assumed equivalent to either Kenya MoH situation. Both manifest entries' `_licence_warning`/`_source_notes` record this reasoning inline, matching the file's existing documentation discipline.
- **Verified**: `python -c "import json; json.load(...)"` confirms valid JSON; `python -m scripts.prepare_sample_guidelines --strict` correctly lists both new entries as `MISSING: licence` (the only missing field) and exits non-zero under `--strict` — the intended fail-closed behavior, not a bug, since the licence genuinely isn't confirmed yet.
- **Real, unrelated state re-confirmed while doing this**: `prepare_sample_guidelines`'s own output also re-surfaced the two unexplained duplicate-content files noted in #167 (`kenya_protocols.pdf`, 174 pages matching Comprehensive Newborn Care Protocols exactly; `who_sepsis.pdf`, 107 pages matching the WHO SBI document exactly) as `MISSING: title, publisher, version_label, effective_date, licence` — still untouched, not manifested, not deleted; the operator did not ask about these two specifically in this request.
- **Not done, and why**: no `source_pages` excerpt was created from either new document in this entry — the operator's request was to manifest the full documents as found in `data/sample_guidelines/`, not to decide a topic/page range for either. Both are ingestable in full once the licence line is confirmed; extracting a smaller topic-specific excerpt (e.g. the Antibiotic Use Guidelines' "5. NEONATAL SEPSIS IN INFANTS < 60 DAYS", PDF page 23) remains a separate, later decision.
- **Reversible?:** yes — `manifest.json` is operator-local and gitignored (never committed); the two draft entries can be edited or removed freely with no effect on any other document's entry or on any code path (nothing reads this file except `prepare_sample_guidelines.py`'s validator and `app.ingestion.page_provenance`'s `source_pages` lookup — neither entry declares `source_pages`, so ingestion behavior is unaffected either way until the licence line is resolved and the file is actually submitted via `POST /ingest/documents`).

### 169. Operator confirmed both new documents' licence lines — recorded per DEVIATIONS #41's precedent, not assumed or carried over between documents
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-21 / operator request ("Confirm the licence lines and update manifest.json. Confirm the new PDFs"), following #168's draft
- **Requirement ID(s):** ARCH-038
- **What was confirmed**: the operator set `"National Antibiotic Use Guidelines"` (Kenya MoH, 2024)'s licence to `CC BY-NC-SA 4.0` directly in `manifest.json` — the **same value**, but a **separately made decision**, from the sibling Kenya MoH document's #41 confirmation (both documents' own text states only "All rights reserved" and grants no licence; the operator's choice to apply the same open licence to both is theirs to make, not something #168 assumed on their behalf). The operator also confirmed the NICE document's licence line, choosing to record NICE's own stated terms verbatim (`© NICE 2026. All rights reserved. Subject to Notice of rights: https://www.nice.org.uk/terms-and-conditions#notice-of-rights`) rather than mapping it to a CC-style label, since it isn't one.
- **What this session did**: updated `manifest.json`'s `_comment`/`_licence_warning` header and both entries' `_source_notes` to record these as confirmed (dated, per DEVIATIONS #41's own documentation convention) rather than leaving #168's "TODO_CONFIRM"/"pending" language in place now that it's stale. Also cleaned up a formatting artifact left over from #168's own draft: the NICE `licence` string had an unmatched trailing `)` (from the original `"TODO_CONFIRM (c) NICE 2026, ..."` draft, where `(c)` stood for "copyright" but the sentence's own closing paren had no matching open) — fixed to read cleanly now that it's a confirmed value, not silently carried forward as a typo.
- **Verified**: `python -m scripts.prepare_sample_guidelines --strict` — all four currently-manifested real documents (the WHO SBI 2024 document, the Comprehensive Newborn Care Protocols document, and the two newly confirmed — Antibiotic Use Guidelines and NICE NG195) now show `OK`, zero missing fields. Only the two long-standing, unexplained duplicate-content files (`kenya_protocols.pdf`, `who_sepsis.pdf`, still unmanifested, still untouched, DEVIATIONS #167/#168) remain flagged `MISSING`, as expected.
- **Real, unexpected, unexplained state discovered while re-validating (flagged immediately, not silently absorbed or fixed)**: `prepare_sample_guidelines` now lists only **6** guideline documents, not 7 — `"WHO recommendations on newborn health guidelines (WHO May 2017).pdf"` (the third of the original Phase-1 three-document reference corpus, per README.md's own description) is **no longer present on disk** in `data/sample_guidelines/`, and its `manifest.json` entry is also gone. Confirmed by direct `ls`: the file does not exist in `data/sample_guidelines/` (only 6 PDFs remain, none named this) and was not moved to the new `data/guideline_sources/` (empty except `.gitkeep`/`README.md`) either. This is not something any tool call in this session did — no `rm`, `mv`, or file-writing command in this session's history touched that filename, and the same "manifest.json changed on disk since you last read it" notice that preceded this turn is the only external signal available. **Not investigated further or acted on** — raised directly to the operator in this turn's chat response rather than guessed at, per the instruction to investigate unexpected state (especially a deletion) before doing anything about it, and never to silently accept a file loss as fine.
- **Reversible?:** the licence-line documentation update is fully reversible (text-only, gitignored file). The missing WHO 2017 PDF is a separate, **not-yet-understood** state change this entry does not attempt to reverse, restore, or explain — flagged, not resolved.

### 170. Operator confirmed the WHO 2017 deletion was intentional; hard-removed it from the live corpus (Postgres + Qdrant) — a genuinely new capability, no precedent in this codebase — plus a real bug found and fixed, a real architectural gap discovered and flagged, and the #167/#168 "mystery duplicate files" finally explained
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-21 / operator confirmation ("I deleted it, remove it from references any ingestion"), following #169's flagged discovery
- **Requirement ID(s):** ARCH-038, ARCH §5.1
- **What "remove it from references any ingestion" required, once investigated**: querying `corpus.document` directly showed the WHO 2017 document (`"WHO recommendations on newborn health guidelines"`) **was actually ingested** — 1 `document_version`, 13 chunks, both in Postgres and as Qdrant vectors — not just a manifest entry. `document_version.status` supports `active|superseded|withdrawn`, but marking it `withdrawn` alone would **not** have actually removed it from retrieval: `app.ingestion.chunk_persistence` writes each chunk's Qdrant payload `status: "active"` once, at ingest time, and nothing in this codebase ever updates that payload afterward — not even on legitimate supersession (`create_or_supersede_document_version` only ever touches Postgres). Since `app.retrieval.hybrid.retrieve()` filters on `status=active` against the *Qdrant* payload, a `withdrawn` Postgres row would have done nothing observable. **This is a real, separate, previously-undiscovered gap**: a superseded document's old chunks are likely still being served by retrieval today, because the status change never reaches Qdrant. Flagged here, not fixed — fixing it generally is a retrieval-path change with its own testing needs, out of scope for removing one document.
- **What was built**: `backend/scripts/remove_document.py` (new) — the first hard-delete capability this codebase has ever had (`app.ingestion.documents`' own docstring: supersession "keeps chunks/vectors retrievable so existing citations still resolve" — deletion was never the designed path). Takes a `--title`, deletes the document's chunks from Qdrant by point id (`store._client.delete`, no method for this exists yet on the `VectorStore` Protocol), then deletes `Chunk`/`DocumentVersion`/`Document` rows from Postgres in FK dependency order. `--dry-run` supported and used first.
- **Real bug found and fixed on the first real run**: the initial version used `session.delete(version)`/`session.delete(document)` (ORM-tracked deletes) rather than explicit ordered statements. SQLAlchemy's unit-of-work flush order does not reliably infer FK dependency from a plain `Mapped[uuid.UUID]` foreign-key column with no declared `relationship()`, and attempted to delete `document` before `document_version` had actually been removed, raising `ForeignKeyViolation` **at commit time** — after the Qdrant delete (a separate, non-transactional client call) had already succeeded and could not be rolled back. Result: a real, transient inconsistency (Qdrant point count 314→301, but Postgres still showed all 13 chunks and the document row). **Fixed** by replacing both `session.delete(...)` calls with explicit `session.execute(delete(...))` statements in dependency order (chunk → document_version → document), each committed immediately rather than left to unit-of-work ordering — then re-ran the corrected script, which completed cleanly and brought Postgres back in sync with Qdrant (verified directly: 301 chunks in both stores, document gone from Postgres, 2 documents remain).
- **#167/#168's "mystery duplicate files" finally explained, not just re-flagged**: querying `corpus.document.source_uri` for the two other real documents showed they were ingested under `data/sample_guidelines/kenya_protocols.pdf` and `data/sample_guidelines/who_sepsis.pdf` — **these are not stray duplicates**, they are the actual files `POST /ingest/documents` stored under the multipart upload's own filename at ingestion time, while the *descriptively-named* copies (`"Comprehensive Newborn Care Protocols (Ministry of Health Kenya, Nov 2022).pdf"`, `"WHO recommendations for management of serious bacterial infections...pdf"`) are separate copies placed alongside them later, matching `manifest.json`'s filename-keyed convention for `prepare_sample_guidelines.py`'s validator. Both copies have identical byte size (confirmed on disk) because they are the same content, not accidental duplication. This corrects, not retracts, #167/#168's "unexplained" framing (those entries stay as written, append-only) — noted here now that the real explanation is known.
- **Gold-chunk fallout, handled proactively rather than left as a new #164-class bug**: 8 `EvalQuestion` rows had `gold_relevant_chunks` pointing into the now-deleted document's 13 chunks. Ran `scripts.refresh_stale_gold_chunks` (built for exactly this class of problem, DEVIATIONS #164) immediately after the removal — see the next log line/entry for the real result, since this was still running as this entry was written.
- **Verified**: `--dry-run` first, matched expectations (13 chunks, 1 version, 1 document); real run's bug caught immediately via direct Postgres/Qdrant re-query (not trusted from the script's own exit code alone, since the script partially succeeded and exited non-zero the first time); fixed script re-verified with `ruff check`/`ruff format --check`/`mypy` clean, then re-run to completion; post-run state re-queried directly (301/301 chunk count match, document absent from Postgres) rather than assumed from print output.
- **Reversible?:** no — this is the first genuinely hard-to-reverse action of this kind taken this session: 13 chunks and their vectors are permanently gone from both stores (no soft-delete, no backup taken here). The source PDF was already gone (operator's own prior action); this only removed its now-orphaned downstream corpus footprint. `remove_document.py` itself is a reusable, reversible *addition* (delete the script, gain nothing back) — the data it removed is not recoverable through this session's own means.

### 171. Renamed `data/sample_guidelines/` to `data/excerpt_guidelines/` — directory path only, code/config identifiers left unchanged
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-21 / operator request ("rename data/sample_guidelines/ to data/excerpt_guidelines/")
- **Requirement ID(s):** ARCH-038
- **Scoping decision made, not assumed**: the request named the directory, not the `SAMPLE_GUIDELINES_DIR` env var, the `sample_guidelines_dir` config attribute, or the `prepare_sample_guidelines.py`/`fetch_sample_guidelines.py` script filenames. Renaming those too would touch public config surface (anyone's existing `.env`) and command muscle-memory (`make prepare-guidelines`'s underlying module path) that wasn't asked for — done literally: every *literal path string* pointing at the actual directory was updated; every *code/env-var identifier* that merely contains the word "sample_guidelines" was left exactly as it was. If the operator wants full-surface consistency (renaming the env var and/or scripts too), that is a separate, larger follow-up, not assumed here.
- **What moved**: `git mv` for the two git-tracked files (`.gitkeep`, `manifest.example.json`); a plain `mv` for the six untracked real files (the operator-supplied PDFs and the gitignored real `manifest.json`) — nothing in the untracked set needed git history preservation. Old directory removed once empty (`rmdir`, not `rm -rf` — confirmed empty first).
- **Every literal path reference updated** (grep-verified, zero remaining `data/sample_guidelines` references outside `DEVIATIONS.md`'s append-only history, which is correctly left alone as historically accurate): `backend/app/config.py`'s default, `.env.example`, **`.env`** (the real, gitignored file — its stale value was silently overriding the code default via env-var precedence, confirmed live: `get_settings().sample_guidelines_dir` still returned the old path after rebuilding the image, until this was found and fixed), `docker-compose.yml` (both `api`/`worker` bind mounts), `Makefile`'s `prepare-guidelines` target, `.gitignore`'s guideline-corpus block, `backend/scripts/prepare_sample_guidelines.py` and `fetch_sample_guidelines.py`'s docstrings, `backend/tests/test_prepare_guidelines.py`'s one literal-path assertion, `data/guideline_sources/README.md`, and `README.md`/`ARCHITECTURE.md`/`ARCHITECTURE-ESSENTIALS.md`/`TRACEABILITY.md`'s prose and layout-tree references.
- **Real bug caught, not assumed fixed**: after updating `config.py`'s default and rebuilding, `get_settings().sample_guidelines_dir` inside the freshly recreated container still printed the *old* path — traced to the real `.env` (not `.env.example`, which was already correct) still declaring `SAMPLE_GUIDELINES_DIR=data/sample_guidelines`, which Pydantic Settings' env-var precedence honors over the class default unconditionally. Fixed and re-verified live before trusting the rename worked.
- **Verified**: live — after the container recreate + `.env` fix, `docker compose exec api env` and `get_settings().sample_guidelines_dir` both show the new path; `python -m scripts.prepare_sample_guidelines --strict` against the renamed directory reproduces the exact same 6-document/2-MISSING result as before the rename (byte-for-byte same validator output modulo the directory name in its own printed header); `tests/test_page_provenance.py`/`test_process_document_task.py`/`test_ingest_routes.py` (35 tests) pass unchanged; `tests/test_prepare_guidelines.py` shows the same 2 pre-existing, unrelated failures as before (the `Path(__file__).resolve().parents[N]` container-layout bug already flagged in #166/#169's investigation) — confirmed same root cause, not a new regression from the rename.
- **Reversible?:** yes — a directory rename plus text substitutions across tracked files, fully undoable by reversing both; the one substantive catch (the stale `.env` override) is now also corrected, closing a gap that would otherwise have silently resurrected the old path on any future config-only edit.
