### 172. #170's gold-chunk refresh completed: 3 of 8 affected questions recovered valid chunks, 5 dropped (no guideline match left for that content) — pool now 64, 0 stale
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-21 / direct continuation of #170 (the refresh was still running when #170 was written)
- **Requirement ID(s):** ARCH-038, PRD-109
- **Real result**: of the 8 `EvalQuestion` rows whose `gold_relevant_chunks` pointed into the removed WHO 2017 document's chunks, 3 resolved to fresh, valid chunks from the remaining corpus (`d00deab2...`, `de33ff61...`, `b1dfba2f...`) and 5 resolved to `None` — the live pipeline, run fresh, found no guideline match for those specific narratives at all now that the WHO newborn-health document's general postnatal-care content is gone and the remaining two documents (SBI/antibiotics-focused) don't cover the same ground. Confirmed directly against the live DB post-run (not just the script's own printed summary): the calibration pool is now **64 questions, 0 with any stale gold chunk id** (down from 69 pre-#166/#169's WHO-document removal chain).
- **Not re-run**: the retrieval-tuning/embedding-ablation reports were not regenerated against this newly-64-question pool in this entry — the operator's requests since #165 (rename, licence confirmation, document removal) didn't ask for it, and re-running them yet again for a 5-question pool shrink is offered, not assumed, this time.
- **Verified**: live query directly against `corpus.chunk`/`eval.eval_question` (not the refresh script's own stdout) confirms 64/0 exactly matches the expected count (69 − 5 dropped = 64).
- **Reversible?:** the same as #164/#170 — a data correction (removing/replacing broken references), not a design change; the pre-refresh values are preserved in #170's chat record and this entry's text, not in a DB backup.

### 173. Fixed the manifest/naming audit findings: re-keyed `manifest.json` to the actual (operator-renamed) filenames with real `source_pages` per excerpt, fixed the two stale `document.source_uri` values, and added a read-only mount so the fix is actually resolvable — plus one real page-numbering verification and one real content-accuracy correction found along the way
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-21 / operator request ("Yes, fix both — manifest re-keying and the source_uri values"), following the naming audit
- **Requirement ID(s):** ARCH-038
- **Context**: since the audit, the operator had (outside this session's tool calls) renamed/reorganized the files themselves — `data/excerpt_guidelines/` now holds 2 documents ingested in full (`..._FULL.pdf`) and 3 genuine page-range excerpts (`..._pages_N_M.pdf`, including **two separate excerpts of the same WHO SBI publication** at different page ranges), with the corresponding full originals moved into `data/guideline_sources/` as `..._FULL.pdf`. The manifest still had the old, pre-rename filenames as keys, matching nothing.
- **Re-keying, done carefully rather than mechanically**: renamed all 4 manifest entries to their current real filenames. **One entry became two**: the WHO SBI document now has two distinct excerpt files (`_pages_16_20`, `_pages_76_77`), each needing its own manifest entry with its own `source_pages`. Each was given `external_ref: null` (not the shared ISBN the full document carries) and a distinguishing title (page range included) — **a real design decision, not a mechanical copy**: `app.ingestion.documents._find_document_by_external_ref` matches on the exact `external_ref` string, so if both excerpts shared the full document's ISBN, ingesting the second would be treated as a newer *version* of the first and would silently supersede it — wrong, since they are two distinct, coexisting excerpts of different content, not two versions of the same thing.
- **Real content-accuracy correction, caught before it shipped**: initially drafted `topic_tags` for the two new WHO SBI excerpts by guessing a subset of the *full document's* TOC-derived tag list (a reasonable-looking shortcut). Before committing that, actually read each excerpt's own extracted text (`pypdf`) — the guess was wrong in both directions: pages 16-20 are the introduction/background and the WHO 7-sign IMCI algorithm plus fast-breathing/suspected-sepsis/suspected-pneumonia case *definitions*, not "antibiotics" (no antibiotic content on these specific pages, even though the parent document is antibiotics-focused overall); pages 76-77 are literally "Table 3.1 Antibiotic dosing" with specific drug/dose regimens (ampicillin+gentamicin, amoxicillin+gentamicin, cloxacillin+gentamicin, ampicillin/cefotaxime/ceftriaxone) plus staphylococcal-sepsis/meningitis case categories — much more specific than the guessed tags. Replaced both with tags read directly from each excerpt's own text, per ARCH-038's "never invent, only what's verifiable from the document" discipline — the same standard already applied to every other manifest entry, just newly at risk here because "copy a subset of the parent's tags" felt safe and wasn't.
- **Real page-numbering verification, not assumed**: the Comprehensive Newborn Care Protocols excerpt's own extracted text shows printed page labels "31"/"32" on it — not "47"/"48", the numbers in its filename. Given the operator's own question two turns earlier about physical-position vs. printed-label page numbering, this was checked directly rather than assumed either way: copied the archival full original into the container (not bind-mounted at the time) and confirmed physical PDF page 47 (0-indexed 46) of the 174-page full document matches this excerpt's content exactly, while physical page 31 is completely different content — confirming `source_pages: [47, 48]` is correct (matches physical position, the same convention this codebase's parser and typical LangChain loaders use) and the printed "31"/"32" is simply ~16 pages of front-matter offset, not an error in how the excerpt was built.
- **`document.source_uri` fixed for the two live-ingested documents**, updated from the dead `data/sample_guidelines/{kenya_protocols,who_sepsis}.pdf` (broken twice over: the #171 rename never touched already-ingested rows' stored paths, and the files were also moved) to their real current location, `data/guideline_sources/...\_FULL.pdf`.
- **Real gap caught before declaring this fixed**: `data/guideline_sources/` was never bind-mounted into `api`/`worker` at all (by design, so `prepare_sample_guidelines.py`'s scan would never discover it) — updating `source_uri` to point there would have "fixed" it to a path structurally invisible inside the container, worse than before in one sense. Verified this directly (`ls` inside the container failed) rather than assuming the update worked once committed to Postgres. **Fixed properly**: added a `:ro` bind mount for `data/guideline_sources/` to both `api`/`worker` in `docker-compose.yml`, with a comment distinguishing "resolvable for one already-known row's own path" from "discoverable by any scan" — the latter is what the original no-mount design was protecting against, and a read-only mount of a directory nothing scans doesn't reopen that risk. `guideline_sources/README.md` updated to describe this precisely rather than leave its now-inaccurate "no ingestion code path scans, reads, or references this directory" claim standing.
- **Verified**: `python -c "import json; json.load(...)"` valid; `python -m scripts.prepare_sample_guidelines --strict` — all 5 real files now show `OK`, zero missing fields (was 5/5 `MISSING` before this entry). `document.source_uri` re-queried directly post-fix: `os.path.exists(...)` is `True` for both, from inside the freshly recreated container (not assumed from the UPDATE statement succeeding). `tests/test_page_provenance.py`/`test_process_document_task.py`/`test_ingest_routes.py` (35 tests) pass unchanged after the mount change.
- **Not done**: none of the 3 new excerpt files have been ingested into the live corpus yet (still just the 2 pre-existing documents, 301 chunks) — this entry only fixed the metadata/provenance layer so ingestion *would* work correctly if triggered, it did not trigger it.
- **Reversible?:** yes — a manifest re-key (gitignored, operator-local file), two `Document.source_uri` UPDATE statements (trivially reversible to their prior, already-broken values if ever needed), a docker-compose mount addition, and a documentation correction. No chunk/vector data was touched.

### 174. Ingested all 3 new excerpts (plus 2 documents that turned out never to have been ingested before) — new `scripts/ingest_manifest_documents.py`, a real column-width bug, and a real "best-effort" bug that wasn't, both found only by real data
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-21 / operator request ("Ingest the three new excerpts")
- **Requirement ID(s):** ARCH-038, PRD-001, PRD-004
- **What was built**: `backend/scripts/ingest_manifest_documents.py` (new) — the first thing in this codebase that drives ingestion directly from `manifest.json`'s own metadata. Previously the *only* ingestion path was `POST /ingest/documents`, one file at a time, with metadata retyped by hand into HTTP form fields; `scripts/prepare_sample_guidelines.py` reads the manifest only to validate it, never to ingest. This script reuses `create_or_supersede_document_version` + `_run_process_document` unchanged — the same pipeline the HTTP route triggers — skipping any entry that's incomplete (never ingesting invented metadata, ARCH-038) or already ingested (`content_sha256` match, the same idempotency `create_or_supersede_document_version` already provides — not a separate check this script adds). Each document processed in its own `session_scope()`, not one shared session for the whole run — the same reasoning as `remove_document.py` (#170): Qdrant's upsert is not transactional with the Postgres commit, so one document's failure must not roll back another already-committed document in the same run. This paid off directly: it did.
- **Real bug #1, found only on first real run**: `corpus.document_version.version_label` was `String(64)`. The WHO SBI document's own manifest entry (drafted in an earlier session, never previously exercised against real ingestion) carries a genuine, verbatim, sourced value — `"2024, corrigenda of 25 March 2025 incorporated into this electronic file"` — 72 characters, silently never inserted before because nothing had ever actually tried. Fixed by widening the column to 256 (matching `document.publisher`'s width) via a new Alembic migration (`9159e0f8bb84`) + matching model change, **not** by truncating or paraphrasing a real, sourced value to fit an arbitrary limit — the value is exactly what the document's own imprint page states, and shortening it would mean inventing a different, unsourced label. Migration applied and verified directly (`information_schema.columns.character_maximum_length = 256`).
- **Real bug #2, found only on first real run against a document with actual embedded images**: `app.ingestion.chunking._extract_pdf_figures` is documented as "best-effort" (ARCH §6 rule 3b) but had no error handling at all — `Pillow` isn't installed, so `page.images` raised `ImportError` and crashed the entire ingest, not just skipped figure extraction. This would have hit the exact same wall via the real `POST /ingest/documents` route for *any* PDF with embedded images, not just this script — a latent, previously-undiscovered gap, not something this task introduced. Fixed two ways: (1) added `pillow>=11.0` as a real base dependency (`pyproject.toml` + regenerated `requirements-lock.txt` via `pip-compile`, so figure extraction actually works as designed, not just fails safely) — caught and fixed a real self-inflicted mistake while doing this: edited `pyproject.toml` on the host but ran `pip-compile` inside the container against its stale, pre-edit baked-in copy twice in a row before noticing pillow was silently absent from the regenerated lock file both times; the second attempt (after actually `docker cp`-ing the edited file in first) produced a correct, duplicate-free lock file (verified: no repeated package entries, consistent with a single clean resolver run, not two racing processes corrupting the output). (2) Wrapped the `page.images` access in a `try/except`, logging a warning and skipping *that page's* figures rather than aborting the document — true to the "best-effort" framing the code already claimed, and defensive against future malformed-image cases pillow's own presence won't fully rule out. 1 new test (`tests/test_chunking.py::test_figure_extraction_failure_is_best_effort_not_fatal`) — a fake page whose `.images` raises `ImportError`, asserting `chunk_document` still returns the document's other chunks (recommendation/prose/etc.) rather than raising.
- **A recurring operational mistake, caught each time rather than shipped**: `backend/` is baked into the image via `COPY . .`, not bind-mounted — editing a host file (`pyproject.toml`, then separately `tests/test_chunking.py`) has no effect on a running container until it's either `docker cp`'d in or the image is rebuilt. Hit this twice in this same entry (once for the lock file, once for the new test silently reporting "0 items collected" against the container's stale copy) — each time caught by directly verifying the *container's* state rather than trusting the host edit, consistent with this session's established discipline, but worth naming explicitly here since it recurred within one entry.
- **Real result**: the 3 excerpts from #173 all ingested cleanly (10, 1, and 2 chunks respectively for pages 16-20, 76-77, and 47-48). Discovered along the way that the other two manifest-complete documents — Antibiotic Use Guidelines and NICE NG195 — **had never actually been ingested at all**, at any point in this project's history (confirmed: no `Document` row existed for either before this run) — they'd only ever been manifest entries sitting on disk with complete, validator-approved metadata. Ingesting them for the first time added 144 and 154 chunks respectively. **Corpus now**: 7 documents, 612 chunks, verified identical in Postgres and Qdrant (`SELECT count(*)` = 612 both places, not assumed from the script's own printed summary).
- **Verified**: `ruff check`/`ruff format --check` clean on all touched files (script, migration excluded per the project's own `extend-exclude` convention, model, chunking.py, test); no new `mypy` findings; full offline suite 627 passed (up from 626 — the new figure-extraction test) + the same 19 pre-existing, unrelated failures (re-confirmed identical names/root causes, not grown or changed by this entry) + 2 skipped. Live: direct Postgres+Qdrant chunk-count cross-check post-ingestion.
- **Reversible?:** the ingestion itself is not trivially reversible (612 real chunks/vectors now in the live corpus — `remove_document.py` from #170 could undo any one of them if ever needed, but nothing here does). The column-width migration is reversible (a `downgrade()` is defined, though re-narrowing would need the WHO SBI row's `version_label` shortened first or it would fail the same way in reverse). The dependency addition and the defensive-handling code are ordinary, fully reversible code changes.

### 175. Retired the two legacy full-document ingestions now that excerpts of the same publications exist — closing the redundant-coverage gap the operator caught, with a much larger gold-chunk cascade than any prior cleanup
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-21 / operator question ("Why is corpus not at most 5 documents..."), then explicit instruction ("Yes, retire the two legacy full ingestions. data/excerpt_guidelines replaces full in the retrieval index, data/guideline_sources stays archival-only")
- **Requirement ID(s):** ARCH-038
- **What was found (by the operator, not this session) and confirmed**: after #174's ingestion, the corpus had 7 documents, not 5 — because `Comprehensive Newborn Care Protocols` (213 chunks) and `WHO recommendations for management of serious bacterial infections in infants` (88 chunks) were **legacy full ingestions from before any excerpting work existed**, never retired once #173/#174 gave their same content real page-range excerpts. Their `page_start`/`page_end` ranges already fully covered the new excerpts' page ranges (pp. 47-48 and pp. 16-20/76-77 respectively) — meaning that content was indexed **twice**, directly contradicting the design intent from the earlier "should I extract just the relevant pages" conversation (full document archival-only, excerpt is what's actually retrieved).
- **What was done**: `scripts.remove_document --title "..."` (DEVIATIONS #170) run for both legacy documents, dry-run first for each (confirmed 213 and 88 chunks respectively, matching expectations exactly), then for real. No FK-ordering issue this time — the #170 fix (explicit ordered `delete()` statements) held on a clean second use.
- **Real result**: corpus is now genuinely 5 documents / 311 chunks (10 + 1 + 2 + 144 + 154, i.e. exactly the 3 excerpts + 2 full non-excerpted documents), verified identical in Postgres and Qdrant directly (not from script output).
- **Gold-chunk cascade, much larger than any prior cleanup**: **all 64** `well_supported`/`auto_generated` calibration questions, plus 5 more outside that filter (69 total `EvalQuestion` rows with a non-null `gold_relevant_chunks`), had at least one chunk id pointing into the two just-removed documents — because every eval question ever generated in this project's history was generated against a corpus that, until today, only ever had full-document ingestions (WHO 2017, Comprehensive Newborn Care Protocols, WHO SBI), never an excerpt-based one. Ran `scripts.refresh_stale_gold_chunks --dry-run` first given the unprecedented scale (69/69, not 8 or 21 like #170/#172) before committing to it — **44 of 69 recovered valid new gold chunks** (substantially better than feared: the two newly-ingested full documents, Antibiotic Use Guidelines and NICE, are large and cover much of the same sepsis/antibiotics ground the retired documents did), **25 resolved to no match** and were dropped. Then ran for real — identical split (44/25), confirming the pipeline's determinism held at this scale too.
- **Real result, verified directly**: calibration pool is now **42 questions, 0 with any stale gold chunk id** (was 64 before this entry; not yet re-verified against the full non-calibration-filtered 69, but the calibration slice — the one every report in this session actually reads — is clean).
- **Not yet done**: the retrieval-tuning/embedding-ablation reports have not been regenerated against this new 42-question, 5-document, excerpt-based corpus — a fundamentally different measurement setting from every prior run this session (narrow topic-specific excerpts + two large general documents, replacing three large general documents) — offered, not assumed, given how much has changed underneath it.
- **Reversible?:** no, for the same reason as every corpus-content removal this session — the 301 chunks/vectors are gone, and the pre-refresh gold_relevant_chunks values exist only in this entry's and #170-174's chat/log text, not a DB backup. The design decision itself (excerpt replaces full in retrieval, full stays archival) is reversible by re-ingesting the two retired documents in full again if ever wanted.

### 176. Re-ran the retrieval-tuning and embedding-ablation reports against the new 5-document/311-chunk/42-question corpus — two ranking reversals, neither seen on the old corpus in any prior run this session
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-21 / operator request ("regenerate them"), following #175
- **Requirement ID(s):** PRD-109, PRD-110, ARCH-040, ARCH-041
- **What was done**: no code change — re-ran `scripts.run_retrieval_weight_sweep` and `scripts.run_model_ablation` (`MODEL_ABLATION_BACKEND=local`) live against the corpus as it now stands after #175 (5 documents: 3 real page-range excerpts + 2 full non-excerpted documents; 301 chunks; 42-question calibration pool). Both scripts' own stdout confirmed the question count (`sweeping 42 calibration question(s)`, `ranked 42 calibration question(s)`) before trusting either chart.
- **Real result — retrieval-tuning**: recall@k now saturates even faster than the prior (already-fast, post-#164/#165) corpus — most alphas and RRF reach ≈0.98-1.0 by k≈20-25. **MRR@24 ranking reversed from every prior run this session**: RRF (production baseline) is now the single best performer (≈0.585-0.59), essentially tied with alpha=0.0 (≈0.585) and clearly ahead of every other alpha, falling to ≈0.37 at alpha=1.0 (pure BM25) — on every previous corpus this session (#123, #161, #165), alpha=0.0 was unambiguously the best performer and RRF sat mid-pack, never best. With N=42 (down from 64-73 on prior runs) this is a much smaller, noisier sample; the ranking flip is real in this run's numbers but not something to treat as a settled conclusion about RRF vs. weighted fusion in general.
- **Real result — embedding ablation**: recall@k for all four arms converges to ≈0.95-1.0 by k≈40-50, similar shape to before. **MRR@24 ranking also reversed**: RRF (≈0.585-0.59) is now the clear top performer, ahead of SapBERT+BM25 (≈0.52 [≈0.42, ≈0.64]), the 3-way combination (≈0.55 [≈0.44, ≈0.66]), and MedCPT+BM25 (≈0.40 [≈0.33, ≈0.47]) — on every previous run this session (#131, #160, #165), SapBERT+BM25 had the highest point estimate. All four CIs are wide and overlapping at this N, so this is not a statistically distinguishable result either way — the honest reading is "no arm is significantly better than RRF here," same qualitative conclusion as every prior run, just via a different point-estimate ordering.
- **Reading this correctly, not overclaiming a cause**: both reversals appeared in the same run, against the same newly-restructured corpus, and both plausibly share one root cause — this corpus is now dominated by two large, broad-coverage documents (Antibiotic Use Guidelines, NICE, 144+154 of the corpus's 311 chunks) plus three tiny, highly specific excerpts, a very different retrieval-difficulty profile from three large, topically-broad documents. Untangling whether that's really the driver, versus simply N shrinking from 64-73 to 42, would need its own investigation (e.g. bootstrapping the same alpha/arm comparison at this new N) — not undertaken here, since it wasn't asked for.
- **Verified**: live only, both reports — regenerated PNGs (`retrieval_tuning_report.png`, `model_ablation_report.png`) visually reviewed and copied into the tracked host paths; question counts confirmed from each script's own stdout before reading any chart.
- **Reversible?:** yes — both are read-only report regenerations; no code or data changed by this entry itself (the corpus/pool change was #170-175's).

### 179. `retrieval_tuning_report.png` panel B (chart 2) narrowed to k=8..16 step 2, per direct follow-up request — surfaced and fixed a real, previously-latent bug in `_MAX_K`'s own invariant
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-21 / operator request ("For the retrieval_tuning_report.png, for panel B, change the range at k to be from 8 to 16, with increments of 2")
- **Requirement ID(s):** PRD-109, ARCH-040
- **Real bug found before making the requested change, not after**: `sweep.py`'s `_MAX_K = max(*K_VALUES, *CHART2_K_VALUES)` never included `MRR_K` (24) in its own max — safe only by coincidence, because every `CHART2_K_VALUES` this constant has ever had (`{20,22,...,40}`, `3..36`, `4..36`, `24..48`, and #178's `K_VALUES` narrowing to `2..20`) happened to have a max ≥ 24. The requested `8..16` (max 16) is the first value ever below `MRR_K` — every ranked list `rank_question` produces is truncated to `_MAX_K` before *either* recall@k or `mrr(..., MRR_K)` is computed from it, so panel C's "Mean Reciprocal Rank@24" would have silently become MRR@16 with no error, no warning, nothing — the title would have kept saying "24" while computing something else entirely.
- **What was done**: `_MAX_K` changed to `max(*K_VALUES, *CHART2_K_VALUES, MRR_K)`, with a comment explaining why (the invariant it must now maintain, and that this was a real gap found by this exact follow-up request, not a hypothetical). Then `CHART2_K_VALUES` changed from `range(24, 49, 4)` (7 values) to `range(8, 17, 2)` (5 values) — the actual requested change. `report.py`'s panel B docstring updated to match. **New regression test** (`tests/test_retrieval_tuning.py::test_max_k_covers_mrr_k_and_both_chart_grids`) asserting `_MAX_K >= MRR_K` and `_MAX_K >= max(K_VALUES)`/`max(CHART2_K_VALUES)` directly — so a future change to any of these three constants that reopens this gap fails a fast offline test instead of silently mislabeling a chart again.
- **Verified**: offline — `ruff check`/`ruff format --check` clean; `mypy` at the pre-existing accepted baseline; `tests/test_retrieval_tuning.py` (11 passed, up from 10 — the new invariant test). Full suite: 628 passed (up from 627), same 19 pre-existing unrelated failures. Live: rebuilt + recreated `api`, re-ran the real sweep against the current 42-question/5-document corpus — panel B now shows exactly k=8,10,12,14,16; panel C's MRR@24 values are numerically unchanged from #178's run (confirming the fix preserves correct behavior when the invariant already happened to hold, it only guards the case where it wouldn't have).
- **Reversible?:** yes — the `_MAX_K` fix and the range change are both small, well-tested code changes; the old 7-value range and the pre-fix `_MAX_K` formula are both fully recoverable from git history (though reverting the `_MAX_K` fix itself would be reintroducing a known bug, not neutral).

### 180. `retrieval_tuning_report.png` panel C changed from MRR@24 to MRR@10, per direct follow-up request — simplest change yet, thanks to #179's fix landing just before it
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-21 / operator request ("For the retrieval_tuning_report.png, for panel C, use the Mean Reciprocal Rank @ 10")
- **Requirement ID(s):** PRD-109, ARCH-040
- **What was done**: `sweep.py`'s `MRR_K` changed from `24` to `10` (still a `CHART2_K_VALUES` member, `{8,10,12,14,16}` as of #179, continuing the same "not tied to production depth or best-recall derivation" history this constant has had since #128). Comment history extended to record this as the constant's fourth follow-up-driven value. `report.py`'s chart title/ylabel already render `f"Mean Reciprocal Rank@{MRR_K}"` dynamically — no template change needed, only the docstring's own history note updated to match.
- **Why this one was simpler than #179**: `_MAX_K = max(*K_VALUES, *CHART2_K_VALUES, MRR_K)` (fixed one entry ago, in #179) already accounts for `MRR_K` explicitly — lowering `MRR_K` to 10 only ever shrinks that max's relevant term, it can't reopen the under-truncation gap #179 just closed. Had this request landed *before* #179's fix, it would have been irrelevant to that specific bug (a lower `MRR_K` was never the failure mode — a `CHART2_K_VALUES` max *below* `MRR_K` was), but it's worth noting the fix's ordering was fortunate rather than by design.

- **Verified**: offline — `ruff check`/`ruff format --check` clean; `mypy` at the pre-existing accepted baseline; `tests/test_retrieval_tuning.py` (11 passed, no test hardcoded `MRR_K`'s value — confirmed via `test_max_k_covers_mrr_k_and_both_chart_grids` passing with the new value in place, exactly the regression test #179 added for this class of change). Full suite: 628 passed (unchanged), same 19 pre-existing unrelated failures. Live: rebuilt + recreated `api`, re-ran the real sweep against the current 42-question/5-document corpus — panel C's title/ylabel now correctly read "Mean Reciprocal Rank@10"; panels A and B visually unchanged, as expected.
- **Reversible?:** yes — a single constant + two docstring updates; the prior value (24) is fully recoverable from git history.
