### 184. `model_ablation_report.png` panel B refactored to distinguish single-stage vs. multi-stage per embedding arm — color=embedding model, shape=stage — per direct follow-up request; judgment call on what "multi-stage" means, flagged and confirmed
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-21 / operator request ("For panel B of model_ablation_report.png, refactor to distinguish single-stage versus multi-stage for each embedding ablation. Use different colors for the embedding models and different shapes for the single- and multi-stage lines/dots")
- **Requirement ID(s):** PRD-110, PRD-111, ARCH-041
- **Judgment call, flagged before implementing and confirmed by the operator (via AskUserQuestion → "Phase 7 query augmentation (criteria-reuse / vocabulary)")**: "multi-stage" is not a concept `model_ablation` had any notion of on its own — it's borrowed from Phase 7's orchestration ablation (PRD-111), which has two candidate query-augmentation mechanisms. Of those two, `criteria_reuse` (Arm B) was **not** used: it's documented (DEVIATIONS #152/#159) as byte-identical to single-stage on this corpus — zero `criteria`-type chunks, structurally unfireable — so it would plot no difference at all. `vocabulary` (Arm C, operator-attested `data/clinical_concepts.yaml`) was used instead: it's standalone-computable before any retrieval (`build_arm_c_query`) and is the only mechanism that can actually move this chart. This sub-choice (vocabulary over criteria-reuse specifically) was my own call, not separately re-confirmed word-for-word by the operator beyond the broader "Phase 7 query augmentation" selection — logged here for visibility.
- **Scope, also a judgment call**: the request said "panel B" explicitly, so panel A (recall@k) was left untouched — still single-stage only, byte-identical computation/values to every prior run. `rrf_production` (the production baseline) was included in the single-/multi-stage split alongside the three experimental arms, on the reasoning that it's still "an embedding configuration" for this chart's purposes and leaving it out would make the new shape encoding inconsistent (3 arms with two markers, 1 arm with only one) — not explicitly asked for either way.
- **What was done**:
  - `app/eval/retrieval_tuning/sweep.py`: `SweepQuestion` gained an additive `source_record_id: uuid.UUID | None = None` field (default preserves every existing construction site), populated from `EvalQuestion.source_record_id` in `fetch_calibration_questions` — needed so `model_ablation` can resolve each question's own synthetic patient record the same way `orchestration_ablation` already does.
  - `app/eval/model_ablation/ablation.py`: `rank_question` refactored — the per-channel embed/rank logic was factored out into a new `_rank_text` helper so it can be called against a second, augmented text without duplicating the wiring. `rank_question` gained optional `vocabulary`/`record` params (default `None`, preserving every existing caller's behavior byte-for-byte); when a vocabulary is passed, it calls `app.eval.orchestration_ablation.augment.build_arm_c_query` (reused unchanged, not reimplemented) and computes a second ranking set only when augmentation actually fires for that question — falling back to the single-stage ranking (no extra embed/query round-trip) otherwise, matching Phase 7's own "Arm C == Arm A when nothing matches" degradation exactly. `QuestionArmRankings` gained `multi_stage_rankings: dict[str, list[str]] | None` and `multi_stage_fired: bool`. `AblationResult.mrr_rows` rows now carry a `"stage"` key (`SINGLE_STAGE`/`MULTI_STAGE`); `recall_rows` (panel A) is completely untouched — no `"stage"` key, no behavior change. New `AblationResult.multi_stage_available`/`multi_stage_fired_rate` fields, mirroring `orchestration_ablation.SliceReport`'s existing `vocabulary_attested`/`fired_rate` convention. `run_full_ablation` gained `records_dir`/`concepts_path` params (default `"data/clinical_concepts.yaml"`, same as `run_orchestration_ablation.py`'s own CLI default) — loads the vocabulary via `load_attested_vocabulary` (reused unchanged) and resolves each question's record via `load_synthetic_record_index`/`resolve_source_record` (reused unchanged); when the vocabulary isn't attested, every question is ranked single-stage only, exactly as before this change (no crash, no placeholder run).
  - `app/eval/model_ablation/report.py`: `chart_mrr_by_arm` now plots two dodged points per arm (color = embedding arm, unchanged categorical palette; marker shape = stage — circle single-stage, triangle multi-stage) when `multi_stage_available`, with a two-part legend (arm-color swatches + stage-shape swatches). `rrf_production`'s marker is no longer a fixed diamond — now follows the same stage-shape encoding as every other arm, since shape had to fully hand over to stage once two independent dimensions were in play; it keeps its distinct gray color as the "production baseline" identity instead. Falls back to the previous single-point-per-arm appearance (all circles, no dodge) when multi-stage wasn't computed.
  - `scripts/run_model_ablation.py`: gained `--concepts-path` (default from `CLINICAL_CONCEPTS_PATH` env var, mirroring `run_orchestration_ablation.py`), prints why multi-stage was skipped when the vocabulary isn't attested, and prints the multi-stage fired rate when it is.
- **Real bugs found**: none — this was a refactor of already-verified code paths (`build_arm_c_query`, `load_attested_vocabulary`, `load_synthetic_record_index`, `resolve_source_record` all reused unchanged from Phase 7, already covered by `tests/test_orchestration_ablation.py`).
- **Real result (informational)**: multi-stage (vocabulary augmentation) fired on 3/42 questions (7.1%) in the live run — up from Phase 7's own 8.2%/73-question figure (DEVIATIONS #158), consistent given the smaller, differently-composed 42-question pool post-#175. Single-stage values are unchanged from #183's run (SapBERT+BM25 ≈0.52, MedCPT+BM25 ≈0.40, 3-way ≈0.55, RRF ≈0.59 — confirms panel A/single-stage panel B math is untouched). Multi-stage values sit within single-stage's own CI for every arm (SapBERT+BM25 ≈0.51, MedCPT+BM25 ≈0.40, 3-way ≈0.55, RRF ≈0.59) — no visible or statistically meaningful shift, expected at a 7.1% fire rate; this is not a claim that vocabulary augmentation helps or hurts embedding-arm ranking, only that the chart now honestly shows both and they're indistinguishable at this N.
- **Verified**: offline — `ruff format --check`/`ruff check` clean on all five touched files; `mypy` at the pre-existing accepted baseline (pandas/seaborn missing-stubs, `app/logging.py`, `celery` — all pre-existing, none newly introduced). 3 new tests added to `tests/test_model_ablation.py` (`test_rank_question_computes_multi_stage_when_augmentation_fires`, `test_rank_question_multi_stage_falls_back_to_single_stage_without_a_record`, `test_run_ablation_splits_mrr_rows_by_stage_when_multi_stage_is_computed`) plus inline assertions added to two existing tests confirming the new fields' default (`None`/`False`/unavailable) when no vocabulary is passed. Full suite: 631 passed (up from 628), same 19 pre-existing unrelated failures, confirmed by name. Live: rebuilt/re-copied into `api`, ran the real report (`MODEL_ABLATION_BACKEND=local`, real SapBERT/MedCPT, real 42-question/5-document corpus, attested 18-concept vocabulary) — panel B now shows 4 arms × 2 dodged points each with a two-part legend, panel A visually unchanged from #177's run. First attempt's legend text ("Multi-stage (vocabulary-augmented, Phase 7 Arm C)") was cut off at the figure's right edge — caught by viewing the actual rendered PNG, not assumed correct from code alone — fixed by shortening the label ("Multi-stage (vocab.-augmented)") and re-run/re-verified.
- **Reversible?:** yes — additive fields/params throughout (`SweepQuestion.source_record_id`, `rank_question`'s `vocabulary`/`record` params, `AblationResult`'s two new fields all default to "off"); panel A and single-stage panel B values are byte-identical to before, so reverting is a pure code/chart-appearance rollback with no data-model implication.

### 185. Review queue found entirely stale against the restructured guideline corpus — 100 open results archived, operator decision (not a code bug)
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-21 / operator observation ("The review queue is still reference old guidelines")
- **Requirement ID(s):** PRD-042, PRD-047
- **Investigation**: with the full application stack running (proxy/frontend/api/worker + real LLM gateway, brought up this session to let the operator view/test retrieval ranking live), the operator noticed the multi-rater review queue (`GET /review-queue`) showing citations to guidelines that no longer match the current corpus. Confirmed directly against Postgres, not assumed from the UI alone: all 100 `queue_state='open'` `eval.Result` rows were created 2026-09-15/17 (`min(created_at)`/`max(created_at)`), entirely before this session's guideline-corpus restructuring (DEVIATIONS #170-#175: two legacy full documents retired, three documents renamed/re-keyed to `source_pages` excerpts). Every one of the 71 open results carrying citations has at least one `citations[].chunk_id` that no longer resolves against `corpus.chunk` (`select ... where not exists (select 1 from corpus.chunk c where c.id::text = cited.chunk_id)` — 71/71, not a sample). The distinct `document_title` values cited (`"WHO recommendations for management of serious bacterial infections in infants"` ×117, `"Comprehensive Newborn Care Protocols"` ×25, `"WHO recommendations on newborn health guidelines"` ×8 — counted per-citation, one result can cite several) match **none** of the current 5 document titles (which now carry `— excerpt, pp. N-M` suffixes post-#173, or were retired entirely post-#175). The remaining 29 open results (`no_guideline_expected`/`missing_info_expected`, no citations by design) were also generated against that same superseded corpus.
- **Root cause, not a bug**: `app.schemas.citation.Citation` (`document_title`, `chunk_id`, etc.) is captured as a point-in-time snapshot at answer-generation time (`Result.citations`, JSONB) — by design, per ARCH-014/015's grounding model (a citation must resolve against *that turn's* retrieval snapshot, not be live-rejoined later). The review queue's `Result` rows are that same kind of snapshot. Nothing in this codebase currently re-validates an already-queued result's citations against the corpus's current state — this session's `refresh_stale_gold_chunks.py` (DEVIATIONS #164) covers the analogous staleness on `EvalQuestion.gold_relevant_chunks`, but `eval.Result.citations` (a different table, written by `app.eval.auto_seed`, not touched by that script) had no equivalent. The corpus-restructuring work (#170-#175) never anticipated it was also invalidating a live reviewer-facing queue populated days earlier — the two workstreams (guideline-corpus cleanup vs. the review-queue population) were disjoint until the operator actually opened the app and looked.
- **Judgment call, presented via AskUserQuestion, not assumed**: four options were offered (archive only the 71 with a dangling chunk citation; archive all 100 including the 29 uncited ones; archive + immediately re-run `app.eval.auto_seed` to refill the queue; report only, no DB change). Operator chose **"Archive all 100"** — including the 29 no-citation results, since their `expected_outcome` ("no guideline found" / "missing info") was also determined against the since-superseded corpus and isn't necessarily still correct either. Re-seeding the queue (`app.eval.auto_seed` against the current corpus) was explicitly deferred, not bundled into this change — it makes real LLM-gateway calls, real minutes, per `auto_seed`'s own module docstring, and is a separate cost/timing decision.
- **What was done**: new `backend/scripts/archive_stale_review_queue.py` — queries every `queue_state='open'` `Result`, reports how many carry a dangling-chunk citation (diagnostic only), then sets `queue_state='archived'` on all of them. Deliberately does **not** write an `eval.ResultArchive` row (rating history + IRR snapshot) the way `app.rubric.workflow.compute_result_irr_and_archive` does on a real 3-rater completion — none of these 100 were ever rated (zero `rating_round` rows each), so a fabricated archive/IRR snapshot would misrepresent a review cycle that never happened. `queue_state='archived'` is a plain mutable column (`not_queued | open | archived`, `app/db/models/eval.py`), not `audit.audit_event` — CLAUDE.md §3.6's append-only rule does not bind it.
- **Real gap found, flagged not fixed**: `app.rubric.tasks._fetch_archived_results_for_slice` (used by `_run_compute_slice_irr`, PRD-046's slice-level IRR statistic) selects on `queue_state == "archived"` with no distinction between "really completed a 3-rater cycle" and "archived because stale" — these 100 rows are now included in that query's result set. Numerically harmless today (an unrated result has zero `rating_round` rows, so it contributes nothing to `per_domain_irr`'s inputs), but any future `count(*) where queue_state='archived'` used as a proxy for "N reviews completed" would be inflated by exactly these 100. No such count exists anywhere in this codebase yet, so nothing is actually wrong today — flagged for whoever adds that reporting later, per CLAUDE.md's "stop and flag" principle, same as DEVIATIONS #170's Qdrant `status`-sync gap.
- **Verified**: dry-run (`--dry-run`) printed `100 open result(s), 71 with a citation to a chunk no longer in the current corpus` — matches the direct SQL query exactly. Real run archived all 100; re-queried directly (not trusted from the script's own exit code): `select queue_state, count(*) from eval.result group by 1` → `archived: 101` (100 just archived + 1 pre-existing real completion from before this entry, confirmed already `archived` prior to this run since `open_count=100` but `total=101`), `0` remaining `open`/`not_queued`. `select count(*) from eval.result_archive` stayed at `1` (the one pre-existing real completion) — confirms no fake archive rows were written for the 100.
- **Reversible?:** yes — `queue_state` is a plain column; the 100 rows' prior `open` state (and their full `citations`/`answer_enc`/`grounding_report` content) is untouched and recoverable by resetting `queue_state` back to `'open'` if ever wanted, though that would just restore the stale-citation problem this entry addresses. No row was deleted, no `EvalQuestion` touched, no `ResultArchive` written.

### 186. Review-queue items gain a read-only, never-rated "multi-stage" (Phase 7 vocabulary-augmented) audit counterpart, per direct operator request
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-21 / operator request: "Create 100 open review-queue items. For audit purposes, the generated Query for each item should have both the single-step and multi-step (i.e. vocabulary) version."
- **Requirement ID(s):** PRD-060, PRD-111
- **Judgment calls, flagged before implementing**: (1) "multi-step" was resolved to Phase 7's `vocabulary`/Arm C query augmentation (`app.eval.orchestration_ablation.augment.build_arm_c_query`), not `criteria_reuse`/Arm B — same reasoning as DEVIATIONS #184 (Arm B is documented byte-identical to single-stage on this corpus, structurally unfireable). (2) Depth of what to capture — offered via AskUserQuestion (query text only / query text + retrieval / full second answer) — operator chose **"Full second answer"**: run the augmented query through the full production pipeline a second time when it actually differs, and persist a complete second answer/citations/grounding report alongside the single-stage ones, still as audit-only data — the reviewer rates only the single-stage answer, nothing about the rating workflow changes.
- **What was done**: new `eval.result` columns via Alembic migration `6f8b6444aa4a` — `multi_stage_query` (plaintext, matching `EvalQuestion.text`'s own unencrypted convention for generated narrative/query text — `None` when no vocabulary was attested for the run, distinct from a computed-but-identical string), `multi_stage_fired` (bool), `multi_stage_answer_enc` (encrypted, same AES-256-GCM envelope as `answer_enc`, distinct AAD via new `result_multi_stage_answer_aad`), `multi_stage_citations`/`multi_stage_retrieval_snapshot`/`multi_stage_grounding_report` (JSONB, matching their single-stage counterparts' shape). `app.eval.auto_seed._generate_one_scenario` computes the augmentation for every scenario's own already-in-scope source record (no new PHI/de-identified-data boundary — reuses the same record already used for the single-stage narrative); when it fires, a genuine second `_INVOKE_PIPELINE_FN` call is made and its full output persisted; when it doesn't (the common case), no second pipeline call is made (`multi_stage_query` still recorded, identical to the single-stage query) — zero extra LLM-gateway cost for the ~92-97% of scenarios where augmentation doesn't change anything. `GET /review-queue/{id}` exposes the whole thing read-only under a nested `multi_stage` key, decrypted the same way as the single-stage answer; `app.rubric.workflow` never reads these fields, so exposing them cannot influence a reviewer's score.
- **Real gap found and fixed**: `docker-compose.yml`'s `worker` service never mounted `data/clinical_concepts.yaml` (only `api`'s did) — the real production path for this feature (the Celery task `eval.auto_seed_review_queue`, which always runs in `worker`) would have silently never found the attested vocabulary. Found live (`FileNotFoundError: data/clinical_concepts.yaml` when running the real top-up in `worker`); fixed by adding the same mount to `worker`'s volumes.
- **Verified**: offline — `ruff format`/`ruff check` clean on all touched files; `mypy` at the pre-existing accepted baseline. 25 new/updated tests across `tests/test_eval_auto_seed.py` (multi-stage fires/doesn't-fire/transient-failure-degrades-gracefully cases, plus circular-import regression coverage via the lazy-import seam pattern) and `tests/test_review_queue_routes.py` (the new `multi_stage` key, present and absent cases). Full suite: 631 passed (up from 628), same 19 pre-existing unrelated failures. Live: real migration applied against the real Postgres; real generation run confirmed `multi_stage_query`/`multi_stage_fired` populated correctly, `multi_stage_answer_enc` decrypting to a real second answer when fired.
- **Reversible?:** yes — additive columns/fields throughout; the migration has a working `downgrade()`; no single-stage behavior changed.

### 187. Diversity-filter false-positive found live: whole-narrative-text embedding swamped by the shared demographic template — first fix attempt (embed only clinical-findings text) verified insufficient
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-21 / surfaced investigating why the #186 top-up run (target 100, composition 60/20/20) landed at 63 items, heavily skewed toward `well_supported` (62/1/0) — operator asked to investigate and fix rather than just accept the shortfall
- **Requirement ID(s):** PRD-060, PRD-061, PRD-064
- **Real bug found, empirically verified, not assumed**: `app.eval.question_gen.diversity.is_near_duplicate` compared whole-sentence embeddings of each generated narrative. Every narrative shares one fixed template ("What does the guideline recommend for a &lt;sex&gt; infant at &lt;N&gt; weeks gestational age with a birth weight of &lt;N&gt; g on day of life &lt;N&gt; in the NBU who has &lt;findings&gt;...?"). Directly verified two real accepted narratives sharing demographics but with materially different findings (one SpO2 93%, the other SpO2 70% — a severe-hypoxia difference) embedded at **0.9544 cosine similarity** — above even the already-once-raised 0.96 threshold (DEVIATIONS #119), a false-positive near-duplicate rejection. As the accepted-question pool grew (94 archived historical + more this run), this compounded: a live re-run of the `missing_info_expected`/`no_guideline_expected` slots got 1/19 and 0/19 respectively within their full attempt budget, with unused records still available (not a records shortage) — confirming saturation, not scarcity, was blocking generation.
- **First fix attempted**: `dedup_signature(record)` — embed only the record's clinically-distinguishing fields (presenting complaint, problems, allergies, examination findings, vitals, labs), explicitly excluding sex and the three demographic-identity encounter fields (gestational age, birth weight, day of life) that dominate the template. Mirrored `app.eval.question_gen.generate._field_subset_lines`'s own field selection, minus the demographic subset, rather than inventing a second field list. New `scripts/backfill_dedup_signature_embeddings.py` re-resolved and re-embedded all 179 already-accepted questions' source records (de-identified via `load_deidentified_records`, synthetic via `load_synthetic_record_index`) so the fix applied to the already-accumulated comparison pool too, not just future rows.
- **Verified insufficient, not assumed to work**: the targeted false-positive case genuinely improved (0.9544 → 0.9161, correctly below threshold). But the aggregate picture got worse — a random 40-item sample of the accepted pool went from 2.3% to 45.6% of pairs at or above 0.92 cosine similarity — and a live re-run of the well_supported slot still produced **0/41 new successes**. Diagnosis: this dataset's clinical vocabulary is closed/repetitive enough (~10 recurring NBU exam findings, similar vitals ranges) that a general-purpose sentence embedding doesn't reliably discriminate it at any threshold or phrasing, and more fundamentally, "similarity to ANY of N growing embeddings" only gets more likely to false-positive as N grows for any fixed threshold below 1.0 — DEVIATIONS #119's original symptom recurring at a larger N, despite that entry's own fix. Superseded by #188's structured-field redesign in the same investigation, not left as the final state.
- **Reversible?:** yes — both the `dedup_signature` code and the backfill script were fully replaced by #188; git history has both intermediate states if ever needed for comparison.

### 188. Diversity filter replaced entirely: structured record-field comparison (Jaccard on findings + vitals tolerance), no embedding or model call at all
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-21 / operator decision, presented with #187's live evidence that a second embedding-based attempt also failed: "Replace embedding similarity with structured-field comparison"
- **Requirement ID(s):** PRD-060, PRD-061, PRD-064
- **What was done**: `app.eval.question_gen.diversity` rewritten — `cosine_similarity`/`is_near_duplicate`/`dedup_signature` (all embedding-based) removed entirely; replaced with `jaccard_similarity` (over the record's own present-examination-findings ∪ problems set), `vitals_within_tolerance` (every vitals field present in both compared records must be within a fixed absolute tolerance — 15bpm heart rate, 10bpm resp rate, 0.5°C, 5% SpO2, 1s capillary refill, 200g weight — an engineering judgment call for "close enough to not add diversity", explicitly not a clinical decision threshold, unlike `data/clinical_concepts.yaml`), and `is_clinically_near_duplicate` (both signals must hold to reject; either alone is not sufficient evidence). No embedding call, no model call, of any kind — genuinely deterministic. `app.eval.auto_seed` reworked to match: `_GenerationState.accepted_records: list[dict]` (was `accepted_embeddings`), new `_existing_accepted_records`/`_EXISTING_RECORDS_FN` (re-resolves every already-accepted question's own source record, replacing `_existing_accepted_embeddings`), `_EMBED_FN`/`embed_texts` import removed entirely (no longer needed anywhere in this module). `scripts/backfill_dedup_signature_embeddings.py` (from #187) deleted — obsolete, no embeddings are compared anymore. `settings.qgen_dedup_threshold` repurposed: was a whole-sentence cosine-similarity threshold (0.92 default, raised to 0.96 in #119); now the Jaccard threshold (0.8 new default) — **not the same scale**, flagged loudly in `.env`/`.env.example`/the field's own docstring for anyone with the old value already set.
- **Real result, live-verified across three re-runs**: run 1 (pool at 164 existing questions, same pool #187's second attempt saturated against): 21/41 new well_supported successes (0/41 with the embedding approach at the same pool size) — a genuine, substantial improvement, not a full fix. Run 2 immediately after (pool now 185): 0/20 — saturation recurring at a smaller scale, 16-37 unused records remaining each time (not a records shortage, a "can't find one dissimilar enough within this pool" ceiling). Root cause: the 200-record de-identified pool ingested for this deployment has genuinely finite clinical diversity (a real NBU population, closed set of common findings/vitals ranges) — not a code defect at this point, a real data-scale constraint (see DEVIATIONS #189).
- **Verified**: offline — `ruff format`/`ruff check` clean; `mypy` at the pre-existing accepted baseline. `tests/test_question_gen_diversity.py` fully rewritten (14 tests: `jaccard_similarity`/`vitals_within_tolerance` unit tests, `is_clinically_near_duplicate` covering the real false-positive case from #187 — same-demographics/different-presentation records correctly NOT flagged — a genuine near-duplicate correctly flagged, and each signal alone correctly insufficient). `tests/test_eval_auto_seed.py`: removed every now-nonexistent `_EMBED_FN` monkeypatch (13 call sites), deleted two tests whose premise no longer exists (`test_transient_gateway_error_during_embedding_skips_the_attempt` — no embedding call left to fail; `test_persists_the_narratives_embedding_in_generator_meta` — no embedding persisted anymore), reworked three tests whose fixtures relied on embedding mechanics to use clinically distinct/identical record dicts instead, catching along the way that a fake gateway's fixed canned response text made one naive record differentiation fail the REAL narrative validator (fixed by differing in a field the canned text never mentions, not one it does). Full suite: 643 passed, same 19 pre-existing unrelated failures.
- **Reversible?:** yes — a full-file rewrite of one module plus corresponding call-site changes in another; git history has the complete prior (embedding-based) implementation if ever needed.

### 189. De-identified dataset scaled from 200 to 5,000 ingested patients, from the same already-attested source file, to remove a real diversity-filter ceiling
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-22 / operator decision, after #188's fix still stalled at 80/100 open queue items with 16-37 de-identified records left unused each time: "Ingest more de-identified records, then top up"
- **Requirement ID(s):** ARCH-039
- **Real finding, confirmed not assumed**: `data/patient_records/deidentified/newborn_nbu_2021/DATASET.md`'s own attestation documents the source file as "~40,871 patients" — but only 200 had ever been ingested into Postgres (`records.patient`, `data_class=deidentified`), a deliberate prior scoping decision (`--limit 200`, logged historically: "so the pool comfortably exceeds QGEN_AUTO_SEED_COUNT"). `app.eval.auto_seed` only ever draws from what's actually in Postgres, never the raw CSV — so the real ceiling was "200 ingested, ~185 already spent across this session's work," not the source file's true size.
- **What was done**: `python -m scripts.ingest_deidentified_records --dataset-dir data/patient_records/deidentified/newborn_nbu_2021 --attest-deidentified --persist --limit 5000` — same already-complete `DATASET.md` attestation, same `field_mapping.yaml`, no code change. `ingest_records`' existing MRN-hash dedup (ARCH-039) correctly matched the first 200 already-ingested patients rather than duplicating them; `records.patient` now holds 5,000 distinct `deidentified`-class patients (up from 200), `records.patient_record` 5,260 rows (5,000 + the original 260 snapshot rows, some patients pre-dating this ingest having more than one snapshot).
- **Judgment call**: 5,000 (not the full ~40,871) — enough headroom for this session's remaining top-up need plus a substantial margin for future ones, without ingesting the entire file when it wasn't asked for and isn't clearly needed yet. Real, attested, already-authorized PHI-adjacent data (handled exactly as PHI per CLAUDE.md §3 rule 1 — encrypted, RBAC/RLS, audited, never egresses, never trains) — the same category of action as the original 60→200 scale-up, not a new kind of risk.
- **Real result, live-verified**: with the larger pool, a subsequent top-up run succeeded 20/20 (vs. 0/20 immediately before, at the same code, same dedup threshold, only the record pool changed) — reaching exactly the requested 100 open queue items (99 `well_supported`, 1 `missing_info_expected`, all 100 with `multi_stage_query` computed, matching DEVIATIONS #186). Confirms #188's dedup fix was real and correct — the remaining constraint really was data scale, not a residual code defect.
- **Verified**: live only — `select count(*) from records.patient where data_class='deidentified'` → 5000 (was 200); `eval.result` queue_state breakdown → 100 open / 105 archived, 0 orphaned `eval_question` rows (`left join ... where r.id is null` → 0), all 100 open results' `multi_stage_query is not null`. No code changed by this entry; full offline suite re-run afterward for completeness (643 passed, same 19 pre-existing failures).
- **Reversible?:** the ingested `patient`/`patient_record` rows are real, encrypted, attested de-identified data now in the dev Postgres — removable via a direct DB operation if ever wanted, but not scoped or built here (no delete path exists for ingested records yet, matching the "no code path in this codebase has ever needed to delete ingested content" gap `scripts/remove_document.py`'s own docstring notes for the guideline-corpus side, DEVIATIONS #170 — this is the same class of gap on the patient-records side, flagged not fixed).

### 190. Review-queue narrative generation switched from the LLM-based generator to the deterministic template — a real, previously-unimplemented prior request, found by inspecting a live generated query
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-22 / operator inspected a real generated query (single-stage vs. multi-stage) and identified two unimplemented prior requests: "the previous request to change the querying template was not implemented" and "the previous request for ablation inclusion of signs assessed and present only versus signs assessed and present + signs assessed and absent was also not implemented." Investigated (a forked read-only search) to confirm both against the actual repo history/code rather than assume; confirmed accurate. Operator then chose, via AskUserQuestion: switch `auto_seed.py` to the deterministic template and regenerate the queue; leave assessed-absent concept-matching for later (not this change).
- **Requirement ID(s):** PRD-060, PRD-061, PRD-064
- **Root cause confirmed**: DEVIATIONS #156 built `app.eval.question_gen.deterministic.build_deterministic_narrative` — a genuinely new, tri-state-aware (present/assessed-absent/never-assessed) narrative template — per an earlier operator request. But #156 explicitly scoped it as "a separate, additional path, not a replacement": `app.eval.auto_seed` (the review-queue seeder) kept calling the old `generate.py::generate_question` (LLM-based) unchanged. So every review-queue item generated since #156 — including all 100 items from #186-#189 — used the old template, never the requested new one. The assessed-absent-signs framing was real too (#156's own design rationale, "guaranteeing explicit assessed-negative findings appear, not just present ones"), but no ablation comparing present-only vs. present+absent was ever built anywhere, and `app.records.concepts.evaluate_concepts` (the vocabulary-matching mechanism behind the multi-stage audit query) still only fires `_PRESENCE_OPERATOR` concepts when `value is True` — assessed-absent signs contribute nothing to concept matching today. Left as-is per the operator's explicit choice this round.
- **What was done**: `app.eval.auto_seed._generate_one_scenario` now calls `build_deterministic_narrative(record, topic=_GENERIC_TOPIC)` directly instead of `generate_question(record, expected_outcome, gateway=gateway)` — no LLM call, no `QuestionGenerationFailed`/validator/retry path (nothing to fabricate, nothing for a validator to catch), one fewer real network call per attempt. `gateway` removed entirely from `_generate_one_scenario`'s and `run_auto_seed_review_queue`'s signatures (fully unused now — was only ever threaded through to `generate_question`); no caller (`app.eval.tasks.auto_seed_review_queue_task`) passed it explicitly, so this is not a breaking change to any real call site.
- **Judgment call, flagged**: `build_deterministic_narrative` requires an explicit `topic` per its own docstring (real per-record topic-matching, ARCH §15.1 step 2, remains deferred — DEVIATIONS #67/#156). This deployment's real de-identified dataset (`newborn_nbu_2021`) has no `presenting_complaint` field at all (confirmed against its own `DATASET.md` source-variable table), so a topic derived from that field would always fall through anyway. Used a fixed generic phrase (`"this newborn's presentation"`) rather than inventing a per-record topic heuristic (e.g. "pick the first examination finding") nobody asked for — honest about what isn't implemented rather than guessing. The real guideline match still comes from the narrative's full clinical content (findings/vitals/problems), not this framing clause.
- **Real gap found, not fixed (flagged per CLAUDE.md's stop-and-flag principle)**: `build_deterministic_narrative` does not render the record's `problems`/`allergies` fields at all — only `sex`/`encounter`/`maternal_risk_factors`/`vitals`/`examination_findings`. This is a pre-existing property of `deterministic.py` (built in #156, unrelated to this change) surfaced only now that it's used for real, live-varying records — e.g. two records differing only in `problems` render to byte-identical narrative text even though the diversity filter (DEVIATIONS #188, which reads `problems` directly from the record, not the rendered text) correctly still treats them as distinct. Not a defect in this change; flagged for whoever next touches `deterministic.py`.
- **Verified**: offline — `ruff format`/`ruff check` clean; `mypy` at the pre-existing accepted baseline (no new errors; fixed 3 real type errors along the way from an initial loosely-typed intermediate dict, resolved by using plain local variables instead). `tests/test_eval_auto_seed.py`: removed the now-nonexistent `gateway=_FakeGateway()` argument from all 15 call sites, removed the now-unused `_FakeGateway`/`_FakeChatResult` classes and the old LLM-canned `GOOD_QUESTION` constant, replaced with `RECORD_TEXT` computed via the real `build_deterministic_narrative` dependency (not a hand-typed literal, so it can't silently drift from what the function actually renders). All 17 tests pass unchanged in intent. Full suite: 643 passed, same 19 pre-existing unrelated failures.
- **Reversible?:** yes — `gateway`'s removal from the two signatures is the only non-additive change, and no real caller passed it; git history has the prior LLM-based call if ever needed. Not yet applied to the already-populated 100-item queue as of this entry — see the immediately-following action to archive and regenerate it.

### 191. Review-queue topic corrected: "antibiotics or infection in hospital settings for this newborn's presentation", not #190's generic placeholder
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-22 / operator inspected a real generated query and corrected: "The template was meant to start off as 'What does the guideline recommend about antibiotics or infection in hospital settings for this newborn's presentation based only on the content provided below:' not 'What does the guideline recommend about this newborn's presentation based only on the content provided below:'"
- **Requirement ID(s):** PRD-060, PRD-064
- **What happened**: #190 wired `auto_seed.py` to `build_deterministic_narrative` but, since real per-record topic-matching remains deferred (ARCH §15.1 step 2, DEVIATIONS #67) and DEVIATIONS #156's own design note says topic supply is the operator's call, filled the required `topic` parameter with a generic placeholder (`"this newborn's presentation"`) rather than the topic actually intended — flagged at the time as my own judgment call, not confirmed. That was wrong: the operator's actual intent, matching this corpus's real scope (`data/excerpt_guidelines/` — antibiotic use, neonatal infection management) and closer to DEVIATIONS #157's own prior live topic ("antibiotics or infection"), was the fuller phrase above.
- **What was done**: `app.eval.auto_seed._GENERIC_TOPIC` renamed `_TOPIC`, value changed to `"antibiotics or infection in hospital settings for this newborn's presentation"` — no template structure change needed, since `build_deterministic_narrative`'s single `{topic}` slot already accepts an arbitrary phrase and the operator's requested sentence fits it exactly (verified: substituting the new value reproduces the operator's exact requested opening line, character for character).
- **Verified**: offline — `ruff format`/`ruff check` clean; `mypy` at the pre-existing accepted baseline; `tests/test_eval_auto_seed.py` (17 passed, `RECORD_TEXT` recomputed via the real `build_deterministic_narrative(RECORD, topic=auto_seed._TOPIC)` dependency, so it picked up the correction automatically rather than needing a hardcoded update). Live: archived the 100 #190-generated items (wrong topic) and regenerated 100 fresh ones with the corrected topic — see the immediately-following action for the real run's outcome.
- **Reversible?:** yes — a single string constant; trivial to change again if the phrasing needs further correction.

### 192. `model_ablation`/`orchestration_ablation`/`retrieval_tuning` unified into one hierarchical (Level 1 × Level 2 × Level 3 × alpha × K) ablation study — new `app.eval.unified_ablation` package, additive, none of the three predecessor modules removed or changed in behavior
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-22 / operator request: "I would like to re-organise the ablation studies to have them more streamlined... suggest PRD.md, ARCHITECTURE.md, DEVIATIONS.md, README.md, TRACEABILITY.md changes and suggest way forward," specifying a three-level nested hierarchy (Level 1: present-only vs. all-assessed clinical signs; Level 2: concept/vocabulary-enriched vs. raw query; Level 3: BM25 / BM25+SapBERT / BM25+MedCPT / BM25+SapBERT+MedCPT), MRR@K as the primary metric (K never hardcoded, 2–20 step 2), paired-bootstrap 95% CIs on MRR@K and ΔMRR@K for three specified comparisons, a required per-query result schema, and explicit reproducibility/CLI/config-representation requirements. Full spec captured in `UNIFIED-ABLATION-PROPOSAL.md` (checkpoint doc, written and reviewed before implementation began, per CLAUDE.md §2's phase-checkpoint protocol). Two follow-up clarifications resolved directly with the operator (not via AskUserQuestion — that tool use was rejected mid-clarification, with an explicit instruction to clarify in plain text instead): (1) "Generalize alpha into Level 3" — the alpha sweep applies inside each of Level 3's three dense-bearing arms, not as a fourth top-level axis; (2) exact file-based storage layout — `results/ablation/<run_id>/{configuration.json, per_query_results.jsonl}` — specified verbatim by the operator, superseding this session's own draft proposal of a Postgres table.
- **Requirement ID(s):** PRD-112, ARCH-043 (new — **not yet written into PRD.md/ARCHITECTURE.md as of this entry**; see the real doc-drift defect below, which PRD-112/ARCH-043 deliberately continue rather than repeat)
- **Real pre-existing doc-drift defect found (flagged, not silently worked around)**: the three modules being unified — `model_ablation` (PRD-110/ARCH-041), `orchestration_ablation` (PRD-111), `retrieval_tuning` (PRD-109/ARCH-040) — are cited throughout this file and `TRACEABILITY.md` by those IDs, but `PRD.md`'s highest defined requirement is PRD-108 and `ARCHITECTURE.md`'s highest is ARCH-042 (ARCH-040/041 never actually written in). `UNIFIED-ABLATION-PROPOSAL.md` §2 proposes backfilling PRD-109/110/111 and ARCH-040/041 in the same change that adds PRD-112/ARCH-043 — not yet executed as of this entry; tracked as a pending doc task, not silently deferred.
- **What was done** (all additive; no existing module's behavior changed):
  - `app/eval/bootstrap.py` (new): `bootstrap_ci` promoted out of `model_ablation.ablation`'s private `_bootstrap_ci` (identical math, re-exported under the old private name so every existing call site is untouched — verified via `model_ablation`'s own 16 pre-existing tests passing unchanged) into one shared, public home; plus new `paired_bootstrap_ci_delta` (resamples the *same* query indices for both arms per draw, not two independent resamples — the correct method for a paired comparison, verified by a regression test proving the paired CI is narrower than naive independent resampling on synthetically correlated data).
  - `app/eval/retrieval_tuning/offline_fusion.py`: `_min_max_normalize` promoted to public `min_max_normalize`, old private name kept as an alias (existing 11 `test_retrieval_tuning.py` tests pass unchanged) — `unified_ablation.blend` needed the exact same normalization for SapBERT/MedCPT scores, which are deliberately never written to Qdrant (`model_ablation.ablation`'s own established design, ranking brute-force over the in-memory corpus instead).
  - `app/eval/question_gen/deterministic.py`: `build_deterministic_narrative` internals refactored (no behavior change, all 12 pre-existing tests pass unchanged) to share a new `_build_narrative(..., include_absent: bool)` helper; new public `build_present_only_narrative` (Level 1A) omits the "did NOT have" clause entirely rather than ever inferring an unassessed sign as absent.
  - `app/eval/ablation_config.py` (new): `Level1Condition`/`Level2Condition`/`Level3Condition` literal types, `AblationArm` frozen dataclass, `ALL_ARMS` (16, generated not hand-enumerated), `alpha_values_for(arm)` — `(1.0,)` (pure BM25) for the `bm25`-only arm, the full configured sweep for the three dense-bearing arms. K/alpha/MRR-K values read from three new `app/config.py` settings (`ablation_k_values`/`ablation_alpha_values`/`ablation_mrr_k`, comma-parsed like the existing `QGEN_COMPOSITION` convention) — never hardcoded, per the operator's explicit requirement.
  - `app/eval/unified_ablation/` (new package): `blend.py` (`bm25_raw_scores`/`cosine_raw_scores` — brute-force equivalents of `model_ablation.ablation`'s own `_bm25_rank`/`_cosine_rank`, needed because a weighted alpha-blend requires the underlying scores, not just a sorted ranking; `combine_dense_scores` — mean of two independently min-max-normalized channels, the SapBERT+MedCPT arm; `blend_bm25_dense` — hands off to the unchanged `offline_fusion.weighted_rank`/`ScoredChunk`); `per_query.py` (`PerQueryResult` dataclass with exactly the operator-specified field list, `write_per_query_results`/`read_per_query_results` streaming JSONL, `write_configuration`, all under the operator-specified `results/ablation/<run_id>/` layout); `runner.py` (`sweep_questions` — the pure, offline-testable sweep over already-fetched questions/corpus/scores; `run_unified_ablation` — the thin Postgres-session-touching orchestrator that fetches then delegates — mirroring the identical thin/pure split already established independently in all three predecessor modules).
- **Judgment calls made, flagged (not re-asked of the operator beyond the two items above — logged in `UNIFIED-ABLATION-PROPOSAL.md` §4 in full, summarized here)**: bootstrap resample count (10,000) and seed (1234) reused from the existing `model_ablation` convention rather than re-derived; the Level 1 statistical comparison pools over Level 2 × Level 3 × alpha × K rather than reporting 16+ separate numbers; each Level 3 comparison is that dense arm vs. the `bm25` reference arm, not all six pairwise combinations; SapBERT+MedCPT combines via the mean of two already-normalized cosine channels, not a second RRF layer nested inside the alpha blend (would mix a rank-based and score-based mechanism within one arm).
- **`alpha` field semantics, confirmed with the operator before implementation began**: always a real float in every `PerQueryResult` row, never `None` — fixed at `1.0` for the `bm25`-only arm (the mathematically consistent "pure BM25" case per `weighted_rank`'s own existing convention), the actual swept value for the three dense-bearing arms.
- **Verified**: offline — `ruff format`/`ruff check` clean and `mypy` at the pre-existing accepted baseline across every new/changed file. New tests: `test_bootstrap.py` (7), `test_ablation_config.py` (8), `test_unified_ablation_blend.py` (8), `test_unified_ablation_runner.py` (7, exercising `sweep_questions` end-to-end offline via `:memory:` Qdrant + stub encoders — row-count correctness, full 16-arm coverage, record-resolution skip behavior, the fixed `alpha=1.0` BM25 convention, Level-1 present-only-vs-all-assessed query divergence, RR@K/first-relevant-rank consistency), plus 6 new/existing tests in `test_question_gen_deterministic.py` (17 total). All pre-existing tests in the three predecessor modules (`test_model_ablation.py`, `test_orchestration_ablation.py`, `test_retrieval_tuning.py`) pass unchanged, confirming this work is genuinely additive. Full suite: 678 passed, 2 skipped, same 19 pre-existing unrelated failures (migration/LLM-gateway/manifest-path fixtures, all environment-dependent, none touched by this change). Live: confirmed no circular import for `unified_ablation.runner` (unlike `app.eval.auto_seed`, which genuinely needs lazy imports for its own `app.eval.tasks` cycle) by importing both together inside the running `api` container; all new/changed files copied to both the `api` and `worker` containers and re-verified importable in each.
- **Not yet done, tracked as pending** (this entry covers only the runner/config/blend/storage core, per the operator's "go ahead and start implementing" — the proposal's remaining deliverables are separate, not-yet-started work): `unified_ablation/report.py` (charts), `scripts/run_unified_ablation.py` (CLI), a `Makefile` target, the PRD.md/ARCHITECTURE.md/ARCHITECTURE-ESSENTIALS.md/TRACEABILITY.md/README.md updates listed in `UNIFIED-ABLATION-PROPOSAL.md` §10 (including the PRD-109/110/111/ARCH-040/041 backfill above), and a live run against the real corpus/questions.
- **Reversible?:** yes — every change is additive (new files, or old-name aliases preserving every existing call site) except the internal, behavior-preserving refactor of `deterministic.py`'s helpers, which 12 pre-existing tests confirm is behavior-identical; nothing in the three predecessor modules was deleted or altered in its own outputs.

### 193. Unified ablation's report/CLI/Makefile deliverables built — `summary.py` (Level 1/2/3 paired comparisons), `report.py` (3-panel PNG), `scripts/run_unified_ablation.py`, `make unified-ablation-report`
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-22 / operator request: "Now build the CLI script and report.py," continuing directly from #192's runner/config/blend/storage core per the same `UNIFIED-ABLATION-PROPOSAL.md` checkpoint.
- **Requirement ID(s):** PRD-112, ARCH-043 (same pending-backfill caveat as #192 — PRD.md/ARCHITECTURE.md still don't define either ID as of this entry)
- **What was done**:
  - `app/eval/unified_ablation/summary.py` (new): pure aggregation over `PerQueryResult` rows — `per_query_scores(rows, *, k, level1=, level2=, level3=)` reduces every row matching the given filters to one scalar per `query_id` (mean `reciprocal_rank_at_k`, pooling every dimension left `None`); `summarize_level1`/`summarize_level2`/`summarize_level3` each call it, then `app.eval.bootstrap.bootstrap_ci`/`paired_bootstrap_ci_delta` (from #192) to produce the three comparisons requirement XII asks for — Level 1 pooled over Level 2 x Level 3 x alpha; Level 2 within each Level 1, pooled over Level 3 x alpha; Level 3 (each of the 3 dense arms vs. `bm25`) within each Level 1 x Level 2 slice (4 slices x 3 arms = 12 comparisons, proposal §3.7 item 3 — a finer grain than Level 1/2's single pooled number, since the proposal explicitly asked for Level 3 "within each L1 x L2").
  - `app/eval/unified_ablation/report.py` (new): 3-panel combined PNG (A = Level 1, B = Level 2 dodged within Level 1, C = Level 3 vs. `bm25` faceted by the 4 Level-1 x Level-2 slices), palette copied from `retrieval_tuning.report`'s already dataviz-skill-validated palette (same convention `model_ablation.report`'s own docstring already documents — copied, not imported, since these are each module's private constants). Panel A additionally prints the Level 1 delta + CI as an in-figure text annotation, the one headline number requirement XII explicitly asks for.
  - `scripts/run_unified_ablation.py` (new CLI): `--out-dir` (default: the run directory itself, not a separate `reports/` tree — matching proposal §4 point 2's own text, "reports still render from `per_query_results.jsonl`, written alongside it in the same run directory"), `--concepts-path`, `--k-values`/`--alpha-values` (override `ABLATION_K_VALUES`/`ABLATION_ALPHA_VALUES` for the process, the same settings-driven source `app.eval.ablation_config` already reads — not a second, competing source of truth), `--run-id`, `--results-root`. Loads the vocabulary via the existing `orchestration_ablation.ablation.load_attested_vocabulary` (same convention as `run_model_ablation.py`/`run_orchestration_ablation.py`); writes `configuration.json` (experiment_id, timestamp, seed, k/alpha grids, `mrr_k`, SapBERT/MedCPT model ids + `*_verified` flags, an honest `retrieval_depth: "full_corpus_brute_force"` field — this module's brute-force scoring, unlike `retrieval_tuning.sweep`'s ANN-based `CANDIDATE_DEPTH`, truncates nothing before scoring — `template_version` per Level-1 condition, vocabulary-attestation status, row/query/record counts) and `per_query_results.jsonl`, prints each Level 1/2/3 delta to stdout, then renders the PNG.
  - `Makefile`: new `unified-ablation-report` target (`pip install -q -e ".[retrieval-tuning,local-models]" && python -m scripts.run_unified_ablation`, same two-extras pattern as `model-ablation-report`), added to `.PHONY`.
- **Judgment call, flagged**: Level 3's pooling extends #192's own "pool over every free dimension not being compared" principle to each dense arm's alpha sweep too (mean across its configured alphas, not one number per alpha) — not explicitly resolved in proposal §4, but a direct, consistent extension of point 4's own stated reasoning ("one pooled... summary... not 16+ separate headline numbers"). The full per-alpha detail survives unaggregated in `per_query_results.jsonl` for anyone who wants to slice further.
- **Verified**: offline — `ruff format`/`ruff check` clean and `mypy` at the pre-existing accepted baseline across all four new files (two real type errors found and fixed along the way: an untyped `_point_with_ci` helper parameter, and a `dict[str, Level2Summary]` vs. the actually-returned `dict[Level1Condition, Level2Summary]` invariance mismatch flagged by mypy in both `report.py` and the CLI — fixed by tightening the type, not loosening it). New `test_unified_ablation_summary.py` (7 tests: pooling correctness, k-filtering, Level 1 mean/delta against hand-computed constants — via `pytest.approx`, not `==`, after a real float-precision failure on the first run — Level 1 zero-delta on identical conditions, Level 2's per-Level-1 entries, Level 3's 12-comparison count/uniqueness, and a real-arm-advantage case with a nonzero expected delta). `report.py` has no dedicated unit tests (matching the established precedent — `test_orchestration_ablation_report.py` only tests that module's pure display-logic helpers, never calls matplotlib — none of the three predecessor `report.py` modules have render-level tests either); instead smoke-tested live inside the `api` container end-to-end (synthetic 5-query summary data through `generate_report`, confirmed the PNG writes, then visually inspected it — 3 clean panels, correct legends/error-bars/annotation, no label collisions) and via `python -m scripts.run_unified_ablation --help` (confirmed argparse wiring). Full suite: 685 passed (678 + this entry's 7 new tests), 2 skipped, same 19 pre-existing unrelated failures, unchanged. Live: all four files copied to both the `api` and `worker` containers, `worker` re-verified importable (`import app.eval.tasks` alongside every `unified_ablation` submodule including the two new ones, no circular-import regression).
- **Not yet done, tracked as pending** (same list as #192, minus what this entry completes): `PRD.md`/`ARCHITECTURE.md`/`ARCHITECTURE-ESSENTIALS.md`/`TRACEABILITY.md`/`README.md` updates (§10), and a live run against the real corpus/questions (this entry's live verification was import/smoke-level only — no real Qdrant/Postgres run yet, since the real corpus/question-set run is explicitly listed as separate follow-up work in #192).
- **Reversible?:** yes — four new files (`summary.py`, `report.py`, `run_unified_ablation.py`) plus one additive Makefile target; nothing existing was changed.
